Mahāpadānasuttaṃ

Dīgha Nikāya 1
← Back to Dīgha Nikāya
1
1. Evaṃ me sutaṃ – ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāme karerikuṭikāyaṃ. Atha kho sambahulānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ pacchābhattaṃ piṇḍapātapaṭikkantānaṃ karerimaṇḍalamāḷe sannisinnānaṃ sannipatitānaṃ pubbenivāsapaṭisaṃyuttā dhammī kathā udapādi – ‘‘itipi pubbenivāso, itipi pubbenivāso’’ti.
Thus have I heard: Once the Blessed One (bhagavā) was staying at Śrāvastī (sāvatthiyaṃ), in Anāthapiṇḍika’s (anāthapiṇḍikassa) park in the Jeta grove (jetavane), in the Kareri hutment (karerikuṭikāyaṃ).

And among a number of monks (bhikkhūnaṃ) who had gathered together after their meal, after the alms-round (piṇḍapātapaṭikkantānaṃ), sitting in the Kareri pavilion (karerimaṇḍalamāḷe), there arose a serious discussion on former lives (pubbenivāsa), as they said: ‘This is how it was in a former life (pubbenivāso)’, or ‘That was how it was.’
1.Evaṃme sutaṃ…pe… karerikuṭikāyanti mahāpadānasuttaṃ. Tatrāyaṃ apubbapadavaṇṇanā – karerikuṭikāyanti karerīti varuṇarukkhassa nāmaṃ, karerimaṇḍapo tassā kuṭikāya dvāre ṭhito, tasmā ‘‘karerikuṭikā’’ti vuccati, yathā kosambarukkhassa dvāre ṭhitattā ‘‘kosambakuṭikā’’ti. Antojetavane kira karerikuṭi kosambakuṭi gandhakuṭi salaḷāgāranti cattāri mahāgehāni, ekekaṃ satasahassapariccāgena nipphannaṃ. Tesu salaḷāgāraṃ raññā pasenadinā kāritaṃ, sesāni anāthapiṇḍikena kāritāni. Iti bhagavā anāthapiṇḍikena gahapatinā thambhānaṃ upari kāritāya devavimānakappāya karerikuṭikāyaṃ viharati . Pacchābhattanti ekāsanikakhalupacchābhattikānaṃ pātova bhuttānaṃ antomajjhanhikepi pacchābhattameva. Idha pana pakatibhattassa pacchato ‘‘pacchābhatta’’nti adhippetaṃ. Piṇḍapātapaṭikkantānanti piṇḍapātato paṭikkantānaṃ, bhattakiccaṃ niṭṭhapetvā uṭṭhitānanti attho. Karerimaṇḍalamāḷeti tasseva karerimaṇḍapassa avidūre katāya nisīdanasālāya. So kira karerimaṇḍapo gandhakuṭikāya ca sālāya ca antare hoti, tasmā gandhakuṭīpi karerikuṭikāpi sālāpi – ‘‘karerimaṇḍalamāḷo’’ti vuccati. Pubbenivāsapaṭisaṃyuttāti ‘‘ekampi jātiṃ, dvepi jātiyo’’ti evaṃ vibhattena pubbenivutthakkhandhasantānasaṅkhātena pubbenivāsena saddhiṃ yojetvā pavattitā. Dhammīti dhammasaṃyuttā. Karerimaṇḍalamāḷe Pubbenivāsapaṭisaṃyuttā Dhammī Udapādīti aho acchariyaṃ dasabalassa pubbenivāsañāṇaṃ , pubbenivāsaṃ nāma ke anussaranti, ke nānussarantīti. Titthiyā anussaranti, sāvakā ca paccekabuddhā ca buddhā ca anussaranti. Kataratitthiyā anussaranti? Ye aggappattakammavādino, tepi cattālīsaṃyeva kappe anussaranti, na tato paraṃ. Sāvakā kappasatasahassaṃ anussaranti. Dve aggasāvakā asaṅkhyeyyañceva kappasatasahassañca. Paccekabuddhā dve asaṅkhyeyyāni kappasatasahassañca. Buddhānaṃ pana ettakanti paricchedo natthi, yāvatakaṃ ākaṅkhanti, tāvatakaṃ anussaranti. Udapādī pubbenivāsaṃ Titthiyā khandhapaṭipāṭiyā anussaranti, paṭipāṭiṃ muñcitvā na sakkonti. Paṭipāṭiyā anussarantāpi asaññabhavaṃ patvā khandhappavattiṃ na passanti, jāle patitā kuṇṭhā viya, kūpe patitā paṅguḷā viya ca honti. Te tattha ṭhatvā ‘‘ettakameva, ito paraṃ natthī’’ti diṭṭhiṃ gaṇhanti. Iti titthiyānaṃ pubbenivāsānussaraṇaṃ andhānaṃ yaṭṭhikoṭigamanaṃ viya hoti. Yathā hi andhā yaṭṭhikoṭiggāhake satiyeva gacchanti, asati tattheva nisīdanti, evameva titthiyā khandhapaṭipāṭiyāva anussarituṃ sakkonti, paṭipāṭiṃ vissajjetvā na sakkonti. Sāvakāpi khandhapaṭipāṭiyāva anussaranti, asaññabhavaṃ patvā khandhappavattiṃ na passanti. Evaṃ santepi te vaṭṭe saṃsaraṇakasattānaṃ khandhānaṃ abhāvakālo nāma natthi. Asaññabhave pana pañcakappasatāni pavattantīti tattakaṃ kālaṃ atikkamitvā buddhehi dinnanaye ṭhatvā parato anussaranti; seyyathāpi āyasmā sobhito. Dve aggasāvakā pana paccekabuddhā ca cutipaṭisandhiṃ oloketvā anussaranti. Buddhānaṃ cutipaṭisandhikiccaṃ natthi, yaṃ yaṃ ṭhānaṃ passitukāmā honti, taṃ tadeva passanti. Titthiyā ca pubbenivāsaṃ anussaramānā attanā diṭṭhakatasutameva anussaranti. Tathā sāvakā ca paccekabuddhā ca. Buddhā pana attanā vā parehi vā diṭṭhakatasutaṃ sabbameva anussaranti. Titthiyānaṃ pubbenivāsañāṇaṃ khajjopanakaobhāsasadisaṃ, sāvakānaṃ padīpobhāsasadisaṃ, aggasāvakānaṃ osadhitārakobhāsasadisaṃ, paccekabuddhānaṃ candobhāsasadisaṃ, buddhānaṃ saradasūriyamaṇḍalobhāsasadisaṃ. Tassa ettakāni jātisatāni jātisahassāni jātisatasahassānīti vā ettakāni kappasatāni kappasahassāni kappasatasahassānīti vā natthi, yaṃ kiñci anussarantassa neva khalitaṃ, na paṭighātaṃ hoti, āvajjanapaṭibaddhameva ākaṅkhamanasikāracittuppādapaṭibaddhameva hoti. Dubbalapattapuṭe vegakkhittanārāco viya, sinerukūṭe vissaṭṭhaindavajiraṃ viya ca asajjamānameva gacchati. ‘‘Aho mahantaṃ bhagavato pubbenivāsañāṇa’’nti evaṃ bhagavantaṃyeva ārabbha kathā uppannā, jātā pavattāti attho. Taṃ sabbampi saṅkhepato dassetuṃ ‘‘itipi pubbenivāso, itipi pubvenivāso’’ti ettakameva pāḷiyaṃ vuttaṃ. Tattha itipīti evampi. itipī
1. Thus have I heard…pe… at the karerikuṭikā—the Mahāpadāna Sutta.

Here follows a word-by-word explanation of apubbapada: karerikuṭikāya means karerī is the name of the varuṇa tree. The karerimaṇḍapa is situated at the gate of that kuṭikā, therefore it is called “karerikuṭikā,” just as it is called “kosambakuṭikā” because it is situated at the gate of the kosamba tree.

It is said that inside Jetavana there are four great dwellings: the karerikuṭi, the kosambakuṭi, the gandhakuṭi, and the salaḷāgāra, each built with an expenditure of one hundred thousand.

Among these, the salaḷāgāra was built by King Pasenadi, and the rest were built by Anāthapiṇḍika.

Thus, the Blessed One dwelt in the karerikuṭikā, built by the householder Anāthapiṇḍika above the pillars, like a divine mansion.

Pacchābhatta means that for those who habitually eat only one meal a day, even if they eat early in the morning, it is still considered pacchābhatta even in the middle of the day.

Here, however, pacchābhatta is intended to mean after the regular meal.

Piṇḍapātapaṭikkantāna means those who have returned from the alms round, meaning those who have finished their meal and have risen.

Karerimaṇḍalamāḷe means at the sitting hall built not far from that same karerimaṇḍapa.

It is said that karerimaṇḍapa is located between the gandhakuṭikā and the hall, therefore the gandhakuṭī, the karerikuṭikā, and the hall are all called “karerimaṇḍalamāḷo.”

Pubbenivāsapaṭisaṃyuttā means connected with the dwelling of former times, distinguished by the dwelling of former aggregates, associated with the dwelling of former times, such as “one birth, two births,” etc., and having a continuity.

Dhammī means connected with the Dharma.

Karerimaṇḍalamāḷe, pubbenivāsapaṭisaṃyuttā, dhammī

Udapādī means: “Oh, how wonderful is the knowledge of the dwelling of former times of the Ten-Powered One!” Pubbenivāsaṃ means: Who remembers? Who does not remember?

The sectarians remember, and so do the disciples, the Paccekabuddhas, and the Buddhas.

Which sectarians remember? Those who assert action achieves pre-eminence, and they only remember forty aeons, and not beyond that.

The disciples remember one hundred thousand aeons.

The two chief disciples remember an incalculable aeon and one hundred thousand aeons.

The Paccekabuddhas remember two incalculable aeons and one hundred thousand aeons.

But for the Buddhas, there is no limit to this; they remember as far as they wish.

Udapādī, pubbenivāsaṃ

The sectarians remember by the order of the aggregates; they are unable to remember by abandoning the order. Even when remembering by the order, having reached the unconscious state, they do not see the arising of the aggregates; like a blunt object fallen into a net, like a cripple fallen into a well.

Having stayed there, they grasp the view, “This is all; there is nothing beyond this.”

Thus, the recollection of former dwellings by the sectarians is like the walking of blind people with the tip of a stick.

Just as blind people only walk when there is a holder of the stick tip, and sit down right there when there is not, so too the sectarians are only able to remember by the order of the aggregates; they are unable to remember by abandoning the order.

The disciples also remember only by the order of the aggregates; having reached the unconscious state, they do not see the arising of the aggregates.

Even so, there is no time of absence of the aggregates of beings wandering in the round.

However, in the unconscious state, five hundred aeons pass, and having passed that much time, standing in the manner given by the Buddhas, they remember from beyond; just like the venerable Sobhita.

However, the two chief disciples and the Paccekabuddhas look back on the passing away and rebirth and remember.

For the Buddhas, there is no such thing as the activity of passing away and rebirth; whatever place they wish to see, they see that very place.
Even the titthiyas, recalling previous existences, recall only what they have seen and heard themselves. So too do disciples and paccekabuddhas. Buddhas, however, recall all that they have seen and heard themselves or [that has been seen and heard] by others.

The knowledge of previous existences of the titthiyas is like the light of a firefly; that of disciples is like the light of a lamp; that of chief disciples is like the light of the morning star; that of paccekabuddhas is like the light of the moon; [while] that of Buddhas is like the light of the autumn sun.

There is no limit to it, [saying,] "So many hundreds of births, so many thousands of births, so many hundreds of thousands of births," or, "So many hundreds of kappas, so many thousands of kappas, so many hundreds of thousands of kappas." Whatever they recall, there is no faltering, no obstruction; it is connected only with adverting, it is connected only with the arising of a mind of expectation and attention. Like an arrow shot with speed from a weak leaf-quiver, and like the thunderbolt of Indra released on the peak of Mount Sineru, it goes without obstruction.

"How great is the Blessed One's knowledge of previous existences!" Thus, a discussion arose concerning the Blessed One himself; that is, it arose and proceeded.

To show all that in brief, only this much is said in the Pali: " Itipī previous existence, itipī previous existence."

There, itipī means in this way also.

Itipī
2
2. Assosi kho bhagavā dibbāya sotadhātuyā visuddhāya atikkantamānusikāya tesaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ imaṃ kathāsallāpaṃ. Atha kho bhagavā uṭṭhāyāsanā yena karerimaṇḍalamāḷo tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā paññatte āsane nisīdi, nisajja kho bhagavā bhikkhū āmantesi – ‘‘kāyanuttha, bhikkhave, etarahi kathāya sannisinnā; kā ca pana vo antarākathā vippakatā’’ti? Evaṃ vutte te bhikkhū bhagavantaṃ etadavocuṃ – ‘‘idha, bhante, amhākaṃ pacchābhattaṃ piṇḍapātapaṭikkantānaṃ karerimaṇḍalamāḷe sannisinnānaṃ sannipatitānaṃ pubbenivāsapaṭisaṃyuttā dhammī kathā udapādi – ‘itipi pubbenivāso itipi pubbenivāso’ti. Ayaṃ kho no, bhante, antarākathā vippakatā. Atha bhagavā anuppatto’’ti.
2. Then the Blessed One heard with the divine ear element, purified and surpassing the human, this conversation of those monks. Then the Blessed One rose from his seat and approached the Kareri pavilion. Having approached, he sat down on the prepared seat. Seated, the Blessed One addressed the monks: "With what conversation are you gathered here now, monks? And what was the discussion that was interrupted?"

When this was said, those monks said to the Blessed One: "Here, venerable sir, after our meal, returning from alms-gathering, gathered and assembled in the Kareri pavilion, a Dhamma talk connected with previous existences arose: 'Thus was a previous existence, thus was a previous existence.' This, venerable sir, was the discussion that was interrupted when the Blessed One arrived.”
3
3. ‘‘Iccheyyātha no tumhe, bhikkhave, pubbenivāsapaṭisaṃyuttaṃ dhammiṃ kathaṃ sotu’’nti? ‘‘Etassa, bhagavā, kālo; etassa, sugata, kālo; yaṃ bhagavā pubbenivāsapaṭisaṃyuttaṃ dhammiṃ kathaṃ kareyya, bhagavato sutvā bhagavato vacanaṃ sutvā (syā.) bhikkhū dhāressantī’’ti. ‘‘Tena hi, bhikkhave, suṇātha,sādhukaṃ manasi karotha, bhāsissāmī’’ti. ‘‘Evaṃ, bhante’’ti kho te bhikkhū bhagavato paccassosuṃ. Bhagavā etadavoca –
"3. Would you like, O monks, to hear a talk connected with past lives?"

"Venerable Sir, this is the right time. O, Well-gone One, this is the right time, if the Blessed One would give a talk connected with past lives. Having heard it from the Blessed One, the monks will remember it."

"Then listen, monks, and attend closely. I will speak."

"As you say, lord," the monks responded to the Blessed One.

The Blessed One said this:
4
4. ‘‘Ito so, bhikkhave, ekanavutikappe yaṃ ekanavuto kappo (syā. kaṃ. pī.) vipassī bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho loke udapādi. Ito so, bhikkhave, ekatiṃse kappe ekatiṃ sakappo (sī.) ekatiṃ so kappo (syā. kaṃ. pī.) yaṃ sikhī bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho loke udapādi. Tasmiññeva kho, bhikkhave, ekatiṃse kappe vessabhū bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho loke udapādi. Imasmiññeva imasmiṃ (katthacī) kho, bhikkhave, bhaddakappe kakusandho bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho loke udapādi. Imasmiññeva kho, bhikkhave, bhaddakappe koṇāgamano bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho loke udapādi. Imasmiññeva kho, bhikkhave, bhaddakappe kassapo bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho loke udapādi. Imasmiññeva kho, bhikkhave, bhaddakappe ahaṃ etarahi arahaṃ sammāsambuddho loke uppanno.
Monks, ninety-one aeons ago the Lord Buddha Vipassī, the arhat, the perfectly enlightened Buddha, arose in the world. Monks, thirty-one aeons ago the Lord Buddha Sikhī, the arhat, the perfectly enlightened Buddha, arose in the world. In that same thirty-first aeon, monks, the Lord Buddha Vessabhū, the arhat, the perfectly enlightened Buddha, arose in the world. In this present fortunate aeon, monks, the Lord Buddha Kakusandha, the arhat, the perfectly enlightened Buddha, arose in the world. In this present fortunate aeon, monks, the Lord Buddha Koṇāgamana, the arhat, the perfectly enlightened Buddha, arose in the world. In this present fortunate aeon, monks, the Lord Buddha Kassapa, the arhat, the perfectly enlightened Buddha, arose in the world. And in this present fortunate aeon, monks, I too have now arisen in the world as a fully-enlightened Buddha, an arhat.
4. Atha bhagavā tesaṃ yācanaṃ gahetvā kathetukāmo ‘‘tena hi, bhikkhave, suṇāthā’’ti te sotāvadhāraṇasādhukamanasikāresu niyojetvā aññesaṃ asādhāraṇaṃ chinnavaṭumakānussaraṇaṃ pakāsetukāmo ito so, bhikkhavetiādimāha. Tattha yaṃ vipassīti yasmiṃ kappe vipassī. Ayañhi ‘ya’nti saddo ‘‘yaṃ me, bhante, devānaṃ tāvatiṃsānaṃ sammukhā sutaṃ sammukhā paṭiggahitaṃ, ārocemi taṃ, bhagavato’’tiādīsu (dī. ni. 2.203) paccattavacane dissati. ‘‘Yaṃ taṃ apucchimha akittayī no, aññaṃ taṃ pucchāma tadiṅgha brūhī’’tiādīsu (su. ni. 881) upayogavacane. ‘‘Aṭṭhānametaṃ, bhikkhave, anavakāso, yaṃ ekissā lokadhātuyā’’tiādīsu (a. ni. 1.277) karaṇavacane. Idha pana bhummattheti daṭṭhabbo. Tena vuttaṃ – ‘‘yasmiṃ kappe’’ti. Udapādīti dasasahassilokadhātuṃ unnādento uppajji. Bhaddakappeti pañcabuddhuppādapaṭimaṇḍitattā sundarakappe sārakappeti bhagavā imaṃ kappaṃ thomento evamāha. Yato paṭṭhāya kira amhākaṃ bhagavatā abhinīhāro kato, etasmiṃ antare ekakappepi pañca buddhā nibbattā nāma natthi. Amhākaṃ bhagavato abhinīhārassa purato pana taṇhaṅkaro, medhaṅkaro, saraṇaṅkaro, dīpaṅkaroti cattāro buddhā ekasmiṃ kappe nibbattiṃsu. Tesaṃ orabhāge ekaṃ asaṅkhyeyyaṃ buddhasuññameva ahosi. Bhaddakappe Asaṅkhyeyyakappapariyosāne pana koṇḍañño nāma buddho ekova ekasmiṃ kappe uppanno. Tatopi asaṅkhyeyyaṃ buddhasuññameva ahosi. Asaṅkhyeyyakappapariyosāne maṅgalo, sumano, revato, sobhitoti cattāro buddhā ekasmiṃ kappe uppannā . Tatopi asaṅkhyeyyaṃ buddhasuññameva ahosi. Asaṅkhyeyyakappapariyosāne pana ito kappasatasahassādhikassa asaṅkhyeyyassa upari anomadassī, padumo, nāradoti tayo buddhā ekasmiṃ kappe uppannā. Tatopi asaṅkhyeyyaṃ buddhasuññameva ahosi. Asaṅkhyeyyakappapariyosāne pana ito kappasatasahassānaṃ upari padumuttaro bhagavā ekova ekasmiṃ kappe uppanno. Tassa orabhāge ito tiṃsakappasahassānaṃ upari sumedho, sujātoti dve buddhā ekasmiṃ kappe uppannā. Tato orabhāge ito aṭṭhārasannaṃ kappasahassānaṃ upari piyadassī, atthadassī, dhammadassīti tayo buddhā ekasmiṃ kappe uppannā. Atha ito catunavutikappe siddhattho nāma buddho ekova ekasmiṃ kappe uppanno. Ito dve navutikappe tisso, phussoti dve buddhā ekasmiṃ kappe uppannā. Ito ekanavutikappe vipassī bhagavā uppanno. Ito ekatiṃse kappe sikhī, vessabhūti dve buddhā uppannā. Imasmiṃ bhaddakappe kakusandho, koṇāgamano, kassapo, gotamo amhākaṃ sammāsambuddhoti cattāro buddhā uppannā, metteyyo uppajjissati. Evamayaṃ kappo pañcabuddhuppādapaṭimaṇḍitattā sundarakappo sārakappoti bhagavā imaṃ kappaṃ thomento evamāha. Kiṃ panetaṃ buddhānaṃyeva pākaṭaṃ hoti – ‘‘imasmiṃ kappe ettakā buddhā uppannā vā uppajjissantīti vā’’ti, udāhu aññesampi pākaṭaṃ hotīti? Aññesampi pākaṭaṃ hoti. Kesaṃ? Suddhāvāsabrahmānaṃ. Kappasaṇṭhānakālasmiñhi ekamasaṅkhyeyyaṃ ekaṅgaṇaṃ hutvā ṭhite lokasannivāse lokassa saṇṭhānatthāya devo vassituṃ ārabhati. Āditova antaraṭṭhake himapāto viya hoti. Tato tilamattā kaṇamattā taṇḍulamattā mugga-māsa-badara-āmalaka-eḷāluka-kumbhaṇḍa-alābumattā udakadhārā hutvā anukkamena usabhadveusabhaaḍḍhagāvutagāvutadvegāvutaaḍḍhayojanayojanadviyojana…pe… yojanasatayojanasahassayojanasatasahassamattā hutvā koṭisatasahassacakkavāḷabbhantare yāva avinaṭṭhabrahmalokā pūretvā tiṭṭhanti. Atha taṃ udakaṃ anupubbena bhassati, bhassante udake pakatidevalokaṭṭhānesu devalokā saṇṭhahanti, tesaṃ saṇṭhahanavidhānaṃ visuddhimagge pubbenivāsakathāyaṃ vuttameva. visuddhimagge Manussalokasaṇṭhahanaṭṭhānaṃ pana patte udake dhamakaraṇamukhe pihite viya vātavasena taṃ udakaṃ santiṭṭhati, udakapiṭṭhe uppalinipaṇṇaṃ viya pathavī saṇṭhahati. Mahābodhipallaṅko vinassamāne loke pacchā vinassati, saṇṭhahamāne paṭhamaṃ saṇṭhahati. Tattha pubbanimittaṃ hutvā eko paduminigaccho uppajjati, tassa sace tasmiṃ kappe buddho nibbattissati, pupphaṃ uppajjati. No ce, nuppajjati. Uppajjamānañca sace eko buddho nibbattissati, ekaṃ uppajjati. Sace dve, tayo, cattāro, pañca buddhā nibbattissanti, pañca uppajjanti. Tāni ca kho ekasmiṃyeva nāḷe kaṇṇikābaddhāni hutvā. Suddhāvāsabrahmāno ‘‘āyāma , mayaṃ mārisā, pubbanimittaṃ passissāmā’’ti mahābodhipallaṅkaṭṭhānaṃ āgacchanti, buddhānaṃ anibbattanakappe pupphaṃ na hoti. Te pana apupphitagacchaṃ disvā – ‘‘andhakāro vata bho loko bhavissati, matā matā sattā apāye pūressanti, cha devalokā nava brahmalokā suññā bhavissantī’’ti anattamanā honti. Pupphitakāle pana pupphaṃ disvā – ‘‘sabbaññubodhisattesu mātukucchiṃ okkamantesu nikkhamantesu sambujjhantesu dhammacakkaṃ pavattentesu yamakapāṭihāriyaṃ karontesu devorohanaṃ karontesu āyusaṅkhāraṃ ossajjantesu parinibbāyantesu dasasahassacakkavāḷakampanādīni pāṭihāriyāni dakkhissāmā’’ti ca ‘‘cattāro apāyā parihāyissanti, cha devalokā nava brahmalokā paripūressantī’’ti ca attamanā udānaṃ udānentā attano attano brahmalokaṃ gacchanti. Imasmiṃ bhaddakappe pañca padumāni uppajjiṃsu. Tesaṃ nimittānaṃ ānubhāvena cattāro buddhā uppannā, pañcamo uppajjissati. Suddhāvāsabrahmānopi tāni padumāni disvā imamatthaṃ jāniṃsu. Tena vuttaṃ – ‘‘aññesampi pākaṭaṃ hotī’’ti. Āyuparicchedavaṇṇanā
4. Then, the Blessed One, having taken their request, wishing to speak, said, "Then, monks, listen," and having directed them to proper attention with attentive hearing, wishing to reveal the uncommon recollection of what was cut off, said, "From here," and so on.

There, yaṃ vipassī means in which cycle (kalpa) Vipassī [appeared].

Indeed, this word "yaṃ" appears in the sense of personal reference in passages such as "That which I, venerable sir, have heard and directly received in the presence of the gods of the Thirty-three, that I announce to the Blessed One" (Dī.
Ni.
2.
203).

It is in the sense of usage in passages such as, "What we asked you, you have declared to us. We ask you another; tell us that here." (Su.
Ni.
881).

It is in the sense of instrumentality in passages such as, "This is impossible, monks, there is no opportunity for [it] in one world-system." (A.
Ni.
1.
277).

Here, however, it should be understood as being in the sense of location.

Therefore, it was said: "In which cycle".

Udapādī means arose, causing the ten-thousand world-systems to resound.

Because the Blessed One praised this cycle (bhadrakalpa) as beautifully adorned with the appearance of five Buddhas, [as] a beautiful cycle, [as] an essential cycle, he said thus:

Since the vow was made by our Blessed One, in this interval there has not been even in one cycle, the appearance of five Buddhas.

Before the vow of our Blessed One, however, Taṇhaṅkara, Medhaṅkara, Saraṇaṅkara, and Dīpaṅkara, these four Buddhas arose in one cycle.

In the part before them, there was one incalculable [period which was] just empty of Buddhas.

In the auspicious cycle (bhaddrakalpa), at the end of an incalculable cycle, Koṇḍañña by name, one Buddha, alone, arose in one cycle.

Also after that, there was an incalculable [period that was] just empty of Buddhas.

At the end of an incalculable cycle, Maṅgala, Sumana, Revata, and Sobhita, these four Buddhas arose in one cycle.

Also after that, there was an incalculable [period which was] just empty of Buddhas.

At the end of an incalculable cycle, however, more than a hundred thousand cycles from here, Anomadassī, Paduma, and Nārada, these three Buddhas arose in one cycle.

Also after that, there was an incalculable [period which was] just empty of Buddhas.

At the end of an incalculable cycle, however, more than a hundred thousand cycles from here, Padumuttara, the Blessed One, alone, arose in one cycle.

In the part before him, more than thirty thousand cycles from here, Sumedha and Sujāta, these two Buddhas arose in one cycle.

In the part before that, more than eighteen thousand cycles from here, Piyadassī, Atthadassī, and Dhammadassī, these three Buddhas arose in one cycle.

Then, ninety-four cycles from here, Siddhāttha by name, one Buddha, alone, arose in one cycle.

Ninety-two cycles from here, Tissa and Phussa, these two Buddhas arose in one cycle.

Ninety-one cycles from here, Vipassī, the Blessed One, arose.

Thirty-one cycles from here, Sikhī and Vessabhū, two Buddhas, arose.

In this auspicious cycle (bhadrakalpa), Kakusandha, Koṇāgamana, Kassapa, Gotama, our perfect and complete Buddha, these four Buddhas arose, and Maitreya will arise.

Thus, the Blessed One, praising this cycle as a beautiful cycle, an essential cycle, because this cycle is beautifully adorned with the appearance of five Buddhas, said thus.
What? Is this known only to the Buddhas – "In this aeon, so many Buddhas arose or will arise," or is it known to others as well? It is known to others as well.

To whom? To the śuddhāvāsa (śuddhāvāsa) Brahmās.

Indeed, at the time of the formation of the aeon, when the world-system is situated having become one asaṅkhyeya and one ekaṅgaṇa, a god begins to send rain for the purpose of the formation of the world.

From the beginning, it is like a snowfall in the antaraṭṭhaka.

Then, having become a stream of water the size of a sesame seed, the size of a grain, the size of a rice grain, the size of a green gram, the size of a bean, the size of a jujube, the size of an emblic myrobalan, the size of an eḷāluka, the size of a gourd, the size of a bottle gourd, gradually, by stages, becoming the size of a bull, two bulls, half a gāvuta, a gāvuta, two gāvutas, half a yojana, a yojana, two yojanas...pe...having become the size of one hundred yojanas, one thousand yojanas, one hundred thousand yojanas, having filled the interior of a koṭisatasahassacakkavāḷa world system, they stand up to the avinaṭṭhabrahmaloka.

Then, that water gradually dissipates, and as the water dissipates, in the places of the pakatidevaloka, the gods establish themselves. The method of their establishment has already been stated in the pubbenivāsakathā in the Visuddhimagga.

In the Visuddhimagga: The place of the formation of the human world, however, when water is obtained, like a face covered with a dhamakaraṇa at the mouth, that water remains by force of the wind, and the earth establishes itself like a water lily lying on the surface of the water.

The Great Bodhi-seat (mahābodhipallaṅka) perishes last when the world is perishing, and establishes itself first when the world is establishing itself.

There, having become a preliminary sign, a single paduminigaccha arises. If a Buddha will be born in that aeon, a flower arises.

If not, it does not arise.

And, while arising, if one Buddha will be born, one arises.

If two, three, four, five Buddhas will be born, five arise.

And those, having become bound by the corolla in a single stalk.

The śuddhāvāsa (śuddhāvāsa) Brahmās, saying, "Come, friends, we will see a preliminary sign," come to the place of the Great Bodhi-seat (mahābodhipallaṅka). In an aeon in which Buddhas are not born, there is no flower.

But they, having seen a flowerless tree, being unhappy, say, "Alas! This world will be dark; the dead beings will fill the apāyas; the six heavens and the nine Brahmā heavens will be empty."

But, at the time of flowering, having seen a flower, being delighted, and uttering a solemn utterance, they go to their own Brahmā-world, saying, "We will see the miracles such as the ten-thousand world-system earthquakes when the omniscient Bodhisattvas descend into their mothers' wombs, emerge, awaken, turn the wheel of the Dharma, perform the twin miracle, perform the descent from heaven, relinquish the life force, and attain parinirvāṇa (parinirvāṇa)," and "The four woeful realms will diminish, the six heavens and the nine Brahmā heavens will be filled."

In this auspicious aeon (bhaddakappa), five lotuses arose.

By the power of those signs, four Buddhas have arisen, and a fifth will arise.

The śuddhāvāsa (śuddhāvāsa) Brahmās, having seen those lotuses, understood this meaning.

Therefore it was said - "It is known to others as well".

Description of the Measurement of Life
5
5. ‘‘Vipassī, bhikkhave, bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho khattiyo jātiyā ahosi, khattiyakule udapādi. Sikhī, bhikkhave, bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho khattiyo jātiyā ahosi, khattiyakule udapādi. Vessabhū, bhikkhave, bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho khattiyo jātiyā ahosi, khattiyakule udapādi. Kakusandho, bhikkhave, bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho brāhmaṇo jātiyā ahosi, brāhmaṇakule udapādi. Koṇāgamano, bhikkhave, bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho brāhmaṇo jātiyā ahosi, brāhmaṇakule udapādi. Kassapo, bhikkhave, bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho brāhmaṇo jātiyā ahosi, brāhmaṇakule udapādi. Ahaṃ, bhikkhave, etarahi arahaṃ sammāsambuddho khattiyo jātiyā ahosiṃ, khattiyakule uppanno.
5. Monks, the Blessed Vipassī, the worthy one, the perfectly awakened Buddha, was born of the Khattiya race, and arose in a Khattiya family.

Sikhī, monks, the Blessed One, the worthy one, the perfectly awakened Buddha, was born of the Khattiya race, and arose in a Khattiya family.

Vessabhū, monks, the Blessed One, the worthy one, the perfectly awakened Buddha, was born of the Khattiya race, and arose in a Khattiya family.

Kakusandha, monks, the Blessed One, the worthy one, the perfectly awakened Buddha, was born of the brāhmaṇa race, and arose in a brāhmaṇa family.

Koṇāgamana, monks, the Blessed One, the worthy one, the perfectly awakened Buddha, was born of the brāhmaṇa race, and arose in a brāhmaṇa family.

Kassapa, monks, the Blessed One, the worthy one, the perfectly awakened Buddha, was born of the brāhmaṇa race, and arose in a brāhmaṇa family.

I, monks, who am now the worthy one, the perfectly awakened Buddha, was born of the Khattiya race, and arose in a Khattiya family.
6
6. ‘‘Vipassī , bhikkhave, bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho koṇḍañño gottena ahosi. Sikhī, bhikkhave, bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho koṇḍañño gottena ahosi. Vessabhū, bhikkhave, bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho koṇḍañño gottena ahosi. Kakusandho, bhikkhave, bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho kassapo gottena ahosi. Koṇāgamano, bhikkhave, bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho kassapo gottena ahosi. Kassapo, bhikkhave, bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho kassapo gottena ahosi. Ahaṃ, bhikkhave, etarahi arahaṃ sammāsambuddho gotamo gottena ahosiṃ.
6. "Monks, the Lord Buddha Vipassi was of the Koṇḍañña clan; the Lord Buddha Sikhi was of the Koṇḍañña clan; the Lord Buddha Vessabhu was of the Koṇḍañña clan; the Lord Buddha Kakusandha was of the Kassapa clan; the Lord Buddha Koṇāgamana was of the Kassapa clan; the Lord Buddha Kassapa was of the Kassapa clan; and I, monks, who am now the Arahant and Fully-Enlightened Buddha, am of the Gotama clan."
7
7. ‘‘Vipassissa, bhikkhave, bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa asītivassasahassāni āyuppamāṇaṃ ahosi. Sikhissa, bhikkhave, bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa sattativassasahassāni āyuppamāṇaṃ ahosi. Vessabhussa, bhikkhave, bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa saṭṭhivassasahassāni āyuppamāṇaṃ ahosi. Kakusandhassa, bhikkhave, bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa cattālīsavassasahassāni āyuppamāṇaṃ ahosi. Koṇāgamanassa, bhikkhave, bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa tiṃsavassasahassāni āyuppamāṇaṃ ahosi. Kassapassa, bhikkhave, bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa vīsativassasahassāni āyuppamāṇaṃ ahosi. Mayhaṃ, bhikkhave, etarahi appakaṃ āyuppamāṇaṃ parittaṃ lahukaṃ; yo ciraṃ jīvati, so vassasataṃ appaṃ vā bhiyyo.
"Bhikkhus, the lifespan of the Blessed One, the Arahant, the Perfectly Enlightened Buddha Vipassi was eighty thousand years.

Bhikkhus, the lifespan of the Blessed One, the Arahant, the Perfectly Enlightened Buddha Sikhi was seventy thousand years.

Bhikkhus, the lifespan of the Blessed One, the Arahant, the Perfectly Enlightened Buddha Vessabhu was sixty thousand years.

Bhikkhus, the lifespan of the Blessed One, the Arahant, the Perfectly Enlightened Buddha Kakusandha was forty thousand years.

Bhikkhus, the lifespan of the Blessed One, the Arahant, the Perfectly Enlightened Buddha Konagamana was thirty thousand years.

Bhikkhus, the lifespan of the Blessed One, the Arahant, the Perfectly Enlightened Buddha Kassapa was twenty thousand years.

Bhikkhus, at present, my lifespan is short, limited, and fleeting; whoever lives long lives for a hundred years or a little more."
8
8. ‘‘Vipassī, bhikkhave, bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho pāṭaliyā mūle abhisambuddho. Sikhī, bhikkhave, bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho puṇḍarīkassa mūle abhisambuddho. Vessabhū, bhikkhave, bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho sālassa mūle abhisambuddho. Kakusandho, bhikkhave, bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho sirīsassa mūle abhisambuddho. Koṇāgamano, bhikkhave, bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho udumbarassa mūle abhisambuddho. Kassapo, bhikkhave, bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho nigrodhassa mūle abhisambuddho. Ahaṃ, bhikkhave, etarahi arahaṃ sammāsambuddho assatthassa mūle abhisambuddho.
8. Monks, the Lord Buddha Vipaśyī (Vipassī), the worthy one, the perfectly enlightened one, gained his full enlightenment at the foot of a trumpet-flower tree (pāṭaliyā mūle abhisambuddho).

Monks, the Lord Buddha Śikhī (Sikhī), the worthy one, the perfectly enlightened one, gained his full enlightenment under a white-mango tree (puṇḍarīkassa mūle abhisambuddho).

Monks, the Lord Buddha Viśvabhū (Vessabhū), the worthy one, the perfectly enlightened one, gained his full enlightenment under a sal-tree (sālassa mūle abhisambuddho).

Monks, the Lord Buddha Kakutsandha (Kakusandho), the worthy one, the perfectly enlightened one, gained his full enlightenment under an acacia-tree (sirīsassa mūle abhisambuddho).

Monks, the Lord Buddha Kanakamuni (Koṇāgamano), the worthy one, the perfectly enlightened one, gained his full enlightenment under a fig-tree (udumbarassa mūle abhisambuddho).

Monks, the Lord Buddha Kāśyapa (Kassapo), the worthy one, the perfectly enlightened one, gained his full enlightenment under a banyan-tree (nigrodhassa mūle abhisambuddho).

Monks, I, at this time, the worthy one, the perfectly enlightened one, gained full enlightenment at the foot of an aśvattha tree (assatthassa mūle abhisambuddho).
8. Bodhiparicchede pana pāṭaliyā mūleti pāṭalirukkhassa heṭṭhā. Tassā pana pāṭaliyā khandho taṃ divasaṃ paṇṇāsaratano hutvā abbhuggato, sākhā paṇṇāsaratanāti ubbedhena ratanasataṃ ahosi. Taṃ divasañca sā pāṭali kaṇṇikābaddhehi viya pupphehi mūlato paṭṭhāya ekasañchannā ahosi, dibbagandhaṃ vāyati. Na kevalañca tadā ayameva pupphitā, dasasahassacakkavāḷe sabbapāṭaliyo pupphitā. Na kevalañca pāṭaliyo, dasasahassacakkavāḷe sabbarukkhānaṃ khandhesu khandhapadumāni, sākhāsu sākhāpadumāni, latāsu latāpadumāni, ākāse ākāsapadumāni pupphitāni, pathavitalaṃ bhinditvāpi mahāpadumāni uṭṭhitāni. Mahāsamuddopi pañcavaṇṇehi padumehi nīluppalarattuppalehi ca sañchanno ahosi. Sakaladasasahassacakkavāḷaṃ dhajamālākulaṃ tattha tattha nibaddhapupphadāmavissaṭṭhamālāguḷavippakiṇṇaṃ nānāvaṇṇakusumasamujjalaṃ nandanavanacittalatāvanamissakavanaphārusakavanasadisaṃ ahosi. Puratthimacakkavāḷamukhavaṭṭiyaṃ ussitaddhajā pacchimacakkavāḷamukhavaṭṭiṃ abhihananti. Pacchimadakkhiṇauttaracakkavāḷamukhavaṭṭiyaṃ ussitaddhajā dakkhiṇacakkavāḷamukhavaṭṭiṃ abhihananti. Evaṃ aññamaññasirīsampattāni cakkavāḷāni ahesuṃ. Abhisambuddhoti sakalaṃ buddhaguṇavibhavasiriṃ paṭivijjhamāno cattāri saccāni abhisambuddho. ‘‘Sikhī, bhikkhave, bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho puṇḍarīkassa mūle abhisambuddho’’tiādīsupi imināva nayena padavaṇṇanā veditabbā. Ettha pana puṇḍarīkoti setambarukkho. Tassāpi tadeva parimāṇaṃ. Taṃ divasañca sopi dibbagandhehi pupphehi susañchanno ahosi. Na kevalañca pupphehi, phalehipi sañchanno ahosi. Tassa ekato taruṇāni phalāni, ekato majjhimāni phalāni, ekato nātipakkāni phalāni, ekato supakkāni pakkhittadibbojāni viya surasāni olambanti. Yathā so, evaṃ sakaladasasahassacakkavāḷesu pupphūpagarukkhā pupphehi, phalūpagarukkhā phalehi paṭimaṇḍitā ahesuṃ. puṇḍarīko Sāloti sālarukkho. Tassāpi tadeva parimāṇaṃ, tatheva pupphasirīvibhavo veditabbo. Sirīsarukkhepi eseva nayo. Udumbararukkhe pupphāni nāhesuṃ, phalavibhūti panettha ambe vuttanayāva, tathā nigrodhe, tathā assatthe. Iti sabbabuddhānaṃ ekova pallaṅko, rukkhā pana aññepi honti. Tesu yassa yassa rukkhassa mūle catumaggañāṇasaṅkhātabodhiṃ buddhā paṭivijjhanti, so so bodhīti vuccati. Ayaṃ bodhiparicchedo nāma. Sālo bodhī Sāvakayugaparicchedavaṇṇanā
8. In the bodhipariccheda: "Pāṭaliyā mūle means at the foot of the pāṭali tree, that is to say, beneath [it]." Furthermore, the trunk of that pāṭali tree rose to a height of fifty ratanas on that day, and its branches were fifty ratanas, so its height (ubbedha) was one hundred ratanas. And on that day, that pāṭali tree was entirely covered with flowers like garlands (kaṇṇikābaddhehi), and it exuded a divine fragrance (dibbagandhaṃ vāyati). And not only this one blossomed at that time, but in the ten thousand world-systems (dasasahassacakkavāḷe) all the pāṭalis blossomed. And not only the pāṭalis, but in the ten thousand world-systems, on the trunks of all the trees were trunk-lotuses (khandhapadumāni), on the branches were branch-lotuses (sākhāpadumāni), on the creepers were creeper-lotuses (latāpadumāni), in the sky were sky-lotuses (ākāsapadumāni) — all blossomed. Piercing through the surface of the earth (pathavitalaṃ), great lotuses (mahāpadumāni) arose. The great ocean (mahāsamuddo) was covered with lotuses (padumehi) of five colors (pañcavaṇṇehi), with blue lotuses (nīluppalas) and red lotuses (rattuppalas). The entire ten thousand world-system (sakaladasasahassacakkavāḷaṃ) was a mass of flags and garlands (dhajamālākulaṃ), scattered with tied-up flower-ropes and garlands (nibaddhapupphadāmavissaṭṭhamālāguḷavippakiṇṇaṃ), radiant with various colored flowers (nānāvaṇṇakusumasamujjalaṃ), like the Nandana garden, the Cittalatā grove, the mixed forest, and the Phārusaka forest (nandanavanacittalatāvanamissakavanaphārusakavanasadisaṃ)! In the eastern world-system-mouth-circle (puratthimacakkavāḷamukhavaṭṭiyaṃ), raised flags (ussitaddhajā) strike against the western world-system-mouth-circle (pacchimacakkavāḷamukhavaṭṭiṃ abhihananti). In the western, southern, and northern world-system-mouth-circle (pacchimadakkhiṇauttaracakkavāḷamukhavaṭṭiyaṃ), raised flags strike against the southern world-system-mouth-circle (dakkhiṇacakkavāḷamukhavaṭṭiṃ abhihananti). Thus, the world-systems (cakkavāḷāni) attained mutual glory (aññamaññasirīsampattāni ahesuṃ). "Abhisambuddho means, comprehending all the glory (sakalaṃ buddhaguṇavibhavasiriṃ paṭivijjhamāno) of the qualities and powers of a Buddha, fully awakened to the four Noble Truths (cattāri saccāni abhisambuddho)."

"Sikhī, bhikkhave, the Blessed One, the arahaṃ, the perfectly awakened Buddha, was fully awakened at the foot of the puṇḍarīka" — in these and other [passages], the explanation of the words (padavaṇṇanā) should be understood in this way (imināva nayena veditabbā). Here, however, puṇḍarīko means the white-sandal tree (setambarukkho). Its measurement is the same (tassāpi tadeva parimāṇaṃ). And on that day, that one too (taṃ divasañca sopi) was well covered (susañchanno ahesi) with divine-fragranced (dibbagandhehi) flowers (pupphehi). Not only with flowers, but he was also covered with fruits (phalehipi sañchanno ahosi). On one side (ekato) were young fruits (taruṇāni phalāni), on one side were middling fruits (majjhimāni phalāni), on one side were not-yet-ripe fruits (nātipakkāni phalāni), on one side were very ripe fruits (supakkāni) hanging down (olambanti), delicious (surasāni) like deposited divine-essences (pakkhittadibbojāni viya). Just as that one was, so too in all the ten thousand world-systems (sakaladasasahassacakkavāḷesu), flower-bearing trees (pupphūpagarukkhā) were adorned with flowers (pupphehi), fruit-bearing trees (phalūpagarukkhā) were adorned with fruits (phalehi paṭimaṇḍitā ahesuṃ).

Puṇḍarīko. Sālo means the sāla tree (sālarukkho). Its measurement is the same (tassāpi tadeva parimāṇaṃ), just so the glory of flowers and power (tatheva pupphasirīvibhavo) should be understood (veditabbo). In the sirīsa tree too (sirīsarukkhepi) this is the same method (eseva nayo). In the udumbara tree there were no flowers (udumbararukkhe pupphāni nāhesuṃ), but the splendor of the fruits (phalavibhūti) was like what was said in the case of the mangoes (ambe vuttanayāva), and likewise in the nigrodha, and likewise in the assattha. Thus, for all Buddhas there is only one seat (ekova pallaṅko), but the trees are different (rukkhā pana aññepi honti). Among them, whichever tree (tesu yassa yassa rukkhassa) at the foot of which the Buddhas comprehend the bodhi, reckoned as the knowledge of the four paths (catumaggañāṇasaṅkhātabodhiṃ buddhā paṭivijjhanti), that one is called "bodhī" (so so bodhīti vuccati). This is called bodhipariccheda.

Sālo. Bodhī. Explanation of the definition of the pair of disciples (sāvakayugaparicchedavaṇṇanā).
9
9. ‘‘Vipassissa , bhikkhave, bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa khaṇḍatissaṃ nāma sāvakayugaṃ ahosi aggaṃ bhaddayugaṃ. Sikhissa, bhikkhave, bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa abhibhūsambhavaṃ nāma sāvakayugaṃ ahosi aggaṃ bhaddayugaṃ. Vessabhussa, bhikkhave, bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa soṇuttaraṃ nāma sāvakayugaṃ ahosi aggaṃ bhaddayugaṃ. Kakusandhassa, bhikkhave, bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa vidhurasañjīvaṃ nāma sāvakayugaṃ ahosi aggaṃ bhaddayugaṃ. Koṇāgamanassa, bhikkhave, bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa bhiyyosuttaraṃ nāma sāvakayugaṃ ahosi aggaṃ bhaddayugaṃ. Kassapassa, bhikkhave, bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa tissabhāradvājaṃ nāma sāvakayugaṃ ahosi aggaṃ bhaddayugaṃ. Mayhaṃ, bhikkhave, etarahi sāriputtamoggallānaṃ nāma sāvakayugaṃ ahosi aggaṃ bhaddayugaṃ.
“Monks, the Blessed One Vipassi, worthy and perfectly enlightened, had a pair of noble disciples named Khaṇḍa and Tissa as his chief and auspicious pair. The Blessed One Sikhi, worthy and perfectly enlightened, had a pair of noble disciples named Abhibhu and Sambhava as his chief and auspicious pair. The Blessed One Vessabhu, worthy and perfectly enlightened, had a pair of noble disciples named Sona and Uttara as his chief and auspicious pair. The Blessed One Kakusandha, worthy and perfectly enlightened, had a pair of noble disciples named Vidhura and Sañjīva as his chief and auspicious pair. The Blessed One Koṇāgamana, worthy and perfectly enlightened, had a pair of noble disciples named Bhiyyosa and Uttara as his chief and auspicious pair. The Blessed One Kassapa, worthy and perfectly enlightened, had a pair of noble disciples named Tissa and Bharadvaja as his chief and auspicious pair. Monks, I myself now have the pair of noble disciples Sariputta and Moggallana as my chief and auspicious pair.
9. Sāvakayugaparicchede pana khaṇḍatissanti khaṇḍo ca tisso ca. Tesu khaṇḍo ekapitiko kaniṭṭhabhātā, tisso purohitaputto . Khaṇḍo paññāpāramiyā matthakaṃ patto, tisso samādhipāramiyā matthakaṃ patto. Agganti ṭhapetvā vipassiṃ bhagavantaṃ avasesehi saddhiṃ asadisaguṇatāya uttamaṃ. Bhaddayuganti aggattāyeva bhaddayugaṃ. Abhibhūsambhavanti abhibhū ca sambhavo ca. Tesu abhibhū paññāpāramiyā matthakaṃ patto. Sikhinā bhagavatā saddhiṃ aruṇavatito brahmalokaṃ gantvā brahmaparisāya vividhāni pāṭihāriyāni dassento dhammaṃ desetvā dasasahassilokadhātuṃ andhakārena pharitvā – ‘‘kiṃ ida’’nti sañjātasaṃvegānaṃ obhāsaṃ pharitvā – ‘‘sabbe me rūpañca passantu, saddañca suṇantū’’ti adhiṭṭhahitvā – ‘‘ārambhathā’’ti gāthādvayaṃ (saṃ. ni. 1.185) bhaṇanto saddaṃ sāvesi. Sambhavo samādhipāramiyā matthakaṃ patto ahosi. Soṇuttaranti soṇo ca uttaro ca. Tesupi soṇo paññāpāramiṃ patto, uttaro samādhipāramiṃ patto ahosi. Vidhurasañjīvanti vidhuro ca sañjīvo ca. Tesu vidhuro paññāpāramiṃ patto ahosi, sañjīvo samādhipāramiṃ patto. Samāpajjanabahulo rattiṭṭhānadivāṭṭhānakuṭileṇamaṇḍapādīsu samāpattibalena jhāyanto ekadivasaṃ araññe nirodhaṃ samāpajji, atha naṃ vanakammikādayo ‘‘mato’’ti sallakkhetvā jhāpesuṃ. So yathāparicchedena samāpattito uṭṭhāya cīvarāni papphoṭetvā gāmaṃ piṇḍāya pāvisi. Tadupādāyeva ca naṃ ‘‘sañjīvo’’ti sañjāniṃsu. Bhiyyosuttaranti bhiyyoso ca uttaro ca. Tesu bhiyyoso paññāya uttaro, uttaro samādhinā aggo ahosi. Tissabhāradvājanti tisso ca bhāradvājo ca . Tesu tisso paññāpāramiṃ patto, bhāradvājo samādhipāramiṃ patto ahosi . Sāriputtamoggallānanti sāriputto ca moggallāno ca. Tesu sāriputto paññāvisaye, moggallāno samādhivisaye aggo ahosi. Ayaṃ sāvakayugaparicchedo nāma. Soṇuttara Vidhurasañjīva Bhiyyosuttara Tissabhāradvāja Sāriputtamoggallāna Sāvakasannipātaparicchedavaṇṇanā
9. In the section on pairs of disciples: Khaṇḍatissa means Khaṇḍa and Tissa.

Among them, Khaṇḍa was the younger brother with one father, and Tissa was the son of a chaplain.

Khaṇḍa reached the summit of the perfection of wisdom ( paññāpāramī), and Tissa reached the summit of the perfection of concentration ( samādhipāramī).

"Agga" means the best, most excellent, because of possessing unique qualities along with Vipassī, the Blessed One, setting him apart.

Bhaddayuga means a blessed pair, indeed a blessed pair is "aggattāyeva".

Abhibhūsambhava means Abhibhū and Sambhava.

Among them, Abhibhū reached the summit of the perfection of wisdom (paññāpāramī).

Sikhī, the Blessed One, together with the Brahma world from Aruṇavatī, having gone to the assembly of Brahmās, showing various miracles, teaching the Dharma, having pervaded the ten-thousand world-systems with darkness – "What is this?" – having pervaded the light for those whose agitation had arisen – "May all of them see my form and hear my voice," having resolved – reciting the two verses "Ārambhathā" (Saṃyutta Nikāya 1.1.185) he announced the sound.

Sambhava reached the summit of the perfection of concentration (samādhipāramī).

Soṇuttara means Soṇa and Uttara.

Among them, Soṇa reached the perfection of wisdom (paññāpāramī), and Uttara reached the perfection of concentration (samādhipāramī).

Vidhurasañjīva means Vidhura and Sañjīva.

Among them, Vidhura reached the perfection of wisdom (paññāpāramī), and Sañjīva reached the perfection of concentration (samādhipāramī).

Being abundant in entering into meditative states, dwelling in night places and day places, caves and pavilions, meditating by the power of attainment, one day he entered cessation (nirodha) in the forest, then the forest workers and others, thinking “he is dead” cremated him.

So, as defined, having arisen from attainment, shaking out his robes, he entered the village for alms.

From that cause they recognized him as "Sañjīva."

Bhiyyosuttara means Bhiyyosa and Uttara.

Among them, Bhiyyosa was pre-eminent in wisdom, and Uttara was best in concentration.

Tissabhāradvāja means Tissa and Bhāradvāja.

Among them, Tissa reached the perfection of wisdom (paññāpāramī), and Bhāradvāja reached the perfection of concentration (samādhipāramī).

Sāriputtamoggallāna means Śāriputta and Maudgalyāyana.

Among them, Śāriputta was pre-eminent in the realm of wisdom, and Maudgalyāyana was pre-eminent in the realm of concentration (samādhi).

This is called the section on pairs of disciples.

Soṇuttara Vidhurasañjīva Bhiyyosuttara Tissabhāradvāja

Sāriputtamoggallāna

Explanation of the Section on the Gathering of Disciples
10
10. ‘‘Vipassissa, bhikkhave, bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa tayo sāvakānaṃ sannipātā ahesuṃ. Eko sāvakānaṃ sannipāto ahosi aṭṭhasaṭṭhibhikkhusatasahassaṃ, eko sāvakānaṃ sannipāto ahosi bhikkhusatasahassaṃ, eko sāvakānaṃ sannipāto ahosi asītibhikkhusahassāni. Vipassissa, bhikkhave, bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa ime tayo sāvakānaṃ sannipātā ahesuṃ sabbesaṃyeva khīṇāsavānaṃ. ‘‘Sikhissa, bhikkhave, bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa tayo sāvakānaṃ sannipātā ahesuṃ. Eko sāvakānaṃ sannipāto ahosi bhikkhusatasahassaṃ, eko sāvakānaṃ sannipāto ahosi asītibhikkhusahassāni, eko sāvakānaṃ sannipāto ahosi sattatibhikkhusahassāni. Sikhissa, bhikkhave, bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa ime tayo sāvakānaṃ sannipātā ahesuṃ sabbesaṃyeva khīṇāsavānaṃ. ‘‘Vessabhussa, bhikkhave, bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa tayo sāvakānaṃ sannipātā ahesuṃ. Eko sāvakānaṃ sannipāto ahosi asītibhikkhusahassāni, eko sāvakānaṃ sannipāto ahosi sattatibhikkhusahassāni, eko sāvakānaṃ sannipāto ahosi saṭṭhibhikkhusahassāni. Vessabhussa, bhikkhave, bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa ime tayo sāvakānaṃ sannipātā ahesuṃ sabbesaṃyeva khīṇāsavānaṃ. ‘‘Kakusandhassa, bhikkhave, bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa eko sāvakānaṃ sannipāto ahosi cattālīsabhikkhusahassāni. Kakusandhassa, bhikkhave, bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa ayaṃ eko sāvakānaṃ sannipāto ahosi sabbesaṃyeva khīṇāsavānaṃ. ‘‘Koṇāgamanassa, bhikkhave, bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa eko sāvakānaṃ sannipāto ahosi tiṃsabhikkhusahassāni. Koṇāgamanassa, bhikkhave, bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa ayaṃ eko sāvakānaṃ sannipāto ahosi sabbesaṃyeva khīṇāsavānaṃ. ‘‘Kassapassa, bhikkhave, bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa eko sāvakānaṃ sannipāto ahosi vīsatibhikkhusahassāni. Kassapassa, bhikkhave, bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa ayaṃ eko sāvakānaṃ sannipāto ahosi sabbesaṃyeva khīṇāsavānaṃ. ‘‘Mayhaṃ, bhikkhave, etarahi eko sāvakānaṃ sannipāto ahosi aḍḍhateḷasāni bhikkhusatāni. Mayhaṃ, bhikkhave, ayaṃ eko sāvakānaṃ sannipāto ahosi sabbesaṃyeva khīṇāsavānaṃ.
10. “Vipassi, monks, the Blessed One, the Worthy One, the Perfectly Enlightened One, had three assemblies of disciples.

One assembly of disciples was of six million eight hundred thousand monks, one assembly of disciples was of one hundred thousand monks, and one assembly of disciples was of eighty thousand monks.

Vipassi, monks, the Blessed One, the Worthy One, the Perfectly Enlightened One, had these three assemblies of disciples, all of whom were those whose corruptions were destroyed ( khīṇāsava ).

“Sikhi, monks, the Blessed One, the Worthy One, the Perfectly Enlightened One, had three assemblies of disciples.

One assembly of disciples was of one hundred thousand monks, one assembly of disciples was of eighty thousand monks, and one assembly of disciples was of seventy thousand monks.

Sikhi, monks, the Blessed One, the Worthy One, the Perfectly Enlightened One, had these three assemblies of disciples, all of whom were those whose corruptions were destroyed ( khīṇāsava ).

“Vessabhu, monks, the Blessed One, the Worthy One, the Perfectly Enlightened One, had three assemblies of disciples.

One assembly of disciples was of eighty thousand monks, one assembly of disciples was of seventy thousand monks, and one assembly of disciples was of sixty thousand monks.

Vessabhu, monks, the Blessed One, the Worthy One, the Perfectly Enlightened One, had these three assemblies of disciples, all of whom were those whose corruptions were destroyed ( khīṇāsava ).

“Kakusandha, monks, the Blessed One, the Worthy One, the Perfectly Enlightened One, had one assembly of disciples of forty thousand monks.

Kakusandha, monks, the Blessed One, the Worthy One, the Perfectly Enlightened One, had this one assembly of disciples, all of whom were those whose corruptions were destroyed ( khīṇāsava ).

“Koṇāgamana, monks, the Blessed One, the Worthy One, the Perfectly Enlightened One, had one assembly of disciples of thirty thousand monks.

Koṇāgamana, monks, the Blessed One, the Worthy One, the Perfectly Enlightened One, had this one assembly of disciples, all of whom were those whose corruptions were destroyed ( khīṇāsava ).

“Kassapa, monks, the Blessed One, the Worthy One, the Perfectly Enlightened One, had one assembly of disciples of twenty thousand monks.

Kassapa, monks, the Blessed One, the Worthy One, the Perfectly Enlightened One, had this one assembly of disciples, all of whom were those whose corruptions were destroyed ( khīṇāsava ).

“Now, monks, I have one assembly of disciples of twelve hundred and fifty monks.

I, monks, have this one assembly of disciples, all of whom are those whose corruptions are destroyed ( khīṇāsava ).
10. Sāvakasannipātaparicchede vipassissa bhagavato paṭhamasannipāto caturaṅgiko ahosi, sabbe ehibhikkhū, sabbe iddhiyā nibbattapattacīvarā, sabbe anāmantitāva āgatā, iti te ca kho pannarase uposathadivase. Atha satthā bījaniṃ gahetvā nisinno uposathaṃ osāresi. Dutiyatatiyesupi eseva nayo. Tathā sesabuddhānaṃ sabbasannipātesu. Yasmā pana amhākaṃ bhagavato paṭhamabodhiyāva sannipāto ahosi, idañca suttaṃ aparabhāge vuttaṃ, tasmā ‘‘mayhaṃ, bhikkhave, etarahi eko sāvakānaṃ sannipāto’’ti aniṭṭhapetvā ‘‘ahosī’’ti vuttaṃ. Tattha aḍḍhateḷasāni bhikkhusatānīti purāṇajaṭilānaṃ sahassaṃ, dvinnaṃ aggasāvakānaṃ parivārāni aḍḍhateyyasatānīti aḍḍhateḷasāni bhikkhusatāni. Tattha dvinnaṃ aggasāvakānaṃ abhinīhārato paṭṭhāya vatthuṃ kathetvā pabbajjā dīpetabbā. Pabbajitānaṃ pana tesaṃ mahāmoggallāno sattame divase arahattaṃ patto. Dhammasenāpati pannarasame divase gijjhakūṭapabbatamajjhe sūkarakhataleṇapabbhāre bhāgineyyassa dīghanakhaparibbājakassa sajjite dhammayāge vedanāpariggahasuttante (ma. ni. 2.201) desiyamāne desanaṃ anubujjhamānaṃ ñāṇaṃ pesetvā sāvakapāramiñāṇaṃ patto. Bhagavā therassa arahattappattiṃ ñatvā vehāsaṃ abbhuggantvā veḷuvaneyeva paccuṭṭhāsi. Thero – ‘‘kuhiṃ nu kho bhagavā gato’’ti āvajjanto veḷuvane patiṭṭhitabhāvaṃ ñatvā sayampi vehāsaṃ abbhuggantvā veḷuvaneyeva paccuṭṭhāsi. Atha bhagavā pātimokkhaṃ osāresi. Taṃ sannipātaṃ sandhāya bhagavā – ‘‘aḍḍhateḷasāni bhikkhusatānī’’ti āha. Ayaṃ sāvakasannipātaparicchedo nāma. aḍḍhateḷasāni bhikkhusatānī Upaṭṭhākaparicchedavaṇṇanā
10. In the section on the assembly of disciples, the Blessed Vipassi's first assembly was fourfold: all were "Come, monks!"; all had bowls and robes created by psychic power; all came without being summoned; and so they were on the fifteenth, the day of the observance. Then the Teacher, having taken a fan, sat down and concluded the observance. The same rule applied in the second and third [assemblies]. So it was in all the assemblies of the remaining Buddhas. But since for our Blessed One the assembly occurred at the first awakening itself, and this sutra was stated in a later portion, therefore, without establishing "Now, monks, there is one assembly of disciples for me," it was stated "There was."

There, one thousand two hundred and fifty monks means one thousand former ascetics, and five hundred disciples of the two chief disciples, thus one thousand two hundred and fifty monks. There, having told the story starting from the aspiration of the two chief disciples, the going forth should be explained. Of those who had gone forth, Mahāmoggallāna attained arahantship on the seventh day. The General of the Dhamma, on the fifteenth day, in the cave-pavilion at Sūkarakhata on the middle of Gijjhakūṭa Mountain, while the Vedanāpariggaha Sutta (M. Ni. 2.201) was being taught in the Dhamma-sacrifice arranged for his nephew, the wanderer Dīghanakha, having sent forth knowledge apprehending the teaching, attained the knowledge of a disciple's perfection. The Blessed One, having known the elder's attainment of arahantship, rose up into the sky and stood in the Veḷuvana itself. The elder, thinking, "Where, now, has the Blessed One gone?" having known his state of being established in the Veḷuvana, himself also rose up into the sky and stood in the Veḷuvana itself. Then the Blessed One concluded the Pāṭimokkha. With reference to that assembly, the Blessed One said, "One thousand two hundred and fifty monks." This is called the section on the assembly of disciples.

One thousand two hundred and fifty monks

Explanation of the section on attendants.
11
11. ‘‘Vipassissa, bhikkhave, bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa asoko nāma bhikkhu upaṭṭhāko ahosi aggupaṭṭhāko. Sikhissa, bhikkhave, bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa khemaṅkaro nāma bhikkhu upaṭṭhāko ahosi aggupaṭṭhāko. Vessabhussa, bhikkhave, bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa upasanto nāma bhikkhu upaṭṭhāko ahosi aggupaṭṭhāko. Kakusandhassa, bhikkhave, bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa buddhijo nāma bhikkhu upaṭṭhāko ahosi aggupaṭṭhāko. Koṇāgamanassa, bhikkhave, bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa sotthijo nāma bhikkhu upaṭṭhāko ahosi aggupaṭṭhāko. Kassapassa, bhikkhave, bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa sabbamitto nāma bhikkhu upaṭṭhāko ahosi aggupaṭṭhāko. Mayhaṃ, bhikkhave, etarahi ānando nāma bhikkhu upaṭṭhāko ahosi aggupaṭṭhāko.
“Monks, the chief personal attendant of the Blessed Lord Vipassi, the worthy, fully enlightened Buddha, was the monk Asoka.

“Monks, the chief personal attendant of the Blessed Lord Sikhi, the worthy, fully enlightened Buddha, was the monk Khemankara.

“Monks, the chief personal attendant of the Blessed Lord Vessabhu, the worthy, fully enlightened Buddha, was the monk Upasanta.

“Monks, the chief personal attendant of the Blessed Lord Kakusandha, the worthy, fully enlightened Buddha, was the monk Buddhija.

“Monks, the chief personal attendant of the Blessed Lord Konagamana, the worthy, fully enlightened Buddha, was the monk Sotthija.

“Monks, the chief personal attendant of the Blessed Lord Kassapa, the worthy, fully enlightened Buddha, was the monk Sabbamitta.

“Monks, my chief personal attendant now is Ananda.
11. Upaṭṭhākaparicchede pana ānandoti nibaddhupaṭṭhākabhāvaṃ sandhāya vuttaṃ. Bhagavato hi paṭhamabodhiyaṃ anibaddhā upaṭṭhākā ahesuṃ. Ekadā nāgasamālo pattacīvaraṃ gahetvā vicari, ekadā nāgito, ekadā upavāno, ekadā sunakkhatto, ekadā cundo samaṇuddeso, ekadā sāgato , ekadā meghiyo. Tattha ekadā bhagavā nāgasamālattherena saddhiṃ addhānamaggapaṭipanno dvedhāpathaṃ patto. Thero maggā okkamma – ‘‘bhagavā, ahaṃ iminā maggena gacchāmī’’ti āha. Atha naṃ bhagavā – ‘‘ehi bhikkhu, iminā maggena gacchāmā’’ti āha. So – ‘‘handa, bhagavā, tumhākaṃ pattacīvaraṃ gaṇhatha, ahaṃ iminā maggena gacchāmī’’ti vatvā pattacīvaraṃ chamāyaṃ ṭhapetuṃ āraddho. Atha naṃ bhagavā – ‘‘āhara, bhikkhū’’ti vatvā pattacīvaraṃ gahetvā gato. Tassapi bhikkhuno itarena maggena gacchato corā pattacīvarañceva hariṃsu, sīsañca bhindiṃsu. So – ‘‘bhagavā idāni me paṭisaraṇaṃ, na añño’’ti cintetvā lohitena gaḷitena bhagavato santikaṃ agamāsi. ‘‘Kimidaṃ bhikkhū’’ti ca vutte taṃ pavattiṃ ārocesi. Atha naṃ bhagavā – ‘‘mā cintayi, bhikkhu, etaṃyeva te kāraṇaṃ sallakkhetvā nivārayimhā’’ti vatvā naṃ samassāsesi. Ekadā pana bhagavā meghiyattherena saddhiṃ pācīnavaṃsamigadāye jantugāmaṃ agamāsi. Tatrāpi meghiyo jantugāme piṇḍāya caritvā nadītīre pāsādikaṃ ambavanaṃ disvā – ‘‘bhagavā, tumhākaṃ pattacīvaraṃ gaṇhatha, ahaṃ tasmiṃ ambavane samaṇadhammaṃ karomī’’ti vatvā bhagavatā tikkhattuṃ nivāriyamānopi gantvā akusalavitakkehi upadduto anvāsatto (a. ni. 9.3; udāna paricchedo 31 daṭṭhabbo). Paccāgantvā taṃ pavattiṃ ārocesi. Tampi bhagavā – ‘‘idameva te kāraṇaṃ sallakkhetvā nivārayimhā’’ti vatvā anupubbena sāvatthiṃ agamāsi. Tattha gandhakuṭipariveṇe paññattavarabuddhāsane nisinno bhikkhusaṅghaparivuto bhikkhū āmantesi – ‘‘bhikkhave, idānimhi mahallako, ‘ekacce bhikkhū iminā maggena gacchāmā’ti vutte aññena gacchanti, ekacce mayhaṃ pattacīvaraṃ nikkhipanti, mayhaṃ nibaddhupaṭṭhākaṃ ekaṃ bhikkhuṃ jānāthā’’ti. Bhikkhūnaṃ dhammasaṃvego udapādi. Athāyasmā sāriputto uṭṭhāyāsanā bhagavantaṃ vanditvā – ‘‘ahaṃ , bhante, tumheyeva patthayamāno satasahassakappādhikaṃ asaṅkhyeyyaṃ pāramiyo pūrayiṃ, nanu mādiso mahāpañño upaṭṭhāko nāma vaṭṭati, ahaṃ upaṭṭhahissāmī’’ti āha. Taṃ bhagavā – ‘‘alaṃ sāriputta, yassaṃ disāyaṃ tvaṃ viharasi, asuññāyeva me sā disā, tava ovādo buddhānaṃ ovādasadiso, na me tayā upaṭṭhākakiccaṃ atthī’’ti paṭikkhipi. Etenevupāyena mahāmoggallānaṃ ādiṃ katvā asītimahāsāvakā uṭṭhahiṃsu. Te sabbepi bhagavā paṭikkhipi. Ānandatthero pana tuṇhīyeva nisīdi. Atha naṃ bhikkhū evamāhaṃsu – ‘‘āvuso, ānanda, bhikkhusaṅgho upaṭṭhākaṭṭhānaṃ yācati, tvampi yācāhī’’ti. So āha – ‘‘yācitvā laddhupaṭṭhānaṃ nāma āvuso kīdisaṃ hoti, kiṃ maṃ satthā na passati, sace rocissati, ānando maṃ upaṭṭhātūti vakkhatī’’ti. Atha bhagavā – ‘‘na, bhikkhave, ānando aññena ussāhetabbo, sayameva jānitvā maṃ upaṭṭhahissatī’’ti āha. Tato bhikkhū – ‘‘uṭṭhehi, āvuso ānanda, uṭṭhehi āvuso ānanda, dasabalaṃ upaṭṭhākaṭṭhānaṃ yācāhī’’ti āhaṃsu. Thero uṭṭhahitvā cattāro paṭikkhepe, catasso ca āyācanāti aṭṭha vare yāci. Cattāro paṭikkhepā nāma – ‘‘sace me, bhante, bhagavā attanā laddhaṃ paṇītaṃ cīvaraṃ na dassati, piṇḍapātaṃ na dassati, ekagandhakuṭiyaṃ vasituṃ na dassati, nimantanaṃ gahetvā na gamissati, evāhaṃ bhagavantaṃ upaṭṭhahissāmī’’ti vatvā – ‘‘kiṃ panettha, ānanda, ādīnavaṃ passasī’’ti vutte – ‘‘sacāhaṃ, bhante, imāni vatthūni labhissāmi, bhavissanti vattāro – ‘ānando dasabalena laddhaṃ paṇītaṃ cīvaraṃ paribhuñjati, piṇḍapātaṃ paribhuñjati, ekagandhakuṭiyaṃ vasati, ekato nimantanaṃ gacchati, etaṃ lābhaṃ labhanto tathāgataṃ upaṭṭhāti, ko evaṃ upaṭṭhahato bhāro’ti’’ ime cattāro paṭikkhepe yāci. Catasso āyācanā nāma – ‘‘sace, bhante, bhagavā mayā gahitanimantanaṃ gamissati, sacāhaṃ tiroraṭṭhā tirojanapadā bhagavantaṃ daṭṭhuṃ āgataṃ parisaṃ āgatakkhaṇe eva bhagavantaṃ dassetuṃ lacchāmi, yadā me kaṅkhā uppajjati, tasmiṃyeva khaṇe bhagavantaṃ upasaṅkamituṃ lacchāmi, yaṃ bhagavā mayhaṃ parammukhā dhammaṃ deseti, taṃ āgantvā mayhaṃ kathessati, evāhaṃ bhagavantaṃ upaṭṭhahissāmī’’ti vatvā – ‘‘kaṃ panettha, ānanda, ānisaṃsaṃ passasī’’ti vutte – ‘‘idha, bhante, saddhā kulaputtā bhagavato okāsaṃ alabhantā maṃ evaṃ vadanti – ‘sve, bhante ānanda, bhagavatā saddhiṃ amhākaṃ ghare bhikkhaṃ gaṇheyyāthā’ti, sace bhante bhagavā tattha na gamissati, icchitakkhaṇeyeva parisaṃ dassetuṃ, kaṅkhañca vinodetuṃ okāsaṃ na lacchāmi, bhavissanti vattāro – ‘kiṃ ānando dasabalaṃ upaṭṭhāti, ettakampissa anuggahaṃ bhagavā na karotī’ti. Bhagavato ca parammukhā maṃ pucchissanti – ‘ayaṃ, āvuso ānanda, gāthā, idaṃ suttaṃ, idaṃ jātakaṃ, kattha desita’nti. Sacāhaṃ taṃ na sampādayissāmi, bhavissanti vattāro – ‘ettakampi, āvuso, na jānāsi, kasmā tvaṃ chāyā viya bhagavantaṃ avijahanto dīgharattaṃ vicarasī’ti. Tenāhaṃ parammukhā desitassapi dhammassa puna kathanaṃ icchāmī’’ti imā catasso āyācanā yāci. Bhagavāpissa adāsi. Evaṃ ime aṭṭha vare gahetvā nibaddhupaṭṭhāko ahosi. Tasseva ṭhānantarassatthāya kappasatasahassaṃ pūritānaṃ pāramīnaṃ phalaṃ pāpuṇīti imassa nibaddhupaṭṭhākabhāvaṃ sandhāya – ‘‘mayhaṃ, bhikkhave, etarahi ānando bhikkhu upaṭṭhāko aggupaṭṭhāko’’ti āha. Ayaṃ upaṭṭhākaparicchedo nāma.
11. In the upaṭṭhāka-pariccheda, it is said referring to Ānanda, "the state of being a constant attendant." Indeed, the Blessed One did not have constant attendants during the first awakening. Once Nāgasamāla went wandering, taking his bowl and robe; once Nāgita, once Upavāna, once Sunakkhatta, once the novice Cunda, once Sāgata, once Meghiya.

There, once, the Blessed One, along with Nāgasamāla Thera, was traveling along a road and came to a fork in the road. The Thera left the road and said, "Blessed One, I will go by this road." Then the Blessed One said to him, "Come, monk, let us go by this road." He said, "Here, Blessed One, take your bowl and robe; I will go by this road," and having said this, he began to place his bowl and robe on the ground. Then the Blessed One said to him, "Take it, monk," and having taken the bowl and robe, he went away. While that monk was going along the other road, thieves stole his bowl and robe, and they split his head. He, thinking, "Now the Blessed One is my refuge, there is no other," bleeding, went to the Blessed One. When it was said, "What is this, monks?" he announced that event. Then the Blessed One, having said to him, "Do not worry, monk, having considered this very reason for you, we prevented it," consoled him.

Once, moreover, the Blessed One, together with Meghiya Thera, went to Jantugāma in Pācīnavaṃsamigadāya. There also, Meghiya, having wandered for alms in Jantugāma, seeing a delightful mango grove on the bank of the river, said, "Blessed One, take your bowl and robe, I will practice the śrāmaṇadharma in that mango grove," and even though he was prevented three times by the Blessed One, having gone, afflicted and pursued by unwholesome thoughts (A.Ni. 9.3; udāna-pariccheda 31 should be seen). Having returned, he announced that event. The Blessed One also, having said, "Having considered this very reason for you, we prevented it," gradually went to Śrāvastī.

There, in the Gandhakuṭi cloister, seated on the proclaimed excellent Buddha seat, surrounded by the community of monks, he addressed the monks: "Monks, now I am old; when it is said, 'Some monks, let us go by this road,' they go by another; some leave my bowl and robe; do you know a monk who is my constant attendant?" A sense of religious urgency arose in the monks. Then the venerable Śāriputra, rising from his seat, having bowed to the Blessed One, said, "I, venerable sir, only desiring you, have fulfilled countless pāramīs for hundreds of thousands of kappas; is an attendant named such a wise one like me suitable? I will attend," he said. The Blessed One said to him, "Enough, Śāriputra, the direction in which you dwell, that direction is indeed not empty for me; your advice is like the advice of the Buddhas; I do not have a need for an attendant's duty from you," he rejected [him]. In this very way, beginning with Mahāmoggallāna, eighty great disciples rose up. The Blessed One rejected all of them.

Ānanda Thera, however, sat down silently. Then the monks said to him thus: "Friend Ānanda, the community of monks requests the position of attendant; you also request it." He said, "What is the attendant's position obtained by requesting, friend? Does the Teacher not see me? If it will please [him], he will say, 'Let Ānanda attend me.'" Then the Blessed One said, "No, monks, Ānanda should not be urged by another, having known [it] himself, he will attend me." Thereupon the monks said, "Rise, friend Ānanda, rise, friend Ānanda, request the position of attendant from the Ten-Powered One!" The Thera, having risen, requested eight boons: four rejections and four requests.
Four Rejections: These are called "four rejections": "If, venerable sir, the Blessed One does not give me a fine robe he has obtained himself, does not give me almsfood, does not give me lodging in a private chamber, does not go to an invitation after accepting it, even so, I will attend upon the Blessed One." Having said this, [the Buddha asks,] "What drawback do you see in this, Ānanda?" [Ānanda replies,] "If, venerable sir, I should obtain these things, there will be those who say, 'Ānanda enjoys the fine robe that was obtained by the Ten-Powered One, enjoys the almsfood, dwells in a private chamber, goes alone to invitations. He attends upon the Tathāgata, obtaining this gain. Who would attend like that, for the sake of reward?'" These are the four rejections that he requested.

Four Requests: These are called "four requests": "If, venerable sir, the Blessed One goes to an invitation that I have accepted, if I can show the Blessed One to the assembly that has come from foreign lands and districts to see the Blessed One, at the very moment of their arrival; when doubt arises for me, if I can approach the Blessed One at that very moment; [and] if the Blessed One preaches the Dharma turning away from me, may he come and tell it to me, even so, I will attend upon the Blessed One." Having said this, [the Buddha asks,] "What benefit do you see in this, Ānanda?" When asked, "[Here,] venerable sir, faithful sons of good families, not obtaining an opportunity from the Blessed One, say this to me: 'Tomorrow, venerable Ānanda, the Blessed One will accept almsfood together with you in our house.' If, venerable sir, the Blessed One does not go there, I will not obtain an opportunity to show the assembly at the very moment desired, and to dispel doubt. There will be those who say, 'What does Ānanda attend upon the Ten-Powered One for? The Blessed One does not show even so much favor to him.'

"And, turning away from the Blessed One, they will ask me, 'Friend Ānanda, where was this verse, this sūtra, this jātaka taught?' If I do not accomplish that, there will be those who say, 'You do not even know this much, friend? Why do you, like a shadow, attend upon the Blessed One day and night, without leaving him?'"

"Therefore, I desire a repeated telling of the Dharma, even when preached turning away.” These are the four requests that he requested.

The Blessed One granted them to him.

In this way, having taken these eight boons, he became a constant attendant.

For the sake of the subsequent benefit of that, in reliance upon the constant state of attendance, that he would attain the fruit of the perfections which had been fulfilled for hundreds of thousands of aeons, [the Buddha] said: "Now, monks, Ānanda is my attendant, the foremost attendant."

This is called the chapter on the attendant.
12
12. ‘‘Vipassissa, bhikkhave, bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa bandhumā nāma rājā pitā ahosi. Bandhumatī nāma devī mātā ahosi janetti janettī (syā.). Bandhumassa rañño bandhumatī nāma nagaraṃ rājadhānī ahosi. ‘‘Sikhissa , bhikkhave, bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa aruṇo nāma rājā pitā ahosi. Pabhāvatī nāma devī mātā ahosi janetti. Aruṇassa rañño aruṇavatī nāma nagaraṃ rājadhānī ahosi. ‘‘Vessabhussa, bhikkhave, bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa suppatito nāma suppatīto nāma (syā.) rājā pitā ahosi. Vassavatī nāma yasavatī nāma (syā. pī.) devī mātā ahosi janetti. Suppatitassa rañño anomaṃ nāma nagaraṃ rājadhānī ahosi. suppatīto nāma (syā.) yasavatī nāma (syā. pī.) ‘‘Kakusandhassa, bhikkhave, bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa aggidatto nāma brāhmaṇo pitā ahosi. Visākhā nāma brāhmaṇī mātā ahosi janetti. Tena kho pana, bhikkhave, samayena khemo nāma rājā ahosi. Khemassa rañño khemavatī nāma nagaraṃ rājadhānī ahosi. ‘‘Koṇāgamanassa, bhikkhave, bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa yaññadatto nāma brāhmaṇo pitā ahosi. Uttarā nāma brāhmaṇī mātā ahosi janetti. Tena kho pana, bhikkhave, samayena sobho nāma rājā ahosi. Sobhassa rañño sobhavatī nāma nagaraṃ rājadhānī ahosi. ‘‘Kassapassa, bhikkhave, bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa brahmadatto nāma brāhmaṇo pitā ahosi. Dhanavatī nāma brāhmaṇī mātā ahosi janetti. Tena kho pana, bhikkhave, samayena kikī nāma kiṃ kī nāma (syā.) rājā ahosi. Kikissa rañño bārāṇasī nāma nagaraṃ rājadhānī ahosi. kiṃ kī nāma (syā.) ‘‘Mayhaṃ, bhikkhave, etarahi suddhodano nāma rājā pitā ahosi. Māyā nāma devī mātā ahosi janetti. Kapilavatthu nāma nagaraṃ rājadhānī ahosī’’ti. Idamavoca bhagavā, idaṃ vatvāna sugato uṭṭhāyāsanā vihāraṃ pāvisi.
‘Monks, the father of the Lord Buddha Vipassi, the Worthy One, the Fully Enlightened One, was King Bandhuma. His mother was Queen Bandhumati. And his father’s royal capital was Bandhumati.

‘Monks, the father of the Lord Buddha Sikhi, the Worthy One, the Fully Enlightened One, was King Aruna. His mother was Queen Pabhavati. King Aruna’s capital was Arunavatl.

‘Monks, the father of the Lord Buddha Vessabhu, the Worthy One, the Fully Enlightened One, was King Suppatlta. His mother was Queen Yasavati; King Suppatlta’s capital was Anopama.

‘Monks, the father of the Lord Buddha Kakusandha, the Worthy One, the Fully Enlightened One, was the Brahmin Aggidatta. His mother was the Brahmin lady Visakha. The king at that time was called Khema; his capital was Khemavatl.

‘Monks, the father of the Lord Buddha Konagamana, the Worthy One, the Fully Enlightened One, was the Brahmin Yannadatta. His mother was the Brahmin lady Uttara. The king at that time was Sobha; his capital was Sobhavat.

‘Monks, the father of the Lord Buddha Kassapa, the Worthy One, the Fully Enlightened One, was the Brahmin Brahmadatta. His mother was the Brahmin lady Dhanavati. The king at that time was Kiki; his capital was Varanasi.

‘And now, monks, my father was King Suddhodana, my mother was Queen Maya.
The royal capital was a city named Kapilavatthu.

The Blessed One said this; having said this, the Well-Gone One rose from his seat and entered his dwelling.
12. Pitiparicchedo uttānatthoyeva. Vihāraṃ pāvisīti kasmā vihāraṃ pāvisi? Bhagavā kira ettakaṃ kathetvā cintesi – ‘‘na tāva mayā sattannaṃ buddhānaṃ vaṃso nirantaraṃ matthakaṃ pāpetvā kathito, ajja mayi pana vihāraṃ paviṭṭhe ime bhikkhū bhiyyoso mattāya pubbenivāsañāṇaṃ ārabbha vaṇṇaṃ kathayissanti. Athāhaṃ āgantvā nirantaraṃ buddhavaṃsaṃ kathetvā matthakaṃ pāpetvā dassāmī’’ti bhikkhūnaṃ kathāvārassa okāsaṃ datvā uṭṭhāyāsanā vihāraṃ pāvisi. Vihāraṃ pāvisī Yañcetaṃ bhagavā tantiṃ kathesi, tattha kappaparicchedo, jātiparicchedo, gottaparicchedo, āyuparicchedo, bodhiparicchedo, sāvakayugaparicchedo, sāvakasannipātaparicchedo, upaṭṭhākaparicchedo, pitiparicchedoti navime vārā āgatā, sambahulavāro anāgato, ānetvā pana dīpetabbo. Sambahulavārakathāvaṇṇanā Sabbabodhisattānañhi ekasmiṃ kulavaṃsānurūpe putte jāte nikkhamitvā pabbajitabbanti ayameva vaṃso, ayaṃ paveṇī. Kasmā? Sabbaññubodhisattānañhi mātukucchiṃ okkamanato paṭṭhāya pubbe vuttappakārāni anekāni pāṭihāriyāni honti, tatra nesaṃ yadi neva jātanagaraṃ, na pitā, na mātā, na bhariyā, na putto paññāyeyya, ‘‘imassa neva jātanagaraṃ, na pitā, na bhariyā, na putto paññāyati, devo vā sakko vā māro vā brahmā vā esa maññe, devānañca īdisaṃ pāṭihāriyaṃ anacchariya’’nti maññamāno jano neva sotabbaṃ, na saddhātabbaṃ maññeyya. Tato abhisamayo na bhaveyya, abhisamaye asati niratthakova buddhuppādo, aniyyānikaṃ sāsanaṃ hoti. Tasmā sabbabodhisattānaṃ – ‘‘ekasmiṃ kulavaṃsānurūpe putte jāte nikkhamitvā pabbajitabba’’nti ayameva vaṃso ayaṃ paveṇī. Tasmā puttādīnaṃ vasena sambahulavāro ānetvā dīpetabbo. Sambahulaparicchedavaṇṇanā Tattha – Samavattakkhandho atulo, suppabuddho ca uttaro; Satthavāho vijitaseno, rāhulo bhavati sattamoti. Ete tāva sattannampi bodhisattānaṃ anukkameneva satta puttā veditabbā. Tattha rāhulabhadde tāva jāte paṇṇaṃ āharitvā mahāpurisassa hatthe ṭhapayiṃsu. Athassa tāvadeva sakalasarīraṃ khobhetvā puttasineho aṭṭhāsi. So cintesi – ‘‘ekasmiṃ tāva jāte evarūpo puttasineho, parosahassaṃ kira me puttā bhavissanti, tesu ekekasmiṃ jāte idaṃ sinehabandhanaṃ evaṃ vaḍḍhantaṃ dubbhejjaṃ bhavissati, rāhu jāto, bandhanaṃ jāta’’nti āha. Taṃ divasameva ca rajjaṃ pahāya nikkhanto. Esa nayo sabbesaṃ puttuppattiyanti. Ayaṃ puttaparicchedo. Sutanā sabbakāmā ca, sucittā atha rocinī; Rucaggatī sunandā ca, bimbā bhavati sattamāti. Etā tesaṃ sattannampi puttānaṃ mātaro ahesuṃ. Bimbādevī pana rāhulakumāre jāte rāhulamātāti paññāyittha. Ayaṃ bhariyaparicchedo. Vipassī kakusandhoti ime pana dve bodhisattā payuttaājaññarathamāruyha mahābhinikkhamanaṃ nikkhamiṃsu. Sikhī koṇāgamanoti ime dve hatthikkhandhavaragatā hutvā nikkhamiṃsu. Vessabhū suvaṇṇasivikāya nisīditvā nikkhami. Kassapo uparipāsāde mahātale nisinnova ānāpānacatutthajjhānaṃ nibbattetvā jhānā uṭṭhāya taṃ jhānaṃ pādakaṃ katvā – ‘‘pāsādo uggantvā bodhimaṇḍe otaratū’’ti adhiṭṭhāsi. Pāsādo ākāsena gantvā bodhimaṇḍe otari. Mahāpurisopi tato otaritvā bhūmiyaṃ ṭhatvā – ‘‘pāsādo yathāṭhāneyeva patiṭṭhātū’’ti cintesi. So yathāṭhāne patiṭṭhāsi. Mahāpurisopi satta divasāni padhānamanuyuñjitvā bodhipallaṅke nisīditvā sabbaññutaṃ paṭivijjhi. Amhākaṃ pana bodhisatto kaṇṭakaṃ assavaramāruyha nikkhantoti. Ayaṃ yānaparicchedo. Vipassissa pana bhagavato yojanappamāṇe padese vihāro patiṭṭhāsi, sikhissa tigāvute, vessabhussa aḍḍhayojane, kakusandhassa gāvute, koṇāgamanassa aḍḍhagāvute, kassapassa vīsatiusabhe. Amhākaṃ bhagavato pakatimānena soḷasakarīse, rājamānena aṭṭhakarīse padese vihāro patiṭṭhitoti. Ayaṃ vihāraparicchedo. Vipassissa pana bhagavato ekaratanāyāmā vidatthivitthārā aṭṭhaṅgulubbedhā suvaṇṇiṭṭhakā kāretvā cūḷaṃsena chādetvā vihāraṭṭhānaṃ kiṇiṃsu. Sikhissa suvaṇṇayaṭṭhiphālehi chādetvā kiṇiṃsu. Vessabhussa suvaṇṇahatthipādāni kāretvā tesaṃ cūḷaṃsena chādetvā kiṇiṃsu. Kakusandhassa vuttanayeneva suvaṇṇiṭṭhakāhi chādetvā kiṇiṃsu. Koṇāgamanassa vuttanayeneva suvaṇṇakacchapehi chādetvā kiṇiṃsu. Kassapassa suvaṇṇakaṭṭīhiyeva chādetvā kiṇiṃsu. Amhākaṃ bhagavato salakkhaṇānaṃ kahāpaṇānaṃ cūḷaṃsena chādetvā kiṇiṃsu. Ayaṃ vihārabhūmiggahaṇadhanaparicchedo. Tattha vipassissa bhagavato tathā bhūmiṃ kiṇitvā vihāraṃ katvā dinnupaṭṭhāko punabbasumitto nāma ahosi, sikhissa sirivaḍḍhano nāma, vessabhussa sotthiyo nāma, kakusandhassa accuto nāma, koṇāgamanassa uggo nāma, kassapassa sumano nāma, amhākaṃ bhagavato sudatto nāma. Sabbe cete gahapatimahāsālā seṭṭhino ahesunti. Ayaṃ upaṭṭhākaparicchedo nāma. punabbasumitto sirivaḍḍhano sotthiyo accuto uggo sumano sudatto Aparāni cattāri avijahitaṭṭhānāni nāma honti. Sabbabuddhānañhi bodhipallaṅko avijahito, ekasmiṃyeva ṭhāne hoti. Dhammacakkappavattanaṃ isipatane migadāye avijahitameva hoti. Devorohanakāle saṅkassanagaradvāre paṭhamapadagaṇṭhikā avijahitāva hoti. Jetavane gandhakuṭiyā cattāri mañcapādaṭṭhānāni avijahitāneva honti. Vihāro pana khuddakopi mahantopi hoti, vihāropi na vijahitoyeva, nagaraṃ pana vijahati. Yadā nagaraṃ pācīnato hoti, tadā vihāro pacchimato; yadā nagaraṃ dakkhiṇato, tadā vihāro uttarato. Yadā nagaraṃ pacchimato, tadā vihāro pācīnato; yadā nagaraṃ uttarato, tadā vihāro dakkhiṇato. Idāni pana nagaraṃ uttarato, vihāro dakkhiṇato. Sabbabuddhānañca āyuvemattaṃ, pamāṇavemattaṃ, kulavemattaṃ, padhānavemattaṃ, rasmivemattanti pañca vemattāni honti. Āyuvemattaṃ nāma keci dīghāyukā honti, keci appāyukā. Tathā hi dīpaṅkarassa vassasatasahassaṃ āyuppamāṇaṃ ahosi, amhākaṃ bhagavato vassasataṃ āyuppamāṇaṃ. Āyuvemattaṃ Pamāṇavemattaṃ nāma keci dīghā honti keci rassā. Tathā hi dīpaṅkaro asītihattho ahosi, sumano navutihattho, amhākaṃ bhagavā aṭṭhārasahattho. Pamāṇavemattaṃ Kulavemattaṃ nāma keci khattiyakule nibbattanti, keci brāhmaṇakule. Padhānavemattaṃ nāma kesañci padhānaṃ ittarakālameva hoti, yathā kassapassa bhagavato. Kesañci addhaniyaṃ, yathā amhākaṃ bhagavato. Kulavemattaṃ Padhānavemattaṃ Rasmivemattaṃ nāma maṅgalassa bhagavato sarīrarasmi dasasahassilokadhātuppamāṇā ahosi. Amhākaṃ bhagavato samantā byāmamattā . Tatra rasmivemattaṃ ajjhāsayappaṭibaddhaṃ, yo yattakaṃ icchati, tassa tattakaṃ sarīrappabhā pharati. Maṅgalassa pana niccampi dasasahassilokadhātuṃ pharatūti ajjhāsayo ahosi. Paṭividdhaguṇesu pana kassaci vemattaṃ nāma natthi. Rasmivemattaṃ Aparaṃ amhākaṃyeva bhagavato sahajātaparicchedañca nakkhattaparicchedañca dīpesuṃ. Sabbaññubodhisattena kira saddhiṃ rāhulamātā, ānandatthero, channo, kaṇṭako, nidhikumbho, mahābodhi, kāḷudāyīti imāni satta sahajātāni. Mahāpuriso ca uttarāsāḷhanakkhatteneva mātukucchiṃ okkami, mahābhinikkhamanaṃ nikkhami, dhammacakkaṃ pavattesi, yamakapāṭihāriyaṃ akāsi. Visākhānakkhattena jāto ca abhisambuddho ca parinibbuto ca. Māghanakkhattenassa sāvakasannipāto ca ahosi, āyusaṅkhārossajjanañca, assayujanakkhattena devorohananti ettakaṃ āharitvā dīpetabbaṃ. Ayaṃ sambahulaparicchedo nāma.
12. The explanation of pitipariccheda is simple.

Why did the Blessed One enter the monastery (vihāraṃ pāvisī)? The Blessed One, having spoken thus much, thought: "I have not yet brought the lineage (vaṃsa) of the seven Buddhas to its peak uninterruptedly. Now, if I enter the monastery, these monks will speak in praise of their knowledge of former dwellings ( pubbenivāsañāṇaṃ) even more excessively.

Therefore, I will come again and, having spoken continuously about the lineage of the Buddhas and brought it to its peak, I will show [it]." Giving the monks an opportunity for conversation, he rose from his seat and entered the monastery (vihāraṃ pāvisi).

The Blessed One spoke of the tanti, where the divisions of the aeon (kappaparicchedo), birth (jātiparicchedo), lineage (gottaparicchedo), lifespan (āyuparicchedo), enlightenment (bodhiparicchedo), pair of disciples (sāvakayugaparicchedo), assembly of disciples (sāvakasannipātaparicchedo), attendant (upaṭṭhākaparicchedo), and joy (pitiparicchedo) – these nine turns have come, and many more have not yet come, but should be brought and explained.

Explanation of the numerous turns:

Indeed, for all bodhisattvas, from the time of descending into their mother's womb, many miracles of the kind previously described occur. If, in their case, neither a birth city (jātanagaraṃ) nor a father (pitā) nor a mother (mātā) nor a wife (bhariyā) nor a son (putto) were to appear, [people] would think, "For this one, neither a birth city nor a father nor a wife nor a son appears. He is either a god (devo) or Śakra (sakko) or Māra or Brahmā, I suppose. Such a miracle of the gods is not astonishing." Thinking thus, people would not think it should be heard or believed.

Then there would be no clear understanding (abhisamaya). In the absence of clear understanding, the arising of a Buddha (buddhuppādo) is pointless, and the teaching (sāsanaṃ) is not a vehicle for deliverance (aniyyānikaṃ).

Therefore, for all bodhisattvas, "Having a son born in a family lineage in accordance with the family lineage, one should renounce and go forth" – this is the lineage, this is the tradition.

Therefore, by way of sons and so on, many turns should be brought and explained.

Explanation of the numerous divisions:

There:

Samavattakkhandha was matchless, Suppabuddha was enlightened and excellent;

Satthavāha, Vijitasena, Rāhula becomes the seventh.

These seven sons, in that order, are to be known as the sons of the seven Buddhas.

There, when Rāhulabhadra was born, they brought a leaf and placed it in the hand of the great man (mahāpurisa).

Then, love for his son arose, shaking his entire body.

He thought: "Such love for a son arises even for one son. I will have hundreds of thousands of sons. As each one is born, this bond of affection will grow and become difficult to break. Rāhu is born, a bond is born," he said.

On that very day, he renounced the kingdom and went forth.

This is the method for the birth of all sons.

This is the division of sons (puttaparicchedo).

Sutanā, and Sabbakāmā, Sucittā, then Rocinī;

Rucaggatī, Sunandā, and Bimbā becomes the seventh.

These were the mothers of those seven sons.

Bimbādevī, however, when Rāhula was born, was known as the mother of Rāhula (rāhulamātāti paññāyittha).

This is the division of wives (bhariyaparicchedo).

Vipassī and Kakusandha – these two bodhisattvas, having mounted a chariot yoked with thoroughbreds, went forth in the great renunciation (mahābhinikkhamanaṃ nikkhamiṃsu).

Sikhī and Koṇāgamana – these two, having gone on the best elephant shoulders, went forth.

Vessabhū, having sat in a golden palanquin (suvaṇṇasivikāya), went forth.
