Kassapa, having sat on the great floor of the upper palace (uparipāsāde mahātale), having produced the fourth jhāna of mindfulness of breathing (ānāpānacatutthajjhānaṃ), and having risen from the jhāna, making that jhāna the basis, he resolved: "May the palace rise up and descend at the bodhimaṇḍa."

The palace went through the sky and descended at the bodhimaṇḍa.

The great man (mahāpurisa), having descended from there and stood on the ground, thought: "May the palace remain in its original place (yathāṭhāneyeva)."

It remained in its original place (yathāṭhāne patiṭṭhāsi).

The great man (mahāpurisa), having practiced exertion for seven days, having sat on the bodhipallaṅka, attained omniscience (sabbaññutaṃ paṭivijjhi).
Moreover, our Bodhisattva departed, ascending the horse Kaṇṭaka.
This is the chapter on the vehicle.

For Vipassī Buddha, the monastery was established in a region measuring one yojana; for Sikhī [it was] three gāvutas; for Vessabhū [it was] half a yojana; for Kakusandha [it was] one gāvuta; for Koṇāgamana [it was] half a gāvuta; for Kassapa [it was] twenty usabhas.
For our Buddha, the monastery was established in a region measuring sixteen karīsas by the standard measure, [and] eight karīsas by the royal measure.
This is the chapter on the monastery.

For Vipassī Buddha, having made golden bricks of one ratana in length, [and] a vidatthi in width, [and] eight aṅgulus in thickness, they covered the monastery site with cūḷaṃsa.
For Sikhī, they covered [it] with golden yaṭṭhiphālas, and purchased [it].
For Vessabhū, having made golden elephant feet, they covered them with cūḷaṃsa, and purchased [it].
For Kakusandha, just as it was said, they covered [it] with golden bricks, and purchased [it].
For Koṇāgamana, just as it was said, they covered [it] with golden tortoises, and purchased [it].
For Kassapa, they covered [it] with golden planks, and purchased [it].
For our Buddha, they covered [it] with kahāpaṇas possessing marks, with cūḷaṃsa, and purchased [it].
This is the chapter on the acquisition of the land for the monastery with wealth.

There, for Vipassī Buddha, having thus purchased the land, made the monastery, the attendant who gave alms was named Punabbasumitto; for Sikhī [he was] named Sirivaḍḍhano, for Vessabhū [he was] named Sotthiyo; for Kakusandha [he was] named Accuto; for Koṇāgamana [he was] named Uggo; for Kassapa [he was] named Sumano; for our Buddha [he was] named Sudatto.
All these householders were great and wealthy merchants.
This is the chapter called the Attendant.

Punabbasumitto, Sirivaḍḍhano, Sotthiyo, Accuto, Uggo, Sumano, Sudatto, there are four other places that are unabandoned.
Indeed, the bodhipallaṅka of all Buddhas is unabandoned; [it] exists in one and the same place.
The turning of the wheel of the Dharma is unabandoned in the Deer Park at Ṛṣipatana.
At the time of the descent from the gods, the first foot-mark at the gate of Saṅkassa city is unabandoned.
In Jetavana, the four leg-post positions of the Perfumed Chamber are unabandoned.
The monastery, moreover, is sometimes small and sometimes large, but the monastery is not entirely unabandoned, but the city is abandoned.
When the city is to the east, then the monastery is to the west; when the city is to the south, then the monastery is to the north.
When the city is to the west, then the monastery is to the east; when the city is to the north, then the monastery is to the south.
Now, moreover, the city is to the north, [and] the monastery is to the south.

And for all Buddhas, there are five differences: difference in lifespan (āyuvematta), difference in stature (pramāṇavematta), difference in family (kulavematta), difference in exertion (pradhānavematta), [and] difference in radiance (rasmivematta).
The difference in lifespan (āyuvematta) means that some have long lifespans, [and] some have short lifespans.
Thus, for Dīpaṅkara [the] lifespan was one hundred thousand years; for our Buddha [the] lifespan is one hundred years.

Difference in lifespan (āyuvematta), difference in stature (pramāṇavematta) means that some are tall, [and] some are short.
Thus, Dīpaṅkara was eighty cubits tall, Sumana [was] ninety cubits tall, [and] our Buddha [was] eighteen cubits tall.

Difference in stature (pramāṇavematta), difference in family (kulavematta) means that some are born into Kṣatriya families, [and] some into Brāhmaṇa families.
Padhānavemattaṃ by name, the exertion [padhāna] of some is only for a short time, like that of the Blessed Kassapa.

The confidence of some is enduring, like that of our Blessed One.

Kulavemattaṃ

Padhānavemattaṃ Rasmivemattaṃ by name, the bodily radiance of the Blessed Maṅgala was the measure of ten thousand world-spheres.

The bodily radiance of our Blessed One is all around, the measure of a fathom.

There, rasmivemattaṃ is connected to intention; whatever amount one desires, the radiance of the body pervades to that extent.

The intention of Maṅgala, however, was that it always pervades ten thousand world-spheres.

Among those who have penetrated the qualities, however, there is no vemattaṃ by name for anyone.

Rasmivemattaṃ

Furthermore, they revealed the delimitation of co-nascent beings and the delimitation of constellations of our Blessed One.

It is said that together with the all-knowing Bodhisattva were Rāhula's mother, Ānanda Thera, Channa, Kaṇṭaka, Nidhikumbha, Mahābodhi, and Kāḷudāyī, these seven co-nascent beings.

And the Great Being descended into his mother's womb under the Uttarāsāḷha constellation, went forth in the Great Renunciation, set in motion the wheel of the Dharma, and performed the Twin Miracle.

He was born, fully awakened, and passed into parinirvāṇa under the Visākha constellation.

Under the Māgha constellation there was a gathering of his disciples, the relinquishing of the life-force, and under the Assayuja constellation, the descent from the divine realms; so much, having brought it forth, should be revealed.

This is called an abundant delimitation.
13
13. Atha kho tesaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ acirapakkantassa bhagavato ayamantarākathā udapādi – ‘‘acchariyaṃ, āvuso, abbhutaṃ, āvuso, tathāgatassa mahiddhikatā mahānubhāvatā. Yatra hi nāma tathāgato atīte buddhe parinibbute chinnapapañce chinnavaṭume pariyādinnavaṭṭe sabbadukkhavītivatte jātitopi anussarissati, nāmatopi anussarissati, gottatopi anussarissati, āyuppamāṇatopi anussarissati, sāvakayugatopi anussarissati, sāvakasannipātatopi anussarissati – ‘evaṃjaccā te bhagavanto ahesuṃ itipi, evaṃnāmā evaṃgottā evaṃsīlā evaṃdhammā evaṃpaññā evaṃvihārī evaṃvimuttā te bhagavanto ahesuṃ itipī’’’ti. ‘‘Kiṃ nu kho, āvuso, tathāgatasseva nu kho esā dhammadhātu suppaṭividdhā, yassā dhammadhātuyā suppaṭividdhattā tathāgato atīte buddhe parinibbute chinnapapañce chinnavaṭume pariyādinnavaṭṭe sabbadukkhavītivatte jātitopi anussarati, nāmatopi anussarati, gottatopi anussarati, āyuppamāṇatopi anussarati, sāvakayugatopi anussarati, sāvakasannipātatopi anussarati – ‘evaṃjaccā te bhagavanto ahesuṃ itipi, evaṃnāmā evaṃgottā evaṃsīlā evaṃdhammā evaṃpaññā evaṃvihārī evaṃvimuttā te bhagavanto ahesuṃ itipī’ti, udāhu devatā tathāgatassa etamatthaṃ ārocesuṃ, yena tathāgato atīte buddhe parinibbute chinnapapañce chinnavaṭume pariyādinnavaṭṭe sabbadukkhavītivatte jātitopi anussarati, nāmatopi anussarati, gottatopi anussarati, āyuppamāṇatopi anussarati, sāvakayugatopi anussarati, sāvakasannipātatopi anussarati – ‘evaṃjaccā te bhagavanto ahesuṃ itipi, evaṃnāmā evaṃgottā evaṃsīlā evaṃdhammā evaṃpaññā evaṃvihārī evaṃvimuttā te bhagavanto ahesuṃ itipī’’’ti. Ayañca hidaṃ tesaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ antarākathā vippakatā hoti.
13. Then, soon after the Lord (bhagavato) had left, this discussion arose among those monks: "It is marvelous, friends (āvuso), it is wonderful, friends (āvuso), the Tathāgata’s (tathāgatassa) great power (mahiddhikatā) and great ability (mahānubhāvatā).

Where indeed the Tathāgata (tathāgato), regarding past Buddhas (atīte buddhe) who have attained complete extinction (parinibbute), cut off proliferation (chinnapapañce), cut off the cycle (chinnavaṭume), ended the round (pariyādinnavaṭṭe), and overcome all suffering (sabbadukkhavītivatte), can recall their birth (jātitopi anussarissati), recall their name (nāmatopi anussarissati), recall their clan (gottatopi anussarissati), recall their lifespan (āyuppamāṇatopi anussarissati), recall their disciples (sāvakayugatopi anussarissati), recall their assemblies of disciples (sāvakasannipātatopi anussarissati) – ‘Those Blessed Ones (bhagavanto) were of such birth (evaṃjaccā te ahesuṃ itipi), such were their names (evaṃnāmā evaṃgottā), such was their virtue (evaṃsīlā), such was their Dharma (evaṃdhammā), such was their wisdom (evaṃpaññā), such was their conduct (evaṃvihārī), such was their liberation (evaṃvimuttā) te bhagavanto ahesuṃ itipī’’’ti.

“Well now, friends (āvuso), how did the Tathāgata (tathāgatasseva) come to thoroughly penetrate (suppaṭividdhā) this element of the Dhamma (dhammadhātu), by which thorough penetration of the element of the Dhamma (dhammadhātuyā suppaṭividdhattā) the Tathāgata (tathāgato), regarding past Buddhas (atīte buddhe) who have attained complete extinction (parinibbute), cut off proliferation (chinnapapañce), cut off the cycle (chinnavaṭume), ended the round (pariyādinnavaṭṭe), and overcome all suffering (sabbadukkhavītivatte), can recall their birth (jātitopi anussarati), recall their name (nāmatopi anussarati), recall their clan (gottatopi anussarati), recall their lifespan (āyuppamāṇatopi anussarati), recall their disciples (sāvakayugatopi anussarati), recall their assemblies of disciples (sāvakasannipātatopi anussarati) – ‘Those Blessed Ones (bhagavanto) were of such birth (evaṃjaccā te ahesuṃ itipi), such were their names (evaṃnāmā evaṃgottā), such was their virtue (evaṃsīlā), such was their Dharma (evaṃdhammā), such was their wisdom (evaṃpaññā), such was their conduct (evaṃvihārī), such was their liberation (evaṃvimuttā) te bhagavanto ahesuṃ itipī’ti, or did some deva (devatā) reveal (ārocesuṃ) this matter (etamatthaṃ) to the Tathāgata (tathāgatassa), so that the Tathāgata (tathāgato), regarding past Buddhas (atīte buddhe) who have attained complete extinction (parinibbute), cut off proliferation (chinnapapañce), cut off the cycle (chinnavaṭume), ended the round (pariyādinnavaṭṭe), and overcome all suffering (sabbadukkhavītivatte), can recall their birth (jātitopi anussarati), recall their name (nāmatopi anussarati), recall their clan (gottatopi anussarati), recall their lifespan (āyuppamāṇatopi anussarati), recall their disciples (sāvakayugatopi anussarati), recall their assemblies of disciples (sāvakasannipātatopi anussarati) – ‘Those Blessed Ones (bhagavanto) were of such birth (evaṃjaccā te ahesuṃ itipi), such were their names (evaṃnāmā evaṃgottā), such was their virtue (evaṃsīlā), such was their Dharma (evaṃdhammā), such was their wisdom (evaṃpaññā), such was their conduct (evaṃvihārī), such was their liberation (evaṃvimuttā) te bhagavanto ahesuṃ itipī’’’ti.

And this conversation (antarākathā) of those monks (bhikkhūnaṃ) was left unfinished (vippakatā hoti).
13. Idāni atha kho tesaṃ bhikkhūnantiādīsu te bhikkhū – ‘‘āvuso, pubbenivāsassa nāma ayaṃ gati, yadidaṃ cutito paṭṭhāya paṭisandhiārohanaṃ. Yaṃ pana idaṃ paṭisandhito paṭṭhāya pacchāmukhaṃ ñāṇaṃ pesetvā cuti gantabbaṃ, idaṃ atigarukaṃ. Ākāse padaṃ dassento viya bhagavā kathesī’’ti ativimhayajātā hutvā – ‘‘acchariyaṃ, āvuso,’’tiādīni vatvā puna aparampi kāraṇaṃ dassento – ‘‘yatra hi nāma tathāgato’’tiādimāhaṃsu . Tattha yatra hi nāmāti acchariyatthe nipāto, yo nāma tathāgatoti attho. Chinnapapañceti ettha papañcā nāma taṇhā māno diṭṭhīti ime tayo kilesā. Chinnavaṭumeti ettha vaṭumanti kusalākusalakammavaṭṭaṃ vuccati. Pariyādinnavaṭṭeti tasseva vevacanaṃ, pariyādinnasabbakammavaṭṭeti attho. Sabbadukkhavītivatteti sabbaṃ vipākavaṭṭasaṅkhātaṃ dukkhaṃ vītivatte . Anussarissatīti idaṃ yatrāti nipātavasena anāgatavacanaṃ, attho panettha atītavasena veditabbo. Bhagavā hi te buddhe anussari, na idāni anussarissati. Evaṃsīlāti maggasīlena phalasīlena lokiyalokuttarasīlena evaṃsīlā. Evaṃdhammāti ettha samādhipakkhā dhammā adhippetā, maggasamādhinā phalasamādhinā lokiyalokuttarasamādhinā, evaṃsamādhayoti attho. Evaṃpaññāti maggapaññādivaseneva evaṃpaññā. Evaṃvihārīti ettha pana heṭṭhā samādhipakkhānaṃ dhammānaṃ gahitattā vihāro gahitova puna kasmā gahitameva gaṇhātīti ce; na idaṃ gahitameva, idañhi nirodhasamāpattidīpanatthaṃ vuttaṃ. Tasmā evaṃ nirodhasamāpattivihārī te bhagavanto ahesunti evamettha attho daṭṭhabbo. Evaṃvimuttāti ettha vikkhambhanavimutti, tadaṅgavimutti, samucchedavimutti, paṭippassaddhivimutti, nissaraṇavimuttīti pañcavidhā vimutti. Tattha aṭṭha samāpattiyo sayaṃ vikkhambhitehi nīvaraṇādīhi vimuttattā vikkhambhanavimuttīti saṅkhyaṃ gacchanti. Aniccānupassanādikā sattānupassanā sayaṃ tassa tassa paccanīkaṅgavasena pariccattāhi niccasaññādīhi vimuttattā tadaṅgavimuttīti saṅkhyaṃ gacchanti. Cattāro ariyamaggā sayaṃ samucchinnehi kilesehi vimuttattā samucchedavimuttīti saṅkhyaṃ gacchanti. Cattāri sāmaññaphalāni maggānubhāvena kilesānaṃ paṭippassaddhante uppannattā paṭippassaddhivimuttīti saṅkhyaṃ gacchanti. Nibbānaṃ sabbakilesehi nissaṭattā apagatattā dūre ṭhitattā nissaraṇavimuttīti saṅkhyaṃ gacchati. Iti imāsaṃ pañcannaṃ vimuttīnaṃ vasena – ‘‘evaṃ vimuttā’’ti ettha attho daṭṭhabbo. Evaṃvimuttā vikkhambhanavimuttī tadaṅgavimuttī samucchedavimuttī paṭippassaddhivimuttī nissaraṇavimuttī
13. Now, regarding "then to those monks," etc., those monks [said]: "Venerable ones, this is the course of prior states of existence, namely, the ascent to rebirth from passing away.

But that which is backward-facing knowledge, sending forth from rebirth to passing away, is very difficult.

The Blessed One spoke as if showing a footprint in the sky," becoming greatly astonished, they said, "Amazing, venerable ones," etc., and again showing another reason, they said, "Where, indeed, is the Tathāgata," etc.

There, "where, indeed," is a particle expressing astonishment; the meaning is: who, indeed, is the Tathāgata.

"Having cut off proliferation ( papañca)": here, proliferation (papañca) means these three defilements: craving ( taṇhā), conceit ( māna), and wrong view (diṭṭhi).

"Having cut off the round (vaṭuma)": here, "round" means what is called the round of wholesome and unwholesome actions (kusalākusalakammavaṭṭaṃ).

"In whom the round is completely consumed (pariyādinnavaṭṭe)": this is a synonym for that; the meaning is: in whom all the round of actions is completely consumed.

"In whom all suffering is transcended (sabbadukkhavītivatte)": in whom all suffering, which is designated as the round of result (vipākavaṭṭasaṅkhātaṃ dukkhaṃ), is transcended.

"Will recollect (anussarissatī)": here, "where" is a word of the future by way of a particle, but the meaning here should be understood in terms of the past.

The Blessed One recollected those Buddhas; now he will not recollect.

"Thus virtuous (evaṃsīlā)": thus virtuous by way of the virtue of the path (maggasīlena), the virtue of the fruit (phalasīlena), worldly and supramundane virtue (lokiyalokuttarasīlena).

"Thus principled (evaṃdhammā)": here, qualities on the side of concentration (samādhi) are intended; thus principled by way of the concentration of the path (maggasamādhinā), the concentration of the fruit (phalasamādhinā), worldly and supramundane concentration (lokiyalokuttarasamādhinā); the meaning is thus concentrated.

"Thus wise (evaṃpaññā)": thus wise, just as by way of the wisdom of the path, etc.

"Thus abiding (evaṃvihārī)": here, since qualities on the side of concentration have been taken below, and "abiding" has already been taken, why again does he take what has already been taken? If you say so, [the answer is:] this is not taking what has already been taken; this, indeed, is said to illuminate the attainment of cessation (nirodhasamāpatti).

Therefore, those Blessed Ones were thus abiding in the attainment of cessation; thus the meaning here should be seen.

Thus liberated (evaṃvimuttā)": here, liberation by suppression (vikkhambhanavimutti), liberation by means of its parts (tadaṅgavimutti), liberation by eradication (samucchedavimutti), liberation by tranquility (paṭippassaddhivimutti), liberation by escape (nissaraṇavimuttī); thus there are five kinds of liberation (vimutti).

There, the eight attainments (samāpatti) are designated as "liberation by suppression" because of being liberated from the hindrances, etc., by way of suppressing [them] themselves.

The seven contemplations beginning with the contemplation of impermanence (anicca), are designated as "liberation by means of its parts" because of being liberated from the perception of permanence, etc., by way of abandoning each of their opposing factors.

The four noble paths (ariyamaggā) are designated as "liberation by eradication" because of being liberated from the defilements by way of eradicating [them] themselves.

The four fruits of asceticism (sāmaññaphalāni) are designated as "liberation by tranquility" because of arising in the tranquility of the defilements through the power of the path.

Nirvāṇa is designated as "liberation by escape" because of escaping from all defilements, because of going away, because of standing far off.

Thus, by way of these five liberations, the meaning here should be seen as "thus liberated."

Thus liberated, liberation by suppression ( vikkhambhanavimutti), liberation by means of its parts (tadaṅgavimutti), liberation by eradication (samucchedavimutti), liberation by tranquility (paṭippassaddhivimutti), liberation by escape (nissaraṇavimutti).
14
14. Atha kho bhagavā sāyanhasamayaṃ paṭisallānā vuṭṭhito yena karerimaṇḍalamāḷo tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā paññatte āsane nisīdi. Nisajja kho bhagavā bhikkhū āmantesi – ‘‘kāyanuttha, bhikkhave, etarahi kathāya sannisinnā; kā ca pana vo antarākathā vippakatā’’ti? Evaṃ vutte te bhikkhū bhagavantaṃ etadavocuṃ – ‘‘idha, bhante, amhākaṃ acirapakkantassa bhagavato ayaṃ antarākathā udapādi – ‘acchariyaṃ, āvuso, abbhutaṃ, āvuso, tathāgatassa mahiddhikatā mahānubhāvatā, yatra hi nāma tathāgato atīte buddhe parinibbute chinnapapañce chinnavaṭume pariyādinnavaṭṭe sabbadukkhavītivatte jātitopi anussarissati, nāmatopi anussarissati, gottatopi anussarissati, āyuppamāṇatopi anussarissati, sāvakayugatopi anussarissati, sāvakasannipātatopi anussarissati – ‘‘evaṃjaccā te bhagavanto ahesuṃ itipi , evaṃnāmā evaṃgottā evaṃsīlā evaṃdhammā evaṃpaññā evaṃvihārī evaṃvimuttā te bhagavanto ahesuṃ itipī’’ti. Kiṃ nu kho, āvuso, tathāgatasseva nu kho esā dhammadhātu suppaṭividdhā, yassā dhammadhātuyā suppaṭividdhattā tathāgato atīte buddhe parinibbute chinnapapañce chinnavaṭume pariyādinnavaṭṭe sabbadukkhavītivatte jātitopi anussarati, nāmatopi anussarati, gottatopi anussarati, āyuppamāṇatopi anussarati, sāvakayugatopi anussarati, sāvakasannipātatopi anussarati – ‘‘evaṃjaccā te bhagavanto ahesuṃ itipi, evaṃnāmā evaṃgottā evaṃsīlā evaṃdhammā evaṃpaññā evaṃvihārī evaṃvimuttā te bhagavanto ahesuṃ itipī’’ti. Udāhu devatā tathāgatassa etamatthaṃ ārocesuṃ, yena tathāgato atīte buddhe parinibbute chinnapapañce chinnavaṭume pariyādinnavaṭṭe sabbadukkhavītivatte jātitopi anussarati, nāmatopi anussarati, gottatopi anussarati, āyuppamāṇatopi anussarati, sāvakayugatopi anussarati, sāvakasannipātatopi anussarati – ‘evaṃjaccā te bhagavanto ahesuṃ itipi, evaṃnāmā evaṃgottā evaṃsīlā evaṃdhammā evaṃpaññā evaṃvihārī evaṃvimuttā te bhagavanto ahesuṃ itipī’ti? Ayaṃ kho no, bhante, antarākathā vippakatā, atha bhagavā anuppatto’’ti.
Then the Blessed One, emerging from his seclusion in the late afternoon (sāyanhasamayaṃ), went to the kareri-tree pavilion (karerimaṇḍalamāḷo) and, on arrival, sat down on a seat laid out (paññatte āsane nisīdi).

Having sat down, the Blessed One addressed the monks (bhikkhū): "Monks, what is the subject you are gathered here to discuss? And what was the discussion that was interrupted (antarākathā vippakatā)?"

Thus addressed, the monks said to the Blessed One: "Here, venerable sir (bhante), after the Blessed One had recently left, this discussion arose among us: 'It is wonderful (acchariyaṃ), friend (āvuso), it is marvelous (abbhutaṃ), friend, how powerful (mahiddhikatā) and majestic (mahānubhāvatā) is the Tathāgata, in that he remembers past Buddhas who have attained parinirvāṇa (parinibbute), who have cut off proliferation (chinnapapañce), cut off the cycle (chinnavaṭume), brought to an end the round (pariyādinnavaṭṭe), and overcome all suffering (sabbadukkhavītivatte), recalling even their birth (jātitopi anussarissati), their name (nāmatopi anussarissati), their clan (gottatopi anussarissati), their lifespan (āyuppamāṇatopi anussarissati), the disciples and assemblies (sāvakayugatopi anussarissati, sāvakasannipātatopi)—‘‘These Blessed Lords (te bhagavanto) were of such birth (evaṃjaccā), such was their name (evaṃnāmā), such their clan (evaṃgottā), such their virtue (evaṃsīlā), such their dharma (evaṃdhammā), such their wisdom (evaṃpaññā), such their dwelling (evaṃvihārī), such their liberation (evaṃvimuttā te bhagavanto ahesuṃ itipī’’ti).’”

"Friends, is it only the Tathāgata who has thoroughly penetrated this element of the dharma (dhammadhātu suppaṭividdhā), that by thoroughly penetrating this element of the dharma, the Tathāgata remembers past Buddhas who have attained parinirvāṇa (parinibbute), who have cut off proliferation (chinnapapañce), cut off the cycle (chinnavaṭume), brought to an end the round (pariyādinnavaṭṭe), and overcome all suffering (sabbadukkhavītivatte), recalling even their birth (jātitopi anussarati), their name (nāmatopi anussarati), their clan (gottatopi anussarati), their lifespan (āyuppamāṇatopi anussarati), the disciples and assemblies (sāvakayugatopi anussarati, sāvakasannipātatopi)—‘‘These Blessed Lords (te bhagavanto) were of such birth (evaṃjaccā), such was their name (evaṃnāmā), such their clan (evaṃgottā), such their virtue (evaṃsīlā), such their dharma (evaṃdhammā), such their wisdom (evaṃpaññā), such their dwelling (evaṃvihārī), such their liberation (evaṃvimuttā te bhagavanto ahesuṃ itipī’’ti)’—or did deities reveal this matter (etamatthaṃ ārocesuṃ) to the Tathāgata, so that the Tathāgata remembers past Buddhas who have attained parinirvāṇa (parinibbute), who have cut off proliferation (chinnapapañce), cut off the cycle (chinnavaṭume), brought to an end the round (pariyādinnavaṭṭe), and overcome all suffering (sabbadukkhavītivatte), recalling even their birth (jātitopi anussarati), their name (nāmatopi anussarati), their clan (gottatopi anussarati), their lifespan (āyuppamāṇatopi anussarati), the disciples and assemblies (sāvakayugatopi anussarati, sāvakasannipātatopi)—‘These Blessed Lords (te bhagavanto) were of such birth (evaṃjaccā), such was their name (evaṃnāmā), such their clan (evaṃgottā), such their virtue (evaṃsīlā), such their dharma (evaṃdhammā), such their wisdom (evaṃpaññā), such their dwelling (evaṃvihārī), such their liberation (evaṃvimuttā te bhagavanto ahesuṃ itipī’’ti)’?"

"This, venerable sir (bhante), was the discussion that was interrupted, and then the Blessed One arrived."
14.Paṭisallānā vuṭṭhitoti ekībhāvā vuṭṭhito.
14. paṭisallānā vuṭṭhito means having arisen from becoming one (ekībhāvā vuṭṭhito).
15
15. ‘‘Tathāgatassevesā, bhikkhave, dhammadhātu suppaṭividdhā, yassā dhammadhātuyā suppaṭividdhattā tathāgato atīte buddhe parinibbute chinnapapañce chinnavaṭume pariyādinnavaṭṭe sabbadukkhavītivatte jātitopi anussarati, nāmatopi anussarati, gottatopi anussarati, āyuppamāṇatopi anussarati, sāvakayugatopi anussarati, sāvakasannipātatopi anussarati – ‘evaṃjaccā te bhagavanto ahesuṃ itipi, evaṃnāmā evaṃgottā evaṃsīlā evaṃdhammā evaṃpaññā evaṃvihārī evaṃvimuttā te bhagavanto ahesuṃ itipī’ti. Devatāpi tathāgatassa etamatthaṃ ārocesuṃ, yena tathāgato atīte buddhe parinibbute chinnapapañce chinnavaṭume pariyādinnavaṭṭe sabbadukkhavītivatte jātitopi anussarati, nāmatopi anussarati, gottatopi anussarati, āyuppamāṇatopi anussarati, sāvakayugatopi anussarati, sāvakasannipātatopi anussarati – ‘evaṃjaccā te bhagavanto ahesuṃ itipi, evaṃnāmā evaṃgottā evaṃsīlā evaṃdhammā evaṃpaññā evaṃvihārī evaṃvimuttā te bhagavanto ahesuṃ itipī’ti. ‘‘Iccheyyātha no tumhe, bhikkhave, bhiyyosomattāya pubbenivāsapaṭisaṃyuttaṃ dhammiṃ kathaṃ sotu’’nti? ‘‘Etassa, bhagavā, kālo; etassa, sugata, kālo; yaṃ bhagavā bhiyyosomattāya pubbenivāsapaṭisaṃyuttaṃ dhammiṃ kathaṃ kareyya, bhagavato sutvā bhikkhū dhāressantī’’ti. ‘‘Tena hi, bhikkhave , suṇātha, sādhukaṃ manasi karotha, bhāsissāmī’’ti. ‘‘Evaṃ, bhante’’ti kho te bhikkhū bhagavato paccassosuṃ. Bhagavā etadavoca –
15. Monks, it is through the complete penetration of the Tathāgata into this element of the Dharma (dhammadhātu) that the Tathāgata remembers past Buddhas who have attained parinirvāṇa, who have cut off complexities (chinnapapañca), severed the cycle (chinnavaṭuma), ended the round of rebirth (pariyādinnavaṭṭa), and passed beyond all suffering (sabbadukkhavītivatta), recalling their birth (jāti), name (nāma), clan (gotta), lifespan (āyuppamāṇa), the pairs of disciples (sāvakayuga), and assemblies of disciples (sāvakasannipāta), thinking, 'Those Blessed Ones were of such a birth, such a name, such a clan, such morality (sīla), such Dharma (dhamma), such wisdom (paññā), such a way of life (vihārī), and such liberation (vimutta)'.

Even the deities (devatā) announced this matter to the Tathāgata, so that the Tathāgata remembers past Buddhas who have attained parinirvāṇa, who have cut off complexities (chinnapapañca), severed the cycle (chinnavaṭuma), ended the round of rebirth (pariyādinnavaṭṭa), and passed beyond all suffering (sabbadukkhavītivatta), recalling their birth (jāti), name (nāma), clan (gotta), lifespan (āyuppamāṇa), the pairs of disciples (sāvakayuga) and assemblies of disciples (sāvakasannipāta), thinking, 'Those Blessed Ones were of such a birth, such a name, such a clan, such morality (sīla), such Dharma (dhamma), such wisdom (paññā), such a way of life (vihārī), and such liberation (vimutta)'.

"Monks, would you like to hear a discourse (dhammiṃ kathaṃ) connected with the remembrance of past lives (pubbenivāsapaṭisaṃyuttaṃ) to a greater extent (bhiyyosomattāya)?"

"Lord (bhagavā), now is the time for that; Well-Gone One (sugata), now is the time for that; if the Lord (bhagavā) would give a discourse (dhammiṃ kathaṃ) connected with the remembrance of past lives (pubbenivāsapaṭisaṃyuttaṃ) to a greater extent (bhiyyosomattāya), the monks (bhikkhū) would listen to and remember it, Blessed One (bhagavato)."

"Then, monks, listen and pay close attention (sādhukaṃ manasi karotha), I will speak."

"Yes, venerable sir (evaṃ bhante)," those monks replied to the Blessed One (bhagavato).

The Blessed One (bhagavā) said this:
16
16. ‘‘Ito so, bhikkhave, ekanavutikappe yaṃ vipassī bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho loke udapādi. Vipassī, bhikkhave, bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho khattiyo jātiyā ahosi, khattiyakule udapādi. Vipassī, bhikkhave, bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho koṇḍañño gottena ahosi. Vipassissa, bhikkhave, bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa asītivassasahassāni āyuppamāṇaṃ ahosi. Vipassī, bhikkhave, bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho pāṭaliyā mūle abhisambuddho. Vipassissa, bhikkhave , bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa khaṇḍatissaṃ nāma sāvakayugaṃ ahosi aggaṃ bhaddayugaṃ. Vipassissa, bhikkhave, bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa tayo sāvakānaṃ sannipātā ahesuṃ. Eko sāvakānaṃ sannipāto ahosi aṭṭhasaṭṭhibhikkhusatasahassaṃ, eko sāvakānaṃ sannipāto ahosi bhikkhusatasahassaṃ, eko sāvakānaṃ sannipāto ahosi asītibhikkhusahassāni. Vipassissa, bhikkhave, bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa ime tayo sāvakānaṃ sannipātā ahesuṃ sabbesaṃyeva khīṇāsavānaṃ. Vipassissa, bhikkhave, bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa asoko nāma bhikkhu upaṭṭhāko ahosi aggupaṭṭhāko. Vipassissa, bhikkhave, bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa bandhumā nāma rājā pitā ahosi. Bandhumatī nāma devī mātā ahosi janetti. Bandhumassa rañño bandhumatī nāma nagaraṃ rājadhānī ahosi. Bodhisattadhammatā
‘Monks, ninety-one aeons ago the Lord, the arhat, the fully enlightened Buddha Vipassī arose in the world.

The Lord Buddha Vipassī was born of the kṣatriya race and arose in a kṣatriya family.

The Lord Buddha Vipassī was of the Koṇḍañña clan (gotra).

The life-span of the Lord Buddha Vipassī was eighty thousand years.

The Lord Buddha Vipassī gained his full enlightenment at the foot of a trumpet flower tree (pāṭaliyā mūle abhisambuddho).

The Lord Buddha Vipassī had the pair of noble disciples Khaṇḍa and Tissa as his chief and excellent followers.

The Lord Buddha Vipassī had three assemblies of disciples.

One assembly was of six million eight hundred thousand monks, one assembly was of a hundred thousand monks, and one assembly was of eighty thousand monks.

These three assemblies of the Lord Buddha Vipassī consisted entirely of those whose āsravas were extinguished (khīṇāsava).

The personal attendant of the Lord Buddha Vipassī was the monk Aśoka; he was his chief attendant.

The father of the Lord Buddha Vipassī was King Bandhuma.

His mother was Queen Bandhumatī.

The royal capital of King Bandhuma was named Bandhumatī.

The nature of a Bodhisattva.
16.‘‘Ito so, bhikkhave’’ti ko anusandhi? Idañhi suttaṃ – ‘‘tathāgatassevesā, bhikkhave, dhammadhātu suppaṭividdhā’’ti ca ‘‘devatāpi tathāgatassa etamatthaṃ ārocesu’’nti ca imehi dvīhi padehi ābaddhaṃ. Tattha devatārocanapadaṃ suttantapariyosāne devacārikakolāhalaṃ dassento vicāressati . Dhammadhātupadānusandhivasena pana ayaṃ desanā āraddhā. Tattha khattiyo jātiyātiādīni ekādasapadāni nidānakaṇḍe vuttanayeneva veditabbāni. Bodhisattadhammatāvaṇṇanā
16. "Where is that, bhikkhus?" What is the connection? For this sūtra—"Indeed, bhikkhus, the dharmadhātu of the Tathāgata is well-penetrated," and "Even the deities announced this matter to the Tathāgata"—is connected by these two terms.

There, the term "deity announcement" will be considered, showing the clamor of the deities' wandering at the end of the sūtra.

Moreover, this teaching is begun with the connection of the term dharmadhātu.

There, "a noble (khattiyo) by birth," etc., the eleven terms should be understood in the same way as explained in the section on origins (nidānakaṇḍa).

Explanation of the nature of the bodhisattva's Dharma.
17
17. ‘‘Atha kho, bhikkhave, vipassī bodhisatto tusitā kāyā cavitvā sato sampajāno mātukucchiṃ okkami. Ayamettha dhammatā.
And so, monks, the bodhisattva Vipaśśī, mindful and clearly aware, descended from the Tusita heaven into his mother’s womb. This is the natural law (ayameṭṭha dhammatā).
17.Athakho, bhikkhave, vipassī bodhisattotiādīsu pana vipassīti tassa nāmaṃ, tañca kho vividhe atthe passanakusalatāya laddhaṃ. Bodhisattoti paṇḍitasatto bujjhanakasatto. Bodhisaṅkhātesu vā catūsu maggesu satto āsatto laggamānasoti bodhisatto. Sato sampajānoti ettha satoti satiyeva. Sampajānoti ñāṇaṃ. Satiṃ sūpaṭṭhitaṃ katvā ñāṇena paricchinditvā mātukucchiṃ okkamīti attho. Okkamīti iminā cassa okkantabhāvo pāḷiyaṃ dassito, na okkamanakkamo. So pana yasmā aṭṭhakathaṃ ārūḷho, tasmā evaṃ veditabbo – Sabbabodhisattā hi samatiṃsa pāramiyo pūretvā, pañca mahāpariccāge pariccajitvā, ñātatthacariyalokatthacariyabuddhacariyānaṃ koṭiṃ patvā, vessantarasadise tatiye attabhāve ṭhatvā, satta mahādānāni datvā, sattakkhattuṃ pathaviṃ kampetvā, kālaṅkatvā, dutiyacittavāre tusitabhavane nibbattanti. Vipassī bodhisattopi tatheva katvā tusitapure nibbattitvā saṭṭhisatasahassādhikā sattapaññāsa vassakoṭiyo tattha aṭṭhāsi. Aññadā pana dīghāyukadevaloke nibbattā bodhisattā na yāvatāyukaṃ tiṭṭhanti. Kasmā? Tattha pāramīnaṃ duppūraṇīyattā. Te adhimuttikālakiriyaṃ katvā manussapatheyeva nibbattanti. Pāramīnaṃ pūrento pana yathā idāni ekena attabhāvena sabbaññutaṃ upanetuṃ sakkonti, evaṃ sabbaso pūritattā tadā vipassī bodhisatto tattha yāvatāyukaṃ aṭṭhāsi. Devatānaṃ pana – ‘‘manussānaṃ gaṇanāvasena idāni sattahi divasehi cuti bhavissatī’’ti pañca pubbanimittāni uppajjanti – mālā milāyanti, vatthāni kilissanti, kacchehi sedā muccanti, kāye dubbaṇṇiyaṃ okkamati, devo devāsane na saṇṭhāti. Tattha mālāti paṭisandhiggahaṇadivase piḷandhanamālā , tā kira saṭṭhisatasahassādhikā sattapaṇṇāsa vassakoṭiyo amilāyitvā tadā milāyanti. Vatthesupi eseva nayo. Ettakaṃ pana kālaṃ devānaṃ neva sītaṃ na uṇhaṃ hoti, tasmiṃ kāle sarīrā bindubinduvasena sedā muccanti. Ettakañca kālaṃ tesaṃ sarīre khaṇḍiccapāliccādivasena vivaṇṇatā na paññāyati, devadhītā soḷasavassuddesikā viya khāyanti, devaputtā vīsativassuddesikā viya khāyanti, maraṇakāle pana tesaṃ kilantarūpo attabhāvo hoti. Ettakañca tesaṃ kālaṃ devaloke ukkaṇṭhitā nāma natthi, maraṇakāle pana nissasanti vijambhanti, sake āsane nābhiramanti. pañca pubbanimittāni mālā Imāni pana pubbanimittāni yathā loke mahāpuññānaṃ rājarājamahāmattādīnaṃyeva ukkāpātabhūmicālacandaggāhādīni nimittāni paññāyanti, na sabbesaṃ; evaṃ mahesakkhadevatānaṃyeva paññāyanti, na sabbesaṃ. Yathā ca manussesu pubbanimittāni nakkhattapāṭhakādayova jānanti, na sabbe; evaṃ tānipi na sabbadevatā jānanti, paṇḍitā eva pana jānanti. Tattha ye mandena kusalakammena nibbattā devaputtā, te tesu uppannesu – ‘‘idāni ko jānāti, ‘kuhiṃ nibbattessāmā’ti’’ bhāyanti. Ye mahāpuññā, te ‘‘amhehi dinnaṃ dānaṃ, rakkhitaṃ sīlaṃ, bhāvitaṃ bhāvanaṃ āgamma upari devalokesu sampattiṃ anubhavissāmā’’ti na bhāyanti. Vipassī bodhisattopi tāni pubbanimittāni disvā ‘‘idāni anantare attabhāve buddho bhavissāmī’’ti na bhāyati. Athassa tesu nimittesu pātubhūtesu dasasahassacakkavāḷadevatā sannipatitvā – ‘‘mārisa, tumhehi dasa pāramiyo pūrentehi na sakkasampattiṃ, na mārasampattiṃ, na brahmasampattiṃ, na cakkavattisampattiṃ patthentehi pūritā, lokanittharaṇatthāya pana buddhattaṃ patthayamānehi pūritā. So vo, idāni kālo, mārisa, buddhattāya, samayo, mārisa, buddhattāyā’’ti yācanti. Atha mahāsatto tāsaṃ devatānaṃ paṭiññaṃ adatvāva kāladīpadesakulajanettiāyuparicchedavasena pañcamahāvilokanaṃ nāma vilokesi. Tattha ‘‘kālo nu kho, na kālo’’ti paṭhamaṃ kālaṃ vilokesi. Tattha vassasatasahassato uddhaṃ vaḍḍhitaāyukālo kālo nāma na hoti. Kasmā? Tadā hi sattānaṃ jātijarāmaraṇāni na paññāyanti, buddhānañca dhammadesanā nāma tilakkhaṇamuttā natthi. Te tesaṃ – ‘‘aniccaṃ dukkhamanattā’’ti kathentānaṃ – ‘‘kiṃ nāmetaṃ kathentī’’ti neva sotuṃ, na saddahituṃ maññanti, tato abhisamayo na hoti, tasmiṃ asati aniyyānikaṃ sāsanaṃ hoti. Tasmā so akālo. Vassasatato ūnaāyukālopi kālo na hoti. Kasmā? Tadā hi sattā ussannakilesā honti, ussannakilesānañca dinno ovādo ovādaṭṭhāne na tiṭṭhati, udake daṇḍarāji viya khippaṃ vigacchati. Tasmā sopi akālova. Vassasatasahassato paṭṭhāya heṭṭhā, vassasatato paṭṭhāya uddhaṃ āyukālo kālo nāma, tadā ca asītivassasahassāyukā manussā. Atha mahāsatto – ‘‘nibbattitabbakālo’’ti kālaṃ passi. Tato dīpaṃ vilokento saparivāre cattāro dīpe oloketvā – ‘‘tīsu dīpesu buddhā na nibbattanti, jambudīpeyeva nibbattantī’’ti dīpaṃ passi. Tato – ‘‘jambudīpo nāma mahā, dasayojanasahassaparimāṇo, katarasmiṃ nu kho padese buddhā nibbattantī’’ti desaṃ vilokento majjhimadesaṃ passi. Majjhimadeso nāma – ‘‘puratthimāya disāya gajaṅgalaṃ nāma nigamo’’tiādinā (mahāva. 259) nayena vinaye vuttova. So āyāmato tīṇi yojanasatāni, vitthārato aḍḍhateyyāni, parikkhepato navayojanasatānīti. Etasmiñhi padese buddhā paccekabuddhā aggasāvakā asīti mahāsāvakā cakkavattirājāno aññe ca mahesakkhā khattiyabrāhmaṇagahapatimahāsālā uppajjanti. Idañcettha bandhumatī nāma nagaraṃ, tattha mayā nibbattitabbanti niṭṭhaṃ agamāsi. Tato kulaṃ vilokento – ‘‘buddhā nāma lokasammate kule nibbattanti. Idāni ca khattiyakulaṃ lokasammataṃ, tattha nibbattissāmi, bandhumā nāma me rājā pitā bhavissatī’’ti kulaṃ passi. Tato mātaraṃ vilokento – ‘‘buddhamātā nāma lolā surādhuttā na hoti, kappasatasahassaṃ pūritapāramī, jātito paṭṭhāya akhaṇḍapañcasīlā hoti, ayañca bandhumatī nāma devī īdisā, ayaṃ me mātā bhavissati , ‘‘kittakaṃ panassā āyū’’ti āvajjanto ‘‘dasannaṃ māsānaṃ upari satta divasānī’’ti passi. Iti imaṃ pañcamahāvilokanaṃ viloketvā ‘‘kālo, me mārisā, buddhabhāvāyā’’ti devatānaṃ saṅgahaṃ karonto paṭiññaṃ datvā – ‘‘gacchatha, tumhe’’ti tā devatā uyyojetvā tusitadevatāhi parivuto tusitapure nandanavanaṃ pāvisi. Sabbadevalokesu hi nandanavanaṃ atthiyeva. Tatra naṃ devatā ito cuto sugatiṃ gacchāti pubbekatakusalakammokāsaṃ sārayamānā vicaranti. So evaṃ devatāhi kusalaṃ sārayamānāhi parivuto tattha vicarantoyeva cavi. Evaṃ cuto ca ‘cavāmī’ti jānāti, cuticittaṃ na jānāti. Paṭisandhiṃ gahetvāpi jānāti, paṭisandhicittameva na jānāti. ‘‘Imasmiṃ me ṭhāne paṭisandhiṃ gahitā’’ti evaṃ pana jānāti. Keci pana therā – ‘‘āvajjanapariyāyo nāma laddhuṃ vaṭṭati, dutiyatatiyacittavāre eva jānissatī’’ti vadanti. Tipiṭakamahāsīvatthero pana āha – ‘‘mahāsattānaṃ paṭisandhi na aññesaṃ paṭisandhisadisā, koṭippattaṃ pana tesaṃ satisampajaññaṃ. Yasmā pana teneva cittena taṃ cittaṃ ñātuṃ na sakkā, tasmā cuticittaṃ na jānāti. Cutikkhaṇepi ‘cavāmī’ti jānāti. Paṭisandhicittaṃ na jānāti. ‘Asukasmiṃ me ṭhāne paṭisandhi gahitā’ti jānāti, tasmiṃ kāle dasasahassilokadhātu kampatī’’ti. Evaṃ sato sampajāno mātukucchiṃ okkamanto pana ekūnavīsatiyā paṭisandhicittesu mettāpubbabhāgassa somanassasahagatañāṇasampayuttaasaṅkhārikakusalacittassa sadisamahāvipākacittena paṭisandhi gaṇhi. Mahāsīvatthero pana upekkhāsahagatenāti āha. Yathā ca amhākaṃ bhagavā, evaṃ sopi āsāḷhīpuṇṇamāyaṃ uttarāsāḷhanakkhatteneva paṭisandhiṃ aggahesi. Tadā kira pure puṇṇamāya sattamadivasato paṭṭhāya vigatasurāpānaṃ mālāgandhādivibhūtisampannaṃ nakkhattakīḷaṃ anubhavamānā bodhisattamātā sattame divase pāto uṭṭhāya gandhodakena nahāyitvā sabbālaṅkāravibhūsitā varabhojanaṃ bhuñjitvā uposathaṅgāni adhiṭṭhāya sirigabbhaṃ pavisitvā sirisayane nipannā niddaṃ okkamamānā idaṃ supinaṃ addasa – ‘‘cattāro kira naṃ mahārājāno sayaneneva saddhiṃ ukkhipitvā anotattadahaṃ netvā nahāpetvā dibbavatthaṃ nivāsetvā dibbagandhehi vilimpetvā dibbapupphāni piḷandhitvā, tato avidūre rajatapabbato, tassa anto kanakavimānaṃ atthi, tasmiṃ pācīnato sīsaṃ katvā nipajjāpesuṃ. Atha bodhisatto setavaravāraṇo hutvā tato avidūre eko suvaṇṇapabbato, tattha caritvā tato oruyha rajatapabbataṃ abhiruhitvā kanakavimānaṃ pavisitvā mātaraṃ padakkhiṇaṃ katvā dakkhiṇapassaṃ phāletvā kucchiṃ paviṭṭhasadiso ahosi’’. Atha pabuddhā devī taṃ supinaṃ rañño ārocesi. Rājā vibhātāya rattiyā catusaṭṭhimatte brāhmaṇapāmokkhe pakkosāpetvā haritūpalittāya lājādīhi katamaṅgalasakkārāya bhūmiyā mahārahāni āsanāni paññapetvā tattha nisinnānaṃ brāhmaṇānaṃ sappimadhusakkarābhisaṅkhatassa varapāyāsassa suvaṇṇarajatapātiyo pūretvā suvaṇṇarajatapātīheva paṭikujjitvā adāsi, aññehi ca ahatavatthakapilagāvīdānādīhi nesaṃ santappesi. Atha nesaṃ sabbakāmasantappitānaṃ taṃ supinaṃ ārocetvā – ‘‘kiṃ bhavissatī’’ti pucchi. Brāhmaṇā āhaṃsu – ‘‘mā cintayi, mahārāja, deviyā te kucchimhi gabbho patiṭṭhito, so ca kho purisagabbho na itthigabbho, putto te bhavissati. So sace agāraṃ ajjhāvasissati, rājā bhavissati cakkavattī. Sace agārā nikkhamma pabbajissati, buddho bhavissati loke vivaṭṭacchado’’ti. Ayaṃ tāva – ‘‘mātukucchiṃ okkamī’’ti ettha vaṇṇanākkamo. Ayametthadhammatāti ayaṃ ettha mātukucchiokkamane dhammatā, ayaṃ sabhāvo, ayaṃ niyāmoti vuttaṃ hoti. Niyāmo ca nāmesa kammaniyāmo, utuniyāmo, bījaniyāmo, cittaniyāmo, dhammaniyāmoti pañcavidho (dha. sa. aṭṭha. 498). Ayamettha dhammatā Niyāmo Tattha kusalassa iṭṭhavipākadānaṃ, akusalassa aniṭṭhavipākadānanti ayaṃ kammaniyāmo. Tassa dīpanatthaṃ – ‘‘na antalikkhe’’ti (khu. pā. 127) gāthāya vatthūni vattabbāni. Apica ekā kira itthī sāmikena saddhiṃ bhaṇḍitvā ubbandhitvā maritukāmā rajjupāse gīvaṃ pavesesi. Aññataro puriso vāsiṃ nisento taṃ itthikammaṃ disvā rajjuṃ chinditukāmo – ‘‘mā bhāyi, mā bhāyī’’ti taṃ samassāsento upadhāvi. Rajju āsīviso hutvā aṭṭhāsi. So bhīto palāyi. Itarā tattheva mari. Yaṃ panetaṃ sālibījato sāliphalameva, madhurato madhurasaṃyeva, tittato tittarasaṃyeva phalaṃ hoti, ayaṃ bījaniyāmo. bījaniyāmo. Purimā purimā cittacetasikā dhammā pacchimānaṃ pacchimānaṃ cittacetasikānaṃ dhammānaṃ upanissayapaccayena paccayoti evaṃ yadetaṃ cakkhuviññāṇādīnaṃ anantarā sampaṭicchanādīnaṃ nibbattanaṃ, ayaṃ cittaniyāmo. cittaniyāmo. Tasmā tamevatthaṃ dassento dhammatā esā bhikkhavetiādimāha. dhammaniyāmo dhammatā esā bhikkhave
17. Now, monks, in the passage beginning with "Vipassī Bodhisattva," vipassī is his name, which he obtained because he was skilled in seeing various meanings.

Bodhisattva means one who is wise and capable of awakening.

Alternatively, bodhisattva means one who is attached to or connected with the four paths known as bodhi.

In the phrase sato sampajāno, sato means mindfulness itself.

Sampajāno means knowledge.

It means establishing mindfulness well, and having ascertained with knowledge, he entered his mother's womb.

Okkamī ("he entered") indicates his entry in the Pāli text, not the manner of entry.

Since he is established in the Aṭṭhakathā, thus it should be understood:

All bodhisattvas, having perfected the thirty perfections, having relinquished the five great donations, having attained the peak of benefiting relatives, benefiting the world, and acting as a Buddha, having remained in the third existence similar to Vessantara, having given the seven great gifts, having caused the earth to tremble seven times, having passed away, are reborn in the Tusita heaven in the second thought-moment.

The Bodhisattva Vipassī also, having done likewise, having been reborn in the Tusita city, remained there for fifty-seven vassakoṭis, exceeding sixty hundred thousand.

However, at other times, bodhisattvas reborn in the long-lived deva-worlds do not remain for their entire lifespan.

Why? Because the perfections are difficult to fulfill there.

Having performed the act of death motivated by aspiration, they are reborn in the human realm itself.

However, while fulfilling the perfections, just as now they are able to lead omniscience with one existence, so Vipassī Bodhisattva, having been completely fulfilled in every way at that time, remained there for his entire lifespan.

However, for deities, the five precursory signs arise when a displacement will occur in seven days due to the calculation of humans: the garlands wither, the garments become soiled, sweat exudes from the armpits, ugliness enters the body, and the deva does not stand firm on the deva-seat.

There, mālā means the garlands worn on the day of conception and rebirth, those that do not wither for fifty-seven vassakoṭis, exceeding sixty hundred thousand, wither at that time.

The same rule applies to garments as well.

For such a long time, there is neither cold nor heat for the devas; at that time, sweat exudes from their bodies in a stream of drops.

And for such a long time, discoloration due to decay, wrinkling, etc., is not apparent in their bodies; the deva-daughters appear like sixteen-year-olds, the deva-sons appear like twenty-year-olds; however, at the time of death, their existence becomes one of weariness.

And for such a long time, there is no weariness in the deva-world for them; however, at the time of death, they sigh, they yawn, they do not delight in their own seat.

The five precursory signs:

These precursory signs, just as in the world, signs such as falling meteors, earthquakes, eclipses of the moon, etc., are apparent only to those of great merit, to kings, great ministers, etc., not to all; likewise, they are apparent only to devas of great power, not to all.

And just as among humans, only astrologers, etc., know the precursory signs, not all; likewise, not all devas know those signs, only the wise ones know them.

There, those deva-sons who are reborn with weak wholesome actions, they, when those signs have arisen, fear, "Now who knows where we will be reborn?"

Those of great merit do not fear, "Having relied on the alms given by us, the morality protected, the cultivation cultivated, we will experience prosperity in the upper deva-worlds."

The Bodhisattva Vipassī also, having seen those precursory signs, does not fear, "Now in the next existence, I will become a Buddha."

Then, when those signs have become manifest to him, ten thousand cakkavāḷa deities, having assembled, implore, "O noble one, the ten perfections have been fulfilled by you, not desiring the prosperity of Śakra, not the prosperity of Māra, not the prosperity of Brahmā, not the prosperity of a cakkavatti, but desiring Buddhahood for the sake of delivering the world, they have been fulfilled.

Now is the time for you, noble one, for Buddhahood, the occasion, noble one, for Buddhahood."

Then, without giving a promise to those deities, the Great Being, based on the division of time, island, country, family, mother, and lifespan, contemplated what is called the Five Great Contemplations.

There, first he contemplated the time, "Is it the time or not?"

There, a time when the lifespan is extended upwards from a hundred thousand years is not called the time.

Why? Because at that time, birth, old age, and death of beings are not apparent, and indeed, there is no teaching of the Dhamma of the Buddhas that is devoid of the three characteristics.
When they speak of "impermanence, suffering, and non-self," people neither listen nor believe, saying, "What is this they talk of?" At such a time there can be no understanding, and without that, the teaching will not lead to salvation.
That therefore is not the time.
Neither is it the right time when the span of life is under one hundred years.
Why? Because then beings are rife with defilements, and admonition given to those with rampant defilements does not endure in its place, but like a streak drawn on water vanishes quickly away.
That, therefore, is also not the time.
When, however, the span of life is under a hundred thousand years and over a hundred years, that is the proper time, and then humans live for eighty thousand years.
Then the Great Being saw that the time of his advent had arrived.

Then, reflecting upon the continent, and considering the four great continents with their surrounding islands, he thought: “In three of the continents the Buddhas are not born, but in Jambudvīpa they are born," and thus he decided on the continent.

Then thinking, " Jambudvīpa is large, ten thousand yojanas in extent; now in which district of it do the Buddhas appear?" he fixed upon the Middle Country (madhyamadeśa).
The Middle Country is—as is stated in the Vinaya according to the method beginning, “To the east is the market town called Gajaṅgala” (Mahāva.
259).
It is three hundred yojanas in length, two hundred and fifty in breadth, and nine hundred yojanas in circumference.
For in this district appear Buddhas, pratyekabuddhas, chief disciples, the eighty great disciples, wheel-turning monarchs (cakravartirājā), and other powerful nobles, kṣatriyas, brāhmaṇas, householders (gṛhapatis), and great families.
And there, in that country, the town named Bandhumatī was the place where I should take birth.

Then, reflecting on the family, he thought: "Buddhas are born in a family held in esteem by the world.
And now the kṣatriya family is held in esteem by the world; there I shall take birth; Bandhumā will be my father, the king."

Then, reflecting on the mother, he thought: “The mother of a Buddha is not lustful or corrupt as to drink, but has fulfilled the perfections (pāramī) for a hundred thousand kalpas, and from her birth upwards has kept the five precepts unbroken. This Bandhumatī, named Devī, is of this sort; she will be my mother," and reflecting, “How long will be her life?” he saw, "Ten months and seven days."

Thus, having reflected on these five great observations, consenting to the deities, “The time has arrived, sirs, for me to become a Buddha,” and giving his promise: “Go now,” and dismissing those deities, surrounded by the deities of the Tuṣita heaven, he entered the Nandanavana garden in the Tuṣita heaven.
For the Nandanavana garden exists in all the heavens.
There the deities, reminding him of the opportunities gained by good deeds done in a former birth, say to him: “Departing hence, go to a good destiny."
Thus he also, surrounded by deities reminding him of his acquired merit, departing from there, was conceived.

Thus, when departing, he knows, ‘I am departing’; he does not know the consciousness of the moment of death (cuticitta).
Having grasped rebirth (pratisaṃdhi), he also knows, he does not know only the consciousness of the moment of rebirth (pratisaṃdhicitta).
He knows, “I have grasped rebirth (pratisaṃdhi) in this place.”
Some elders say: “The turn of adverting (āvajjanapariyāyo) is possible to attain, he will know only at the second or third moment of consciousness (cittavāre).”
The Tipiṭaka Mahāsīva Thera said: "The rebirth (pratisaṃdhi) of great beings (mahāsattvas) is not like the rebirth (pratisaṃdhi) of others, their mindfulness and clear comprehension (satisampajañña) is attained to its peak.
Since it is not possible to know that consciousness (citta) with that same consciousness (citta), therefore he does not know the consciousness of the moment of death (cuticitta).
Even at the moment of death (cutikkhaṇe) he knows, ‘I am dying’.
He does not know the consciousness of the moment of rebirth (pratisaṃdhicitta).
He knows, ‘I have grasped rebirth (pratisaṃdhi) in such and such a place’; at that time, the ten-thousand world-system shakes.”
Thus, mindful and clearly comprehending, while descending into the mother's womb (mātukucchiṃ okkamanto), with nineteen rebirth consciousnesses (paṭisandhicittesu), with the consciousness (citta) similar to the wholesome consciousness (kusalacitta) of the unprompted kind (asaṅkhārika) associated with knowledge (ñāṇa) accompanied by joy (somanassa) of the preliminary stage of loving-kindness (mettāpubbabhāgassa), he grasped rebirth (pratisaṃdhi) with the similar great resultant consciousness (sadisamahāvipākacitta).
The venerable Mahāsīva, however, said that it was accompanied by equanimity.

Just as our Lord, in this way, he also took rebirth on the Āsāḷhī full moon, under the Uttarāsāḷha constellation.

At that time, it is said that in the past, starting from the seventh day of the full moon, the mother of the bodhisattva, experiencing a festival game endowed with the magnificence of garlands, perfumes, and so on, free from the drinking of liquor, on the seventh day, rising early in the morning, bathing with scented water, adorned with all ornaments, having eaten excellent food, having undertaken the Uposatha vows, entering the Siri chamber, lying down on the Siri bed, as she was falling asleep, she saw this dream: "Four great kings, it is said, lifting [her] up together with the bed, taking [her] to the Anotatta lake, bathing [her], dressing [her] in divine clothes, anointing [her] with divine perfumes, adorning [her] with divine flowers, then, not far away, there is a silver mountain, inside it there is a golden mansion, in that they laid [her] down, having made [her] head face eastward.

Then the bodhisattva, having become a superb white elephant, not far from there, there is a golden mountain, having wandered there, then descending from there, ascending the silver mountain, entering the golden mansion, circumambulating his mother, splitting [her] right side, he became as if he had entered [her] womb."

Then, awakened, the goddess announced that dream to the king.

The king, in the dawning night, having summoned sixty-four eminent brahmins, having prepared magnificent seats on a ground adorned with auspicious offerings made with green turmeric and parched grain, and so on, having filled golden and silver bowls with excellent rice gruel mixed with ghee, honey, and sugar for the brahmins seated there, he gave [it to them], overturning [the bowls] with the golden and silver bowls themselves, and he satisfied them with other gifts of untouched cloths, tawny cows, and so on.

Then, having announced that dream to them, having satisfied all their desires, he asked, "What will happen?"

The brahmins said, "Do not worry, great king, a fetus is established in your queen's womb, and it is a male fetus, not a female fetus, you will have a son.

If he lives in a house, he will become a king, a universal monarch.

If, leaving his home, he goes forth, he will become a Buddha, with the covering of ignorance removed in the world."

This is, firstly, the manner of description of "he entered his mother's womb."

Here, "dhammatā" means this is the nature in the entry into the mother's womb, this is the inherent nature, this is the fixed order," it is said.

Fixed order is of five kinds, namely, the fixed order of karma, the fixed order of seasons, the fixed order of seeds, the fixed order of mind, the fixed order of dharma (Dha.

Sa.

Aṭṭha.
498).

Here, the "dhammatā" , "niyāmo"

There, the giving of desired results to wholesome actions, the giving of undesired results to unwholesome actions—this is the fixed order of karma.

To illuminate that, the topics of the gāthā "not in the sky" (Khu.

Pā.
127) should be stated.

Moreover, it is said that a certain woman, having quarreled with her husband, desiring to die by hanging herself, placed her neck in a noose of rope.

Another man, sharpening an axe, seeing that woman's action, desiring to cut the rope—"Do not fear, do not fear!"—comforting her, ran up.

The rope became a venomous snake and remained there.

He, frightened, fled.

The other one died right there.

Such topics should be shown here.

Fixed order of karma.

In those various countries, at those various times, the trees bearing flowers and fruits and so on with a single strike, the blowing and not blowing of the wind, the sharpness and mildness of the heat, the raining and not raining of the god, the lotuses blooming during the day and closing at night—all this is the fixed order of seasons.

Fixed order of seasons.That which, from a rice seed, comes forth as rice fruit, from sweetness comes forth as sweet taste, from bitterness comes forth as bitter taste, that is called the bījaniyāmaḥ (seed-order/law of the seed).

Bījaniyāmaḥ (seed-order/law of the seed).

The preceding mental states and mental factors are a condition by way of dependence for the succeeding mental states and mental factors; thus, the arising of visual consciousness (cakkhuviññāṇa) etc., immediately after, the reception (sampaṭicchana) etc., that is called cittaniyāmaḥ (mind-order/law of mind).

Cittaniyāmaḥ (mind-order/law of mind).

That which is the manifestation of the entry into the mother's womb (mātukucchiokkamanādi) by Bodhisattvas, the quaking of the ten-thousand world-systems (dasasahassilokadhātukampanādi) etc., that is called dhammaniyāmaḥ (dharma-order/law of dharma).

Among these, here, dhammaniyāmaḥ is intended.

Therefore, showing that very meaning, he said "This is the nature of things, dhammatā esā bhikkhave" etc.

Dhammaniyāmaḥ (dharma-order/law of dharma), dhammatā esā bhikkhave (This is the nature of things, monks).
18
18. ‘‘Dhammatā, esā, bhikkhave, yadā bodhisatto tusitā kāyā cavitvā mātukucchiṃ okkamati. Atha sadevake loke samārake sabrahmake sassamaṇabrāhmaṇiyā pajāya sadevamanussāya appamāṇo uḷāro obhāso pātubhavati atikkammeva devānaṃ devānubhāvaṃ. Yāpi tā lokantarikā aghā asaṃvutā andhakārā andhakāratimisā , yattha pime candimasūriyā evaṃmahiddhikā evaṃmahānubhāvā ābhāya nānubhonti, tatthapi appamāṇo uḷāro obhāso pātubhavati atikkammeva devānaṃ devānubhāvaṃ. Yepi tattha sattā upapannā, tepi tenobhāsena aññamaññaṃ sañjānanti – ‘aññepi kira, bho, santi sattā idhūpapannā’ti. Ayañca dasasahassī lokadhātu saṅkampati sampakampati sampavedhati. Appamāṇo ca uḷāro obhāso loke pātubhavati atikkammeva devānaṃ devānubhāvaṃ. Ayamettha dhammatā.
18. “This is the rule, monks, that when a bodhisattva descends from the Tuṣita heaven, departs from that realm, and enters his mother’s womb (mātukucchiṃ okkamati), there appears in this world with its devas, maras, and Brahmās, its ascetics and Brāhmaṇas, princes and people, an immeasurable, splendid light (appamāṇo uḷāro obhāso pātubhavati) surpassing the glory of the most powerful devas (devānaṃ devānubhāvaṃ).

And whatever abysmal world interspaces (lokantarikā) of vacancy (aghā), gloom (asaṃvutā andhakārā), and utter darkness (andhakāratimisā), where the moon and the sun (candimasūriyā), mighty (evaṃmahiddhikā) and powerful (evaṃmahānubhāvā) as they are, cannot make their light prevail (ābhāya nānubhonti) - there too an immeasurable and glorious radiance (appamāṇo uḷāro obhāso pātubhavati) appears, surpassing the splendor of the gods (devānaṃ devānubhāvaṃ).

And the beings (sattā) born there perceive each other by that light: “So other beings (sattā), indeed, have appeared here (idhūpapannā).”

And this ten-thousandfold world-system (dasasahassī lokadhātu) trembles (saṅkampati), quakes (sampakampati), and is convulsed (sampavedhati).

An immeasurable and glorious radiance (appamāṇo uḷāro obhāso) appears in the world, surpassing the splendor of the gods (devānaṃ devānubhāvaṃ).

This is the natural law (ayamettha dhammatā).
18. Tattha kucchiṃ okkamatīti ettha kucchiṃ okkanto hotīti ayamevattho . Okkante hi tasmiṃ evaṃ hoti, na okkamamāne. Appamāṇoti vuḍḍhippamāṇo, vipuloti attho. Uḷāroti tasseva vevacanaṃ. Uḷārāni uḷārāni khādanīyāni khādantītiādīsu (ma. ni. 1.399) hi madhuraṃ uḷāranti vuttaṃ. Uḷārāya khalu bhavaṃ vacchāyano samaṇaṃ gotamaṃ pasaṃsāya pasaṃsatītiādīsu (ma. ni. 1.288) seṭṭhaṃ uḷāranti vuttaṃ. Idha pana vipulaṃ adhippetaṃ. Devānaṃ devānubhāvanti ettha devānaṃ ayamānubhāvo nivatthavatthassa pabhā dvādasayojanāni pharati, tathā sarīrassa, tathā alaṅkārassa, tathā vimānassa, taṃ atikkamitvāti attho. Lokantarikāti tiṇṇaṃ tiṇṇaṃ cakkavāḷānaṃ antarā ekeko lokantariko hoti, tiṇṇaṃ sakaṭacakkānaṃ vā tiṇṇaṃ pattānaṃ vā aññamaññaṃ āhacca ṭhapitānaṃ majjhe okāso viya. So pana lokantarikanirayo parimāṇato aṭṭhayojanasahasso hoti. Aghāti niccavivaṭā. Asaṃvutāti heṭṭhāpi appatiṭṭhā. Andhakārāti tamabhūtā. Andhakāratimisāti cakkhuviññāṇuppattinivāraṇato andhabhāvakaraṇatimisena samannāgatā. Tattha kira cakkhuviññāṇaṃ na jāyati. Evaṃmahiddhikāti candimasūriyā kira ekappahāreneva tīsu dīpesu paññāyanti, evaṃ mahiddhikā. Ekekāya disāya nava nava yojanasatasahassāni andhakāraṃ vidhamitvā ālokaṃ dassenti, evaṃmahānubhāvā. Ābhāya nānubhontīti attano pabhāya nappahonti. Te kira cakkavāḷapabbatassa vemajjhena vicaranti, cakkavāḷapabbatañca atikkamma lokantarikanirayā. Tasmā te tattha ābhāya nappahonti. Lokantarikā Aghā Asaṃvutā Andhakārā Andhakāratimisā Evaṃmahiddhikā evaṃmahānubhāvā. Ābhāya nānubhontī Yepi tattha sattāti yepi tasmiṃ lokantarikamahāniraye sattā uppannā. Kiṃ pana kammaṃ katvā tattha uppajjantīti. Bhāriyaṃ dāruṇaṃ mātāpitūnaṃ dhammikasamaṇabrāhmaṇānañca upari aparādhaṃ, aññañca divase divase pāṇavadhādisāhasikakammaṃ katvā uppajjanti, tambapaṇṇidīpe abhayacoranāgacorādayo viya. Tesaṃ attabhāvo tigāvutiko hoti, vaggulīnaṃ viya dīghanakhā honti. Te rukkhe vagguliyo viya nakhehi cakkavāḷapabbate lagganti. Yadā saṃsappantā aññamaññassa hatthapāsaṃ gatā honti, atha ‘‘bhakkho no laddho’’ti maññamānā tattha vāvaṭā viparivattitvā lokasandhārakaudake patanti, vāte paharantepi madhukaphalāni viya chijjitvā udake patanti, patitamattāva accantakhāre udake piṭṭhapiṇḍi viya vilīyanti. Yepi tattha sattā Aññepi kira bho santi sattāti bho yathā mayaṃ mahādukkhaṃ anubhavāma, evaṃ aññe kira sattāpi imaṃ dukkhamanubhavanatthāya idhūpapannāti taṃ divasaṃ passanti. Ayaṃ pana obhāso ekayāgupānamattampi na tiṭṭhati, accharāsaṅghāṭamattameva vijjobhāso viya niccharitvā – ‘‘kiṃ ida’’nti bhaṇantānaṃyeva antaradhāyati. Saṅkampatīti samantato kampati. Itaradvayaṃ purimapadasseva vevacanaṃ. Puna appamāṇo cātiādi nigamanatthaṃ vuttaṃ. Aññepi kira bho santi sattā Saṅkampatī appamāṇo cā
18. Namely, “kucchiṃ okkamatī”: here, the meaning is “one has entered the womb.”

When [one] has entered, it is like that; it is not like that when [one] is entering.

“Appamāṇo”: the measure of growth; the meaning is vipulo, vast.

"Uḷāro": [this is] a synonym of that.

In such statements as “they consume excellent hard food, eat excellent soft food” (Mahāvagga, Nissaggiya 1.399), “excellent” means madhuraṃ, sweet.

In such statements as “Master Vacchāyana praises the ascetic Gotama with high praise” (Majjhima Nikāya 1.288), “excellent” means seṭṭhaṃ, the best.

Here, however, vipulaṃ, vast, is intended.

"Devānaṃ devānubhāva": here, this is the power of the gods: the radiance of the garments they are wearing extends twelve yojanas, as does that of their bodies, their ornaments and their mansions. The meaning is that it surpasses that.

Lokantarikā: Between three cakkavāḷas, there is one lokantarika; like the space in the middle of three cartwheels or three bowls placed touching each other.

That hell of the lokantarika is eight thousand yojanas in extent.

Aghā: always open.

Asaṃvutā: without support even below.

Andhakārā: the nature of darkness.

Andhakāratimisā: endowed with darkness that causes blindness, preventing the arising of visual consciousness.

There, it is said that visual consciousness does not arise.

Evaṃmahiddhikā: it is said that the moon and the sun appear in the three continents with a single striking, evaṃ mahiddhikā, thus mighty.

In each direction, they dispel the darkness for nine hundred thousand yojanas and show the light, evaṃmahānubhāvā, thus greatly powerful.

"Ābhāya nānubhontī": they do not have power over their own radiance.

It is said that they wander in the middle of the cakkavāḷapabbata, the cakravāla mountain, and the lokantarikanirayā, the hells of the lokantarika, beyond the cakravāla mountain.

Therefore, they do not have power over radiance there.

Lokantarikā
Aghā
Asaṃvutā
Andhakārā
Andhakāratimisā
Evaṃmahiddhikā
Evaṃmahānubhāvā.
Ābhāya nānubhontī

"Yepi tattha sattā": those beings who were born in that great hell of the lokantarika.

But what deeds did they do to be born there?

They are born having committed heavy and cruel offenses against mothers, fathers, virtuous ascetics, Brahmins and so on, and day after day committing violent deeds such as killing, like Abhaya, Cora, Nāga, Cora and others in Tambapaṇṇidīpa.

Their body is three gāvutas in size, and their nails are long like those of bats.

Like bats on trees, they cling to the cakravāla mountain with their nails.

When, crawling, they reach each other's hand, thinking, "We have not obtained food", those wretches fall into the water that sustains the world, turning around; even when the wind strikes them, they are torn off like madhuka fruits and fall into the water; as soon as they have fallen, they dissolve in the extremely salty water like a lump of flour.

Yepi tattha sattā
Indeed, sir, other beings exist. Just as we experience great suffering, so other beings have also come here to experience this suffering. They see that day.

However, this radiance does not last even for the duration of one rice-gruel drink; it only lasts for the duration of a finger-snap, like the radiance of lightning, and vanishes as soon as they say, "What is this?"

Saṅkampatī means it trembles all around.

The other two are synonyms of the preceding phrase.

Furthermore, appamāṇo cā, etc., was said to conclude the matter.

Indeed, sir, other beings exist. Saṅkampatī. Appamāṇo cā.
19
19. ‘‘Dhammatā esā, bhikkhave, yadā bodhisatto mātukucchiṃ okkanto hoti, cattāro naṃ devaputtā catuddisaṃ cātuddisaṃ (syā.) rakkhāya upagacchanti – ‘mā naṃ bodhisattaṃ vā bodhisattamātaraṃ vā manusso vā amanusso vā koci vā viheṭhesī’ti. Ayamettha dhammatā.
‘It is the rule (dharmatā) that when a bodhisattva has entered his mother’s womb, four deities (devaputtā) come to protect him in all four directions (catuddisaṃ), saying: “Let no man, no non-human being, no one at all harm this bodhisattva or this bodhisattva's mother!” This, monks, is the rule (dharmatā).
19.Cattāronaṃ devaputtā cātuddisaṃ rakkhāya upagacchantīti ettha cattāroti catunnaṃ mahārājānaṃ vasena vuttaṃ. Dasasahassacakkavāḷesu pana cattāro cattāro katvā cattālīsasahassāni honti. Tattha imasmiṃ cakkavāḷe mahārājāno khaggahatthā bodhisattassa ārakkhatthāya upagantvā sirigabbhaṃ paviṭṭhā, itare gabbhadvārato paṭṭhāya avaruddhake paṃsupisācakādiyakkhagaṇe paṭikkamāpetvā yāva cakkavāḷā ārakkhaṃ gaṇhiṃsu. Kimatthāya panāyaṃ rakkhā? Nanu paṭisandhikkhaṇe kalalakālato paṭṭhāya sacepi koṭisatasahassamārā koṭisatasahassasineruṃ ukkhipitvā bodhisattassa vā bodhisattamātuyā vā antarāyakaraṇatthaṃ āgaccheyyuṃ, sabbe antarāva antaradhāyeyyuṃ. Vuttampi cetaṃ bhagavatā ruhiruppādavatthusmiṃ – ‘‘aṭṭhānametaṃ, bhikkhave, anavakāso, yaṃ parupakkamena tathāgataṃ jīvitā voropeyya. Anupakkamena, bhikkhave, tathāgatā parinibbāyanti. Gacchatha, tumhe bhikkhave, yathāvihāraṃ, arakkhiyā, bhikkhave tathāgatā’’ti (cūḷava. 341). Evameva, tena parupakkamena na tesaṃ jīvitantarāyo atthi, santi kho pana amanussā virūpā duddasikā bheravarūpā migapakkhino, yesaṃ rūpaṃ vā disvā saddaṃ vā sutvā bodhisattamātu bhayaṃ vā santāso vā uppajjeyya, tesaṃ nivāraṇatthāya rakkhaṃ aggahesuṃ. Apica bodhisattassa puññatejena sañjātagāravā attano gāravacoditāpi te evamakaṃsu. Kiṃ pana te antogabbhaṃ pavisitvā ṭhitā cattāro mahārājāno bodhisattassa mātuyā attānaṃ dassenti, na dassentīti? Nahānamaṇḍanabhojanādisarīrakiccakāle na dassenti, sirigabbhaṃ pavisitvā varasayane nipannakāle pana dassenti. Tattha kiñcāpi amanussadassanaṃ nāma manussānaṃ sappaṭibhayaṃ hoti, bodhisattassa mātā pana attano ceva puttassa ca puññānubhāvena te disvā na bhāyati, pakatiantepurapālakesu viya assā etesu cittaṃ uppajjati.
19. "Four sons of the gods approach from the four directions for protection" means here that the four are mentioned in terms of the four Great Kings (catunnaṃ mahārājānaṃ vasena vuttaṃ).

Moreover, in ten thousand world-systems, making four and four, there are forty thousand.

There, in this world-system, the Great Kings, sword in hand, having approached for the Bodhisattva’s protection, entered the auspicious chamber; the others, beginning from the door of the womb, having driven back the groups of yakṣas, beginning with the paṃsupisācakas, maintained guard as far as the periphery of the world-system.

What is this protection for? Surely, from the moment of rebirth-linking, from the kalala stage onward, even if hundreds of thousands of Māras, having lifted hundreds of thousands of Mount Sinerus, should come for the purpose of causing an obstruction to the Bodhisattva or the Bodhisattva's mother, all would vanish in the intervening space.

Also, this was said by the Blessed One in the context of the origin of blood: "This is impossible, monks, there is no opportunity for one to deprive a Tathāgata of life by violence. Tathāgatas attain parinirvāṇa without violence. Go, monks, according to your dwelling places; Tathāgatas are unprotected" (Cūḷava. 341). Just so, by that violence there is no interruption of their life.

There are, however, non-human beings, deformed, of hideous appearance, of terrifying form, beasts and birds, whose form having been seen, or whose sound having been heard, fear or terror might arise in the Bodhisattva's mother; for the sake of preventing them, they maintained guard.

Furthermore, being respectful due to the power of the Bodhisattva’s merit, even urged on by their own respect, they did thus.

But do those four Great Kings, having entered the inner chamber, show themselves to the Bodhisattva’s mother, or do they not show themselves? They do not show themselves at the time of bathing, adorning, eating food, and other bodily activities; but having entered the auspicious chamber, they do show themselves at the time of lying down on the excellent bed.

There, although the sight of non-human beings is frightening to humans, the Bodhisattva’s mother, however, having seen them, does not fear, due to the power of her own merit and that of her son; confidence arises in her towards them, as towards the regular palace guards.
20
20. ‘‘Dhammatā esā, bhikkhave, yadā bodhisatto mātukucchiṃ okkanto hoti, pakatiyā sīlavatī bodhisattamātā hoti, viratā pāṇātipātā, viratā adinnādānā, viratā kāmesumicchācārā , viratā musāvādā, viratā surāmerayamajjappamādaṭṭhānā. Ayamettha dhammatā.
It is the natural law, monks, that when a Bodhisattva enters his mother's womb, his mother becomes naturally virtuous, abstaining from killing, abstaining from taking what is not given, abstaining from sexual misconduct, abstaining from false speech, and abstaining from intoxicants that cause negligence. This is the natural law ( ayamettha dhammatā).
20.Pakatiyā sīlavatīti sabhāveneva sīlasampannā. Anuppanne kira buddhe manussā tāpasaparibbājakānaṃ santike vanditvā ukkuṭikaṃ nisīditvā sīlaṃ gaṇhanti. Bodhisattamātāpi kāladevilassa isino santike sīlaṃ gaṇhāti. Bodhisatte pana kucchigate aññassa pādamūle nisīdituṃ nāma na sakkā, samānāsane nisīditvā gahitasīlampi āvajjanakaraṇamattaṃ hoti. Tasmā sayameva sīlaṃ aggahesīti vuttaṃ hoti.
20.
“Naturally virtuous” means endowed with virtue by nature itself.
It is said that when Buddhas have not yet arisen, humans, after venerating ascetics and wanderers, would sit squatting and take the precepts (śīla).
The Bodhisattva's mother also takes the precepts (śīla) in the presence of the sage Kāḷadevala.
However, when the Bodhisattva is in the womb, it is not possible to sit at the foot of another; even taking the precepts (śīla) while sitting on an equal seat is merely an act of consideration.
Therefore, it is said that she took the precepts (śīla) herself.
21
21. ‘‘Dhammatā esā, bhikkhave, yadā bodhisatto mātukucchiṃ okkanto hoti, na bodhisattamātu purisesu mānasaṃ uppajjati kāmaguṇūpasaṃhitaṃ, anatikkamanīyā ca bodhisattamātā hoti kenaci purisena rattacittena. Ayamettha dhammatā.
21. “This is the rule, bhikkhus, that when a bodhisattva has entered his mother’s womb (mātukucchiṃ okkanto hoti), no sensual thought (kāmaguṇūpasaṃhitaṃ) concerning men arises in his mother, and the bodhisattva's mother is unconquerable by any man with lustful thoughts (rattacittena). This is the natural law (ayamettha dhammatā)."
21.Purisesūti bodhisattassa pitaraṃ ādiṃ katvā kesuci manussesu purisādhippāyacittaṃ nuppajjati. Bodhisattamāturūpaṃ pana kusalā sippikā potthakammādīsupi kātuṃ na sakkonti. Taṃ disvā purisassa rāgo nuppajjatīti na sakkā vattuṃ, sace pana taṃ rattacitto upasaṅkamitukāmo hoti, pādā na vahanti, dibbasaṅkhalikā viya bajjhanti. Tasmā ‘‘anatikkamanīyā’’tiādi vuttaṃ.
21. Purisesū means that, starting with the bodhisattva's father, a desire focused on men does not arise in some people. However, even skilled artisans are unable to create a likeness of the bodhisattva in paintings and the like. It cannot be said that lust does not arise in a man upon seeing him; but if someone with a lustful mind wants to approach him, his feet do not carry him, they are bound as if by divine chains. Therefore, it is said, "unattainable," and so on.
22
22. ‘‘Dhammatā esā, bhikkhave, yadā bodhisatto mātukucchiṃ okkanto hoti, lābhinī bodhisattamātā hoti pañcannaṃ kāmaguṇānaṃ. Sā pañcahi kāmaguṇehi samappitā samaṅgībhūtā paricāreti. Ayamettha dhammatā.
“This is the rule, O monks, that when a bodhisattva has entered his mother’s womb (mātukucchiṃ okkanto), his mother obtains the five strands of sensual pleasure (pañcannaṃ kāmaguṇānaṃ). She enjoys herself, being endowed and possessed of the five strands of sensual pleasure (pañcahi kāmaguṇehi samappitā samaṅgībhūtā paricāreti). This is the natural law (ayamettha dhammatā)."
22.Pañcannaṃ kāmaguṇānanti pubbe kāmaguṇūpasañhitanti iminā purisādhippāyavasena vatthupaṭikkhepo kato, idha ārammaṇappaṭilābho dassito. Tadā kira deviyā evarūpo putto kucchiṃ upapannoti sutvā samantato rājāno mahagghaābharaṇatūriyādivasena pañcadvārārammaṇavatthubhūtaṃ paṇṇākāraṃ pesenti. Bodhisattassa ca bodhisattamātu ca katakammassa ussannattā lābhasakkārassa pamāṇaparicchedo natthi.
22. pañcannaṃ kāmaguṇāna - Previously, the rejection of the object (vatthu) was done based on the intention (adhippāya) of the person (purisa) through this “kāmaguṇūpasañhita”. Here, the attainment of the object (ārammaṇa) is shown.

Then, having heard that a son of such form had entered the womb (kucchiṃ upapanno) of the goddess (deviyā), kings from all around send tribute (paṇṇākāraṃ) consisting of precious (mahaggha) ornaments (ābharaṇa), musical instruments (tūriya), etc., through the five doors of sense perception which are the objects (ārammaṇa) of the basis (vatthu).

Because of the abundance of meritorious deeds (katakammassa ussannattā) of the Bodhisattva and the mother of the Bodhisattva, there is no limit (pamāṇaparicchedo natthi) to the gain (lābha) and honor (sakkāra).
23
23. ‘‘Dhammatā esā, bhikkhave, yadā bodhisatto mātukucchiṃ okkanto hoti, na bodhisattamātu kocideva ābādho uppajjati. Sukhinī bodhisattamātā hoti akilantakāyā, bodhisattañca bodhisattamātā tirokucchigataṃ passati sabbaṅgapaccaṅgiṃ ahīnindriyaṃ. Seyyathāpi, bhikkhave, maṇi veḷuriyo subho jātimā aṭṭhaṃso suparikammakato accho vippasanno anāvilo sabbākārasampanno. Tatrāssa tatrassa (syā.) suttaṃ āvutaṃ nīlaṃ vā pītaṃ vā lohitaṃ vā odātaṃ vā paṇḍusuttaṃ vā. Tamenaṃ cakkhumā puriso hatthe karitvā paccavekkheyya – ‘ayaṃ kho maṇi veḷuriyo subho jātimā aṭṭhaṃso suparikammakato accho vippasanno anāvilo sabbākārasampanno. Tatridaṃ suttaṃ āvutaṃ nīlaṃ vā pītaṃ vā lohitaṃ vā odātaṃ vā paṇḍusuttaṃ vā’ti. Evameva kho, bhikkhave, yadā bodhisatto mātukucchiṃ okkanto hoti, na bodhisattamātu kocideva ābādho uppajjati, sukhinī bodhisattamātā hoti akilantakāyā , bodhisattañca bodhisattamātā tirokucchigataṃ passati sabbaṅgapaccaṅgiṃ ahīnindriyaṃ. Ayamettha dhammatā.
23. It is the rule (dhammatā), monks, that when a Bodhisattva (bodhisatto) has entered his mother’s womb (mātukucchiṃ okkanto hoti), no sickness (ābādho) of any kind arises in the Bodhisattva’s mother (bodhisattamātu).

The Bodhisattva’s mother (bodhisattamātā) is happy (sukhinī) and without fatigue of body (akilantakāyā), and she can see the Bodhisattva (bodhisattañca) inside her womb (tirokucchigataṃ), complete with all his members and faculties (sabbaṅgapaccaṅgiṃ ahīnindriyaṃ).

Monks, it is as if there were a beautiful beryl gem (maṇi veḷuriyo) of purest water, eight-faceted (aṭṭhaṃso), well cut (suparikammakato), clear (accho), bright (vippasanno), unflawed (anāvilo), perfect in every respect (sabbākārasampanno).

And through it there would run a thread (suttaṃ āvutaṃ), blue (nīlaṃ) or yellow (pītaṃ) or red (lohitaṃ) or white (odātaṃ) or brown (paṇḍusuttaṃ).

Then a man with good sight (cakkhumā puriso), taking it in his hand (hatthe karitvā), might review it thus (paccavekkheyya): “This is a beautiful beryl gem (maṇi veḷuriyo) of purest water, eight-faceted (aṭṭhaṃso), well cut (suparikammakato), clear (accho), bright (vippasanno), unflawed (anāvilo), perfect in every respect (sabbākārasampanno).

And through it runs this thread (suttaṃ āvutaṃ), blue (nīlaṃ) or yellow (pītaṃ) or red (lohitaṃ) or white (odātaṃ) or brown (paṇḍusuttaṃ).”

So too, monks, when the Bodhisattva (bodhisatto) has descended into his mother’s womb (mātukucchiṃ okkanto hoti), no kind of affliction (ābādho) arises in the Bodhisattva’s mother (bodhisattamātu); the Bodhisattva’s mother (bodhisattamātā) is blissful (sukhinī) and free from bodily fatigue (akilantakāyā); and the Bodhisattva’s mother (bodhisattamātā) sees the Bodhisattva (bodhisattañca) within her womb (tirokucchigataṃ) with all his limbs (sabbaṅgapaccaṅgiṃ), lacking no faculty (ahīnindriyaṃ).

This is the rule (ayamettha dhammatā).
23.Akilantakāyāti yathā aññā itthiyo gabbhabhārena kilamanti hatthapādā uddhumātatādīni pāpuṇanti, evaṃ tassā koci kilamatho nāhosi. Tirokucchigatanti antokucchigataṃ. Passatīti kalalādikālaṃ atikkamitvā sañjātaaṅgapaccaṅgaahīnindriyabhāvaṃ upagataṃyeva passati. Kimatthaṃ passati? Sukhavāsatthaṃyeva. Yatheva hi mātā puttena saddhiṃ nipannā vā nisinnā vā – ‘‘hatthaṃ vāssa pādaṃ vā olambantaṃ ukkhipitvā saṇṭhapessāmī’’ti sukhavāsatthaṃ puttaṃ oloketi, evaṃ bodhisattamātāpi yaṃ taṃ mātu uṭṭhānagamanaparivattananisajjādīsu uṇhasītaloṇikatittakakaṭukāhāraajjhoharaṇakālesu ca gabbhassa dukkhaṃ uppajjati, ‘‘atthi nu kho me taṃ puttassā’’ti sukhavāsatthaṃ olokayamānā pallaṅkaṃ ābhujitvā nisinnaṃ bodhisattaṃ passati. Yathā hi aññe antokucchigatā pakkāsayaṃ avattharitvā āmāsayaṃ ukkhipitvā udarapaṭalaṃ piṭṭhito katvā piṭṭhikaṇḍakaṃ nissāya ukkuṭikaṃ dvīsu muṭṭhīsu hanukaṃ ṭhapetvā deve vassante rukkhasusire makkaṭā viya nisīdanti, na evaṃ bodhisatto, bodhisatto pana piṭṭhikaṇḍakaṃ piṭṭhito katvā dhammāsane dhammakathiko viya pallaṅkaṃ ābhujitvā puratthābhimukho nisīdati. Pubbekatakammaṃ panassā vatthuṃ sodheti, suddhe vatthumhi sukhumacchavilakkhaṇaṃ nibbattati. Atha naṃ kucchitaco paṭicchādetuṃ na sakkoti, olokentiyā bahiṭhito viya paññāyati. Tamatthaṃ upamāya vibhāvento bhagavā seyyathāpītiādimāha. Bodhisatto pana antokucchigato mātaraṃ na passati. Na hi antokucchiyaṃ cakkhuviññāṇaṃ uppajjati.
23. Akilantakāyā means that unlike other women who are exhausted by the burden of pregnancy and experience swollen hands and feet, she had no exhaustion.

Tirokucchigata means inside the womb.

Passatī means that having passed the stage of the embryo, she sees him with all his limbs developed and faculties unimpaired.

Why does she see? Just for his well-being. Just as a mother, lying down or sitting with her son, watches over her son for his well-being, thinking, "I will lift up and arrange his hand or foot if it is dangling," so too, the Bodhisatta's mother, while rising, walking, moving, sitting, and at times when the embryo experiences discomfort due to hot, cold, salty, bitter, or pungent food, she sees the Bodhisattva sitting cross-legged, looking to see if her son is suffering.

Just as other embryos lie with their backs against the stomach, pushing up the stomach, placing the abdominal wall behind the spine, crouching on their haunches, placing their chins on their fists like monkeys sitting in a tree hollow when the gods send rain, the Bodhisattva does not do that. The Bodhisattva, however, with his spine behind the spine, sits cross-legged facing east, like a speaker of the Dharma on a Dharma seat.

His previous meritorious actions purify his womb, and in the purified womb, a subtle, smooth, and distinctive complexion arises. Then, the impure skin is unable to conceal him, and he appears as if he is outside to the one who is looking. Explaining this matter with an analogy, the Blessed One said, "Seyyathāpi," and so forth.

However, the Bodhisattva inside the womb does not see his mother. For eye-consciousness does not arise inside the womb.
24
24. ‘‘Dhammatā esā, bhikkhave, sattāhajāte bodhisatte bodhisattamātā kālaṅkaroti tusitaṃ kāyaṃ upapajjati. Ayamettha dhammatā.
‘‘This is the natural law ( dharmatā eṣā), monks, that the Bodhisattva’s mother, seven days after the Bodhisattva's birth, dies and is reborn in the Tuṣita heaven. This is the rule (ayamettha dhammatā).’
24.Kālaṅkarotīti na vijātabhāvapaccayā, āyuparikkhayeneva. Bodhisattena vasitaṭṭhānañhi cetiyakuṭisadisaṃ hoti, aññesaṃ aparibhogārahaṃ, na ca sakkā bodhisattamātaraṃ apanetvā aññaṃ aggamahesiṭṭhāne ṭhapetunti tattakaṃyeva bodhisattamātu āyuppamāṇaṃ hoti, tasmā tadā kālaṅkaroti. Katarasmiṃ pana vaye kālaṃ karotīti? Majjhimavaye. Paṭhamavayasmiñhi sattānaṃ attabhāve chandarāgo balavā hoti, tena tadā sañjātagabbhā itthī gabbhaṃ anurakkhituṃ na sakkoti, gabbho bahvābādho hoti. Majjhimavayassa pana dve koṭṭhāse atikkamma tatiye koṭṭhāse vatthu visadaṃ hoti, visade vatthumhi nibbattadārakā arogā honti, tasmā bodhisattamātāpi paṭhamavaye sampattiṃ anubhavitvā majjhimavayassa tatiye koṭṭhāse vijāyitvā kālaṃ karotīti ayamettha dhammatā.
24. kālaṅkarotī iti [means]: not because of the condition of being born, but merely due to the exhaustion of lifespan.
