uṭṭhehī means he speaks requesting the Blessed One to wander for the purpose of teaching the Dhamma. In vīrā etc., the Blessed One is called vīro because he is energetic, vijitasaṅgāmo because he has conquered the gods, death, defilements, and Māra, satthavāho because he is capable of conveying beings to be trained across the ocean of existence, aṇaṇo because of the absence of the debt of sensual desire.

uṭṭhehī vīrā vīro, vijitasaṅgāmo, satthavāho, aṇaṇo
71
71. ‘‘Atha kho, bhikkhave, vipassī bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho taṃ mahābrahmānaṃ gāthāya ajjhabhāsi – ‘Apārutā tesaṃ amatassa dvārā, Ye sotavanto pamuñcantu saddhaṃ; Vihiṃsasaññī paguṇaṃ na bhāsiṃ, Dhammaṃ paṇītaṃ manujesu brahme’ti. ‘‘Atha kho so, bhikkhave, mahābrahmā ‘katāvakāso khomhi vipassinā bhagavatā arahatā sammāsambuddhena dhammadesanāyā’ti vipassiṃ bhagavantaṃ arahantaṃ sammāsambuddhaṃ abhivādetvā padakkhiṇaṃ katvā tattheva antaradhāyi. Aggasāvakayugaṃ
"71. Then, monks, Vipassī, the Blessed One, the worthy and perfectly enlightened one, addressed that Great Brahma with a verse:

‘Open are the doors to the deathless for those who have ears to hear; let them show their faith. Thinking of causing no harm, I did not speak of what is excellent, the refined dharma, among humans, Brahma.’

Then, monks, that Great Brahma, [thinking:] ‘I have been given the opportunity by Vipassī, the Blessed One, the worthy and perfectly enlightened one, for the teaching of the Dharma,’ having bowed down to Vipassī, the Blessed One, the worthy and perfectly enlightened one, and keeping his right side towards him, vanished there and then.

The foremost pair of disciples"
71.Apārutāti vivaṭā. Amatassa dvārāti ariyamaggo. So hi amatasaṅkhātassa nibbānassa dvāraṃ. So mayā vivaritvā ṭhapitoti dasseti. Pamuñcantu saddhanti sabbe attano saddhaṃ pamuñcantu vissajjentu. Pacchimapadadvaye ayamattho, ahañhi attano paguṇaṃ suppavattitampi imaṃ paṇītaṃ uttamaṃ dhammaṃ kāyavācākilamathasaññī hutvā na bhāsiṃ, idāni pana sabbe janā saddhābhājanaṃ upanentu, pūressāmi tesaṃ saṅkappanti. Aggasāvakayugavaṇṇanā
"apārutā" means open, uncovered.
"amatassa dvārā" means the path of the deathless (ariyamaggo).
For it is the gateway to nirvāṇa, which is known as the deathless.
He shows that it was opened and established by me.
"pamuñcantu saddhaṃ": Let everyone release and send forth their faith (saddhā).
This is the meaning of the latter two lines: Indeed, I, being weary in body and speech, did not speak this excellent and supreme dharma, [although it was] my own, skillful, and well-practiced. But now, let all people bring forth a vessel of faith; I will fulfill their intention.

Explanation of the foremost disciples.
72
72. ‘‘Atha kho, bhikkhave, vipassissa bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa etadahosi – ‘kassa nu kho ahaṃ paṭhamaṃ dhammaṃ deseyyaṃ, ko imaṃ dhammaṃ khippameva ājānissatī’ti? Atha kho, bhikkhave, vipassissa bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa etadahosi – ‘ayaṃ kho khaṇḍo ca rājaputto tisso ca purohitaputto bandhumatiyā rājadhāniyā paṭivasanti paṇḍitā viyattā medhāvino dīgharattaṃ apparajakkhajātikā. Yaṃnūnāhaṃ khaṇḍassa ca rājaputtassa, tissassa ca purohitaputtassa paṭhamaṃ dhammaṃ deseyyaṃ , te imaṃ dhammaṃ khippameva ājānissantī’ti.
"72.
Then, monks, it occurred to Vipassī, the Blessed One, the Worthy One, the Perfectly Enlightened One: ‘To whom should I first teach the dharma (dhammaṃ), who will quickly understand this dharma (dhammaṃ)?’ Then, monks, it occurred to Vipassī, the Blessed One, the Worthy One, the Perfectly Enlightened One: ‘Khaṇḍa the prince and Tissa the son of the purohita (purohitaputto) dwell in Bandhumatī, the capital city; they are wise, learned, intelligent, and for a long time have been of little passionate nature.
If now I teach the dharma (dhammaṃ) first to Khaṇḍa the prince and to Tissa the son of the purohita (purohitaputto), they will quickly understand this dharma (dhammaṃ).’"
73
73. ‘‘Atha kho, bhikkhave, vipassī bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho seyyathāpi nāma balavā puriso samiñjitaṃ vā bāhaṃ pasāreyya, pasāritaṃ vā bāhaṃ samiñjeyya; evameva bodhirukkhamūle antarahito bandhumatiyā rājadhāniyā kheme migadāye pāturahosi. Atha kho, bhikkhave, vipassī bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho dāyapālaṃ migadāyapālaṃ (syā.) āmantesi – ‘ehi tvaṃ, samma dāyapāla, bandhumatiṃ rājadhāniṃ pavisitvā khaṇḍañca rājaputtaṃ tissañca purohitaputtaṃ evaṃ vadehi – vipassī, bhante, bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho bandhumatiṃ rājadhāniṃ anuppatto kheme migadāye viharati, so tumhākaṃ dassanakāmo’ti. ‘Evaṃ, bhante’ti kho, bhikkhave, dāyapālo vipassissa bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa paṭissutvā bandhumatiṃ rājadhāniṃ pavisitvā khaṇḍañca rājaputtaṃ tissañca purohitaputtaṃ etadavoca – ‘vipassī, bhante, bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho bandhumatiṃ rājadhāniṃ anuppatto kheme migadāye viharati; so tumhākaṃ dassanakāmo’ti.
"73. Then, monks, the Blessed Vipassī, the Arhat, the perfectly awakened Buddha, just as a strong man might stretch out his flexed arm or flex it again, vanished from the foot of the Bodhi tree and reappeared in the royal capital of Bandhumatī, in the Deer Park of Khema.

Then, monks, the Blessed Vipassī, the Arhat, the perfectly awakened Buddha, addressed the park-keeper: ‘Come, good park-keeper, go to Bandhumatī, the royal capital, and say this to Prince Khaṇḍa and Tissa, the chaplain’s son: “The Blessed Vipassī, the Arhat, the perfectly awakened Buddha, has arrived at Bandhumatī, the royal capital, and is staying in the Deer Park of Khema; he wishes to see you.”’

‘Even so, venerable sir,’ said the park-keeper to the Blessed Vipassī, the Arhat, the perfectly awakened Buddha. Having agreed, he entered Bandhumatī, the royal capital, and said this to Prince Khaṇḍa and Tissa, the chaplain’s son: ‘The Blessed Vipassī, the Arhat, the perfectly awakened Buddha, has arrived at Bandhumatī, the royal capital, and is staying in the Deer Park of Khema; he wishes to see you.’"
73.Bodhirukkhamūleti bodhirukkhassa avidūre ajapālanigrodhe antarahitoti attho. Kheme migadāyeti isipatanaṃ tena samayena khemaṃ nāma uyyānaṃ hoti, migānaṃ pana abhayavāsatthāya dinnattā migadāyoti vuccati. Taṃ sandhāya vuttaṃ – ‘‘kheme migadāye’’ti. Yathā ca vipassī bhagavā, evaṃ aññepi buddhā paṭhamaṃ dhammadesanatthāya gacchantā ākāsena gantvā tattheva otaranti. Amhākaṃ pana bhagavā upakassa ājīvakassa upanissayaṃ disvā – ‘‘upako imaṃ addhānaṃ paṭipanno, so maṃ disvā sallapitvā gamissati. Atha puna nibbindanto āgamma arahattaṃ sacchikarissatī’’ti ñatvā aṭṭhārasayojanamaggaṃ padasāva agamāsi. Dāyapālaṃ āmantesīti disvāva punappunaṃ oloketvā – ‘‘ayyo no, bhante, āgato’’ti vatvā upagataṃ āmantesi.
73. bodhirukkhamūle means "at the foot of the bodhi tree," not far from the bodhi tree, at the ajapālanigrodha, without an interval.

kheme migadāye means that at that time, the deer park (isipatana) was a garden named Khema; it is called "deer park" (migadāya) because it was given for the sake of the deer to live without fear (abhayavāsa).

With reference to this, it was said, "kheme migadāye."

Just as Vipassī, the Blessed One (bhagavā), so also other Buddhas, when going for the first time to teach the Dharma, go through the sky and descend right there.

But our Blessed One (bhagavā), seeing the potential of Upaka, the ājīvaka, [thought,] "Upaka has set out on this path; having seen me, he will converse with me and go on.

Then, again, becoming disenchanted, he will come back and realize arhatship." Knowing this, he went on foot for eighteen yojana.

dāyapālaṃ āmantesī means that having seen him, looking again and again, he called out to the approaching one, saying, "Are you our master, venerable sir (bhante)?"
74
74. ‘‘Atha kho, bhikkhave, khaṇḍo ca rājaputto tisso ca purohitaputto bhaddāni bhaddāni yānāni yojāpetvā bhaddaṃ bhaddaṃ yānaṃ abhiruhitvā bhaddehi bhaddehi yānehi bandhumatiyā rājadhāniyā niyyiṃsu. Yena khemo migadāyo tena pāyiṃsu. Yāvatikā yānassa bhūmi, yānena gantvā yānā paccorohitvā pattikāva padikāva (syā.) yena vipassī bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho tenupasaṅkamiṃsu. Upasaṅkamitvā vipassiṃ bhagavantaṃ arahantaṃ sammāsambuddhaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdiṃsu.
“Then, monks, Khaṇḍa the prince, and Tissa the son of the chaplain, having harnessed fine carriages, mounted fine carriages, and drove out of Bandhumatī in fine carriages. They headed for the deer park of Khema. As far as the ground was suitable for carriages, they went by carriage, then alighted from the carriages and proceeded on foot to where Vipassī, the Blessed One, the worthy one, the fully awakened Buddha, was. Having approached Vipassī, the Blessed One, the worthy one, the fully awakened Buddha, they bowed down and sat down to one side.
75
75. ‘‘Tesaṃ vipassī bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho anupubbiṃ kathaṃ ānupubbikathaṃ (sī. pī.) kathesi, seyyathidaṃ – dānakathaṃ sīlakathaṃ saggakathaṃ kāmānaṃ ādīnavaṃ okāraṃ saṃkilesaṃ nekkhamme ānisaṃsaṃ pakāsesi. Yadā te bhagavā aññāsi kallacitte muducitte vinīvaraṇacitte udaggacitte pasannacitte, atha yā buddhānaṃ sāmukkaṃsikā dhammadesanā, taṃ pakāsesi – dukkhaṃ samudayaṃ nirodhaṃ maggaṃ. Seyyathāpi nāma suddhaṃ vatthaṃ apagatakāḷakaṃ sammadeva rajanaṃ paṭiggaṇheyya, evameva khaṇḍassa ca rājaputtassa tissassa ca purohitaputtassa tasmiṃyeva āsane virajaṃ vītamalaṃ dhammacakkhuṃ udapādi – ‘yaṃ kiñci samudayadhammaṃ, sabbaṃ taṃ nirodhadhamma’nti.
75. The Blessed Vipassī, the arhat, the perfectly awakened Buddha, gave them a graduated talk, that is to say, a talk on giving (dānakathā), a talk on virtue (śīlakathā), a talk on heaven (svargakathā), showing the danger, degradation, and corruption of sense-desires, and the profit of renunciation (naiskramya).

When the Lord knew that they had ready minds, pliable minds, open minds, uplifted minds, confident minds, he explained to them that teaching on the dharma which the awakened ones have themselves discovered: suffering (duḥkha), its origin (samudaya), its cessation (nirodha), and the path (mārga).

Just as a clean piece of cloth, free from grime, would properly take dye, in the same way the dustless, stainless eye of dharma arose for Khaṇḍa the prince and Tissa the chaplain’s son as they were sitting right there—"Whatever is subject to origination (samudayadharma) is all subject to cessation (nirodhadharma)."
76
76. ‘‘Te diṭṭhadhammā pattadhammā viditadhammā pariyogāḷhadhammā tiṇṇavicikicchā vigatakathaṃkathā vesārajjappattā aparappaccayā satthusāsane vipassiṃ bhagavantaṃ arahantaṃ sammāsambuddhaṃ etadavocuṃ – ‘abhikkantaṃ, bhante, abhikkantaṃ, bhante. Seyyathāpi, bhante, nikkujjitaṃ vā ukkujjeyya, paṭicchannaṃ vā vivareyya, mūḷhassa vā maggaṃ ācikkheyya, andhakāre vā telapajjotaṃ dhāreyya ‘‘cakkhumanto rūpāni dakkhantī’’ti. Evamevaṃ bhagavatā anekapariyāyena dhammo pakāsito. Ete mayaṃ, bhante, bhagavantaṃ saraṇaṃ gacchāma dhammañca. Labheyyāma mayaṃ, bhante, bhagavato santike pabbajjaṃ, labheyyāma upasampada’nti.
‘And they, having seen the dhamma, having attained the dhamma, having known the dhamma, having fathomed the dhamma, having crossed over and beyond uncertainty (vicikicchā), having no more perplexity, having gained fearlessness (vesārajja), independence of others with regard to the Teacher’s doctrine, said to Vipassi, the Blessed One, the worthy one, the perfectly enlightened Buddha (sammāsambuddha), “Magnificent, Lord, magnificent!

Just as, Lord, one might set upright what had been overturned, or reveal what was hidden, or show the way to one who was lost, or carry a lamp into the darkness so that those with eyes could see forms. Even so, Lord, the dhamma has been revealed in many ways by the Blessed One.

We go to the Blessed One for refuge (saraṇaṃ gacchāma), and to the dhamma. May we receive the going-forth (pabbajja) in the Lord’s presence, may we receive ordination (upasampada)!”
77
77. ‘‘Alatthuṃ kho , bhikkhave, khaṇḍo ca rājaputto, tisso ca purohitaputto vipassissa bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa santike pabbajjaṃ alatthuṃ upasampadaṃ. Te vipassī bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho dhammiyā kathāya sandassesi samādapesi samuttejesi sampahaṃsesi; saṅkhārānaṃ ādīnavaṃ okāraṃ saṃkilesaṃ nibbāne nekkhamme (syā.) ānisaṃsaṃ pakāsesi. Tesaṃ vipassinā bhagavatā arahatā sammāsambuddhena dhammiyā kathāya sandassiyamānānaṃ samādapiyamānānaṃ samuttejiyamānānaṃ sampahaṃsiyamānānaṃ nacirasseva anupādāya āsavehi cittāni vimucciṃsu. Mahājanakāyapabbajjā
“And so, monks, Prince Khaṇḍa and Tissa, the chaplain’s son, received the going-forth and ordination in the presence of the Blessed Vipassī, the worthy, fully awakened one. Then the Blessed Vipassī, the worthy, fully awakened one, instructed, encouraged, inspired, and gladdened them with a Dhamma talk, revealing the danger, inferiority, and defilement in conditioned things, and the benefit in nirvāṇa. As they were being instructed, encouraged, inspired, and gladdened by the Blessed Vipassī, the worthy, fully awakened one, with a Dhamma talk, before long their minds were freed from the corruptions without clinging."

The going forth of a large group.
77.Alatthunti kathaṃ alatthuṃ? Ehibhikkhubhāvena. Bhagavā kira tesaṃ iddhimayapattacīvarassūpanissayaṃ olokento anekāsu jātīsu cīvaradānādīni disvā etha bhikkhavotiādimāha . Te tāvadeva bhaṇḍū kāsāyavasanā aṭṭhahi bhikkhuparikkhārehi sarīrapaṭimukkeheva vassasatikattherā viya bhagavantaṃ namassamānāva nisīdiṃsu. Sandassesītiādīsu idhalokatthaṃ sandassesi, paralokatthaṃ sandassesi. Idhalokatthaṃ dassento aniccanti dassesi, dukkhanti dassesi, anattāti dassesi, khandhe dassesi, dhātuyo dassesi, āyatanāni dassesi, paṭiccasamuppādaṃ dassesi, rūpakkhandhassa udayaṃ dassento pañca lakkhaṇāni dassesi, tathā vedanākkhandhādīnaṃ, tathā vayaṃ dassentopi udayabbayavasena paññāsalakkhaṇāni dassesi, paralokatthaṃ dassento nirayaṃ dassesi, tiracchānayoniṃ, pettivisayaṃ, asurakāyaṃ, tiṇṇaṃ kusalānaṃ vipākaṃ, channaṃ devalokānaṃ, navannaṃ brahmalokānaṃ sampattiṃ dassesi. Sandassesī Samādapesīti catupārisuddhisīlaterasadhutaṅgadasakathāvatthuādike kalyāṇadhamme gaṇhāpesi. Samādapesī Samuttejesīti suṭṭhu uttejesi, abbhussāhesi. Idhalokatthañceva paralokatthañca tāsetvā tāsetvā adhigataṃ viya katvā kathesi. Dvattiṃsakammakāraṇapañcavīsatimahābhayappabhedañhi idhalokatthaṃ buddhe bhagavati tāsetvā tāsetvā kathayante pacchābāhaṃ, gāḷhabandhanaṃ bandhitvā cātumahāpathe pahārasatena tāḷetvā dakkhiṇadvārena niyyamāno viya āghātanabhaṇḍikāya ṭhapitasīso viya sūle uttāsito viya mattahatthinā maddiyamāno viya ca saṃviggo hoti. Paralokatthañca kathayante nirayādīsu nibbatto viya devalokasampattiṃ anubhavamāno viya ca hoti. Samuttejesī Sampahaṃsesīti paṭiladdhaguṇena codesi, mahānisaṃsaṃ katvā kathesīti attho. Sampahaṃsesī Saṅkhārānaṃ ādīnavanti heṭṭhā paṭhamamaggādhigamatthaṃ kāmānaṃ ādīnavaṃ kathesi, idha pana uparimaggādhigamatthaṃ – ‘‘aniccā, bhikkhave, saṅkhārā addhuvā anassāsikā, yāvañcidaṃ, bhikkhave, alameva sabbasaṅkhāresu nibbindituṃ alaṃ virajjituṃ alaṃ vimuccitu’’ntiādinā (a. ni. 7.66; saṃ. ni. 2.134) nayena saṅkhārānaṃ ādīnavañca lāmakabhāvañca tappaccayañca kilamathaṃ pakāsesi. Yathā ca tattha nekkhamme, evamidha – ‘‘santamidaṃ, bhikkhave, nibbānaṃ nāma paṇītaṃ tāṇaṃ leṇa’’ntiādinā nayena nibbāne ānisaṃsaṃ pakāsesi. Saṅkhārānaṃ ādīnava Mahājanakāyapabbajjāvaṇṇanā
77. "Alatthuṃ": How did they obtain? By becoming "Come, mendicants."

It is said that the Blessed One, looking at the underlying conditions for their bowl and robe made by psychic power, seeing the giving of robes, etc., in many births, said, "Etha bhikkhavo" [Come, mendicants] etc.

As soon as they were shaved, wearing brown robes, with the eight requisites of a monk, with bodies fully equipped, they sat down, venerating the Blessed One, like elders of a hundred years.

In "sandassesī" etc., he revealed the benefit for this world, he revealed the benefit for the next world.

Showing the benefit for this world, he showed impermanence, he showed suffering, he showed no-self, he showed the aggregates, he showed the elements, he showed the sense bases, he showed dependent origination, showing the arising of the form aggregate, he showed the five characteristics, likewise of the feeling aggregate, etc., likewise showing the passing away, he showed the fifty characteristics by way of arising and passing away; showing the benefit for the next world, he showed hell, the animal realm, the realm of ghosts, the demon body, the result of the three wholesome [deeds], the attainment of the six heavens, of the nine Brahma worlds.

Sandassesī

"Samādapesī": he made them grasp the wholesome qualities such as the fourfold purity of virtue, the thirteen ascetic practices, the ten topics of conversation.

Samādapesī

"Samuttejesī": he greatly encouraged, he heartened. Having frightened them repeatedly with the benefit for this world and the benefit for the next world, he spoke as if he had attained it.

Indeed, while describing the thirty-two acts of karma and the twenty-five divisions of great fear for the benefit of this world, having frightened them repeatedly in the Buddha, the Blessed One, one becomes agitated as if being led away backwards, having bound a tight bond, having struck with a hundred blows in the four great crossroads, being led out through the southern gate, as if his head were placed on the executioner's block, as if impaled on a stake, as if being crushed by a drunken elephant.

And while describing the benefit for the next world, one is as if reborn in hells, and as if experiencing the attainment of heavenly worlds.

Samuttejesī

"Sampahaṃsesī": he blamed them with the acquired qualities, meaning he spoke having made it of great benefit.

Sampahaṃsesī

"Saṅkhārānaṃ ādīnavaṃ": Below, he spoke of the danger of sensual pleasures for the attainment of the first path, but here, for the attainment of the higher paths - "Impermanent, monks, are conditioned things, unstable, unreliable; for as long as this, monks, it is enough to become disenchanted with all conditioned things, enough to be dispassionate, enough to be liberated" etc. (A. Ni. 7.66; Saṃ. Ni. 2.134), in this way he revealed the danger of conditioned things, their wretchedness, their conditionality, and the toil.

And just as there [it was] renunciation, so here - "Peaceful, monks, is this Nibbāna by name, excellent, a refuge, a shelter" etc., in this way he revealed the benefit in Nibbāna.

Saṅkhārānaṃ ādīnavaṃ

Description of Mahājanaka’s Going Forth
78
78. ‘‘Assosi kho, bhikkhave, bandhumatiyā rājadhāniyā mahājanakāyo caturāsītipāṇasahassāni – ‘vipassī kira bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho bandhumatiṃ rājadhāniṃ anuppatto kheme migadāye viharati. Khaṇḍo ca kira rājaputto tisso ca purohitaputto vipassissa bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa santike kesamassuṃ ohāretvā kāsāyāni vatthāni acchādetvā agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajitā’ti. Sutvāna nesaṃ etadahosi – ‘na hi nūna so orako dhammavinayo, na sā orakā pabbajjā, yattha khaṇḍo ca rājaputto tisso ca purohitaputto kesamassuṃ ohāretvā kāsāyāni vatthāni acchādetvā agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajitā. Khaṇḍo ca rājaputto tisso ca purohitaputto kesamassuṃ ohāretvā kāsāyāni vatthāni acchādetvā agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajissanti, kimaṅgaṃ pana maya’nti. Atha kho so, bhikkhave, mahājanakāyo caturāsītipāṇasahassāni bandhumatiyā rājadhāniyā nikkhamitvā yena khemo migadāyo yena vipassī bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho tenupasaṅkamiṃsu; upasaṅkamitvā vipassiṃ bhagavantaṃ arahantaṃ sammāsambuddhaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdiṃsu.
"Monks, the great crowd of eighty-four thousand beings in the capital city of Bandhumatī heard that the Blessed One Vipaśyī, the worthy and perfectly enlightened one, had arrived in Bandhumatī and was staying in the Khemā Deer Park.

They heard that Prince Khaṇḍa and Tissā, the son of the chaplain, had shaved off their hair and beards in the presence of the Blessed One Vipaśyī, the worthy and perfectly enlightened one, donned yellow robes, and gone forth from the household life into homelessness.

Having heard this, they thought: “This is certainly no common teaching and discipline, nor a common going forth, for which Prince Khaṇḍa and Tissā, the son of the chaplain, have shaved off their hair and beards, donned yellow robes, and gone forth from the household life into homelessness.

If Prince Khaṇḍa and Tissā, the son of the chaplain, can shave off their hair and beards, don yellow robes, and go forth from the household life into homelessness, why not us?”

Then, monks, that great crowd of eighty-four thousand beings left the capital city of Bandhumatī and went to the Khemā Deer Park where the Blessed One Vipaśyī, the worthy and perfectly enlightened one, was. Having approached, they paid homage to the Blessed One Vipaśyī, the worthy and perfectly enlightened one, and sat down to one side."
78.Mahājanakāyoti tesaṃyeva dvinnaṃ kumārānaṃ upaṭṭhākajanakāyoti.
78. Mahājanakāyo means the attending multitude of those two boys.
79
79. ‘‘Tesaṃ vipassī bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho anupubbiṃ kathaṃ kathesi. Seyyathidaṃ – dānakathaṃ sīlakathaṃ saggakathaṃ kāmānaṃ ādīnavaṃ okāraṃ saṃkilesaṃ nekkhamme ānisaṃsaṃ pakāsesi. Yadā te bhagavā aññāsi kallacitte muducitte vinīvaraṇacitte udaggacitte pasannacitte , atha yā buddhānaṃ sāmukkaṃsikā dhammadesanā, taṃ pakāsesi – dukkhaṃ samudayaṃ nirodhaṃ maggaṃ. Seyyathāpi nāma suddhaṃ vatthaṃ apagatakāḷakaṃ sammadeva rajanaṃ paṭiggaṇheyya, evameva tesaṃ caturāsītipāṇasahassānaṃ tasmiṃyeva āsane virajaṃ vītamalaṃ dhammacakkhuṃ udapādi – ‘yaṃ kiñci samudayadhammaṃ sabbaṃ taṃ nirodhadhamma’nti.
‘And the Lord Buddha Vipassī, worthy one, completely awakened, delivered to them a graduated discourse. Specifically, he gave a talk on generosity (dānakathā), a talk on morality (śīlakathā), a talk on heaven (svargakathā), and he revealed the danger (ādīnava), degradation (okāra), and corruption (saṃkilesa) of sense-desires (kāmānaṃ), and the profit (ānisaṃsa) of renunciation (naiskramya).

When the Blessed One understood that they had ready minds, pliable minds, minds free from hindrances, uplifted minds, and confident minds, then he revealed the teaching unique to the Buddhas: suffering (duḥkha), its origin (samudaya), its cessation (nirodha), and the path (mārga).

Just as a clean cloth, free from stains, would properly absorb dye, in the same way, in those eighty-four thousand beings, as they sat there, the spotless, immaculate eye of the Dharma (dharmacakṣu) arose: “Whatever is subject to origination (samudayadharma) is all subject to cessation (nirodhadharma).”
80
80. ‘‘Te diṭṭhadhammā pattadhammā viditadhammā pariyogāḷhadhammā tiṇṇavicikicchā vigatakathaṃkathā vesārajjappattā aparappaccayā satthusāsane vipassiṃ bhagavantaṃ arahantaṃ sammāsambuddhaṃ etadavocuṃ – ‘abhikkantaṃ, bhante, abhikkantaṃ, bhante. Seyyathāpi, bhante, nikkujjitaṃ vā ukkujjeyya, paṭicchannaṃ vā vivareyya, mūḷhassa vā maggaṃ ācikkheyya, andhakāre vā telapajjotaṃ dhāreyya ‘‘cakkhumanto rūpāni dakkhantī’’ti. Evamevaṃ bhagavatā anekapariyāyena dhammo pakāsito. Ete mayaṃ, bhante, bhagavantaṃ saraṇaṃ gacchāma dhammañca bhikkhusaṅghañca ( ) natthi aṭṭhakathāyaṃ, pāḷiyaṃ pana sabbatthapi dissati. Labheyyāma mayaṃ, bhante, bhagavato santike pabbajjaṃ labheyyāma upasampada’’nti.
‘And they, having seen the Dhamma ( dṛṣṭadharma), attained the Dhamma (prāptadharma), known the Dhamma (viditadharma), fathomed the Dhamma (pariyogāḍhadharma), having crossed over doubt (tīrṇavicikitsa), having no more uncertainty (vigatakathaṃkathā), having gained fearlessness (vaiśārajyaprāpta), independence of others with regard to the Teacher’s message, said to Vipassin, the Blessed One, the Worthy One, the perfectly awakened Buddha: “Excellent, Lord, excellent!

Just as if he were to place upright what was overturned, to reveal what was hidden, to show the way to one who was lost, or to carry a lamp into the dark so that those with eyes could see forms.

Just so has the Blessed One made clear the Dhamma in various ways (anekapariyāyena).

We go to the Blessed One for refuge, and to the Dhamma, and to the monastic community (bhikṣusaṃgha).

May we receive the going forth (pravrajyā) in the presence of the Blessed One, may we receive full ordination (upasampadā)!”
80.Bhagavantaṃ saraṇaṃ gacchāma, dhammañcāti saṅghassa aparipuṇṇattā dvevācikameva saraṇamagamaṃsu.
We go for refuge to the Blessed One (bhagavantaṃ saraṇaṃ gacchāma), and to the Dharma (dhammañcā). Because the Saṅgha was incomplete (saṅghassa aparipuṇṇattā), they took refuge with only a twofold declaration (dvevācikameva saraṇamagamaṃsu).
81
81. ‘‘Alatthuṃ kho, bhikkhave, tāni caturāsītipāṇasahassāni vipassissa bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa santike pabbajjaṃ, alatthuṃ upasampadaṃ. Te vipassī bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho dhammiyā kathāya sandassesi samādapesi samuttejesi sampahaṃsesi; saṅkhārānaṃ ādīnavaṃ okāraṃ saṃkilesaṃ nibbāne ānisaṃsaṃ pakāsesi. Tesaṃ vipassinā bhagavatā arahatā sammāsambuddhena dhammiyā kathāya sandassiyamānānaṃ samādapiyamānānaṃ samuttejiyamānānaṃ sampahaṃsiyamānānaṃ nacirasseva anupādāya āsavehi cittāni vimucciṃsu. Purimapabbajitānaṃ dhammābhisamayo
“And those eighty-four thousand, monks, received the going-forth at the hands of the Blessed Lord Vipassi (Vipassī), the Worthy One, the Perfectly Enlightened One, and they received ordination.

Then the Lord Buddha Vipassi (Vipassī), the Worthy One, the Perfectly Enlightened One, instructed them with a discourse on the Dhamma, inspired them, fired them and delighted them, revealing the danger, degradation, and corruption of conditioned things (saṅkhārānaṃ), and the benefit of nirvāṇa (nibbāne).

And through their being instructed, inspired, fired and delighted with this discourse by the Lord Vipassi (Vipassī), the Worthy One, the Perfectly Enlightened One, it was not long before their minds were freed from the corruptions without remainder.

The Realization of the Dhamma by the Former Ascetics
81.Alatthunti pubbe vuttanayeneva ehibhikkhubhāveneva alatthuṃ. Ito anantare pabbajitavārepi eseva nayo. Cārikāanujānanavaṇṇanā
alatthu, as before, was obtained through the state of "come, monk."
So too with the following ordination.
Explanation of allowing wanderings.
82
82. ‘‘Assosuṃ kho, bhikkhave, tāni purimāni caturāsītipabbajitasahassāni – ‘vipassī kira bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho bandhumatiṃ rājadhāniṃ anuppatto kheme migadāye viharati, dhammañca kira desetī’ti. Atha kho, bhikkhave, tāni caturāsītipabbajitasahassāni yena bandhumatī rājadhānī yena khemo migadāyo yena vipassī bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho tenupasaṅkamiṃsu; upasaṅkamitvā vipassiṃ bhagavantaṃ arahantaṃ sammāsambuddhaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdiṃsu.
Monks, those former eighty-four thousand ascetics heard, "The Blessed Vipassī, the worthy, fully awakened one, has arrived at Bandhumatī, the capital city, and is staying in the deer park of Khema, and it is said he is teaching the Dharma." Then, monks, those eighty-four thousand ascetics went to Bandhumatī, the capital city, to the deer park of Khema, to the Blessed Vipassī, the worthy, fully awakened one; having approached Vipassī, the Blessed One, the worthy, fully awakened one, they paid homage and sat down to one side.
83
83. ‘‘Tesaṃ vipassī bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho anupubbiṃ kathaṃ kathesi. Seyyathidaṃ – dānakathaṃ sīlakathaṃ saggakathaṃ kāmānaṃ ādīnavaṃ okāraṃ saṃkilesaṃ nekkhamme ānisaṃsaṃ pakāsesi. Yadā te bhagavā aññāsi kallacitte muducitte vinīvaraṇacitte udaggacitte pasannacitte, atha yā buddhānaṃ sāmukkaṃsikā dhammadesanā, taṃ pakāsesi – dukkhaṃ samudayaṃ nirodhaṃ maggaṃ. Seyyathāpi nāma suddhaṃ vatthaṃ apagatakāḷakaṃ sammadeva rajanaṃ paṭiggaṇheyya, evameva tesaṃ caturāsītipabbajitasahassānaṃ tasmiṃyeva āsane virajaṃ vītamalaṃ dhammacakkhuṃ udapādi – ‘yaṃ kiñci samudayadhammaṃ sabbaṃ taṃ nirodhadhamma’nti.
‘And the Lord Vipassī, the Blessed One, the arhat, the perfectly awakened Buddha, delivered to them a graduated discourse. Namely, he proclaimed a talk on generosity (dānakathā), a talk on morality (śīlakathā), a talk on heaven (svargakathā), [and] the danger (ādīnava), degradation (okāra), and corruption (saṃkilesa) of sensual pleasures (kāma), and the benefit (ānisaṃsa) of renunciation (naiskramya).

When the Blessed One knew that their minds were ready (kallacitte), supple (muducitte), free from hindrances (vinīvaraṇacitte), uplifted (udaggacitte), and confident (pasannacitte), then he revealed the teaching (dharmadeśanā) unique to the Buddhas (buddhānām): suffering (duḥkha), its origin (samudaya), its cessation (nirodha), and the path (mārga).

Just as a clean cloth, free of stains, would properly absorb dye, in the same way, as those eighty-four thousand renunciates were sitting right there, the stainless, immaculate eye of the Dharma (dharmacakṣus) arose within them: “Whatever is subject to origination (samudayadharma) is all subject to cessation (nirodhadharma).”
84
84. ‘‘Te diṭṭhadhammā pattadhammā viditadhammā pariyogāḷhadhammā tiṇṇavicikicchā vigatakathaṃkathā vesārajjappattā aparappaccayā satthusāsane vipassiṃ bhagavantaṃ arahantaṃ sammāsambuddhaṃ etadavocuṃ – ‘abhikkantaṃ , bhante, abhikkantaṃ, bhante. Seyyathāpi, bhante, nikkujjitaṃ vā ukkujjeyya, paṭicchannaṃ vā vivareyya, mūḷhassa vā maggaṃ ācikkheyya, andhakāre vā telapajjotaṃ dhāreyya ‘‘cakkhumanto rūpāni dakkhantī’’ti. Evamevaṃ bhagavatā anekapariyāyena dhammo pakāsito. Ete mayaṃ, bhante, bhagavantaṃ saraṇaṃ gacchāma dhammañca bhikkhusaṅghañca. Labheyyāma mayaṃ, bhante, bhagavato santike pabbajjaṃ labheyyāma upasampada’’nti.
‘And they, having seen, attained, experienced and penetrated the Dhamma (dharma), having passed beyond doubt, having gained perfect confidence in the Teacher’s (śāstṛ) doctrine (śāsana) without relying on others, said to Vipassī, the Blessed One (bhagavān), the Worthy One (arhan), the Perfectly Awakened One (samyaksaṃbuddha): “Excellent, O Lord (bhante), excellent, O Lord. Just as one might set upright what had been overturned, or reveal what had been hidden, or show the way to one who was lost, or bring a lamp into the darkness so that those with eyes might see what is there—in the same way has the Blessed One (bhagavatā) made the Dhamma (dharma) clear in various ways (anekapariyāyena). We go for refuge to the Blessed One (bhagavantaṃ), to the Dhamma (dhamma), and to the Sangha (saṅgha) of monks (bhikkhusaṅghañca). May we receive the going-forth (pravrajyā) at the Lord’s (bhagavato) hands, may we receive ordination (upasampadā)!”’
85
85. ‘‘Alatthuṃ kho, bhikkhave, tāni caturāsītipabbajitasahassāni vipassissa bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa santike pabbajjaṃ alatthuṃ upasampadaṃ. Te vipassī bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho dhammiyā kathāya sandassesi samādapesi samuttejesi sampahaṃsesi; saṅkhārānaṃ ādīnavaṃ okāraṃ saṃkilesaṃ nibbāne ānisaṃsaṃ pakāsesi. Tesaṃ vipassinā bhagavatā arahatā sammāsambuddhena dhammiyā kathāya sandassiyamānānaṃ samādapiyamānānaṃ samuttejiyamānānaṃ sampahaṃsiyamānānaṃ nacirasseva anupādāya āsavehi cittāni vimucciṃsu. Cārikāanujānanaṃ
‘And those eighty-four thousand who had gone forth, monks, received the going forth at the hands of the Blessed Lord Vipassī, the Worthy One, the Fully Awakened One; they received ordination.

Then the Blessed Vipassī, the Worthy One, the Fully Awakened One, instructed, roused, inspired, and delighted them with a discourse on the Dharma, revealing the danger, degradation, and corruption of conditioned things and the benefit of nirvāṇa (nibbāna).

And through their being instructed, roused, inspired, and delighted with this discourse by the Blessed Vipassī, the Worthy One, the Fully Awakened One, before long their minds were freed from the corruptions without grasping.

Permission to Wander
85.Parivitakko udapādīti kadā udapādi? Sambodhito satta saṃvaccharāni satta māse satta divase atikkamitvā udapādi. Bhagavā kira pitusaṅgahaṃ karonto vihāsi. Rājāpi cintesi – ‘‘mayhaṃ jeṭṭhaputto nikkhamitvā buddho jāto, dutiyaputto me nikkhamitvā aggasāvako jāto, purohitaputto dutiyaaggasāvako, ime ca avasesā bhikkhū gihikālepi mayhaṃ puttameva parivāretvā vicariṃsu. Ime sabbe idānipi mayhaṃyeva bhāro, ahameva ca ne catūhi paccayehi upaṭṭhahissāmi, aññesaṃ okāsaṃ na dassāmī’’ti vihāradvārakoṭṭhakato paṭṭhāya yāva rājagehadvārā ubhayato khadirapākāraṃ kārāpetvā kilañjehi chādāpetvā vatthehi paṭicchādāpetvā upari ca chādāpetvā suvaṇṇatārakavicittaṃ samolambitatālakkhandhamattaṃ vividhapupphadāmavitānaṃ kārāpetvā heṭṭhā bhūmiyaṃ cittattharaṇehi santharāpetvā anto ubhosu passesu mālāvacchake puṇṇaghaṭe, sakalamaggavāsatthāya ca gandhantare pupphāni pupphantare gandhe ca ṭhapāpetvā bhagavato kālaṃ ārocāpesi. Bhagavā bhikkhusaṅghaparivuto antosāṇiyāva rājagehaṃgantvā bhattakiccaṃ katvā vihāraṃ paccāgacchati. Añño koci daṭṭhumpi na labhati, kuto pana bhikkhaṃ vā dātuṃ, pūjaṃ vā kātuṃ, dhammaṃ vā sotuṃ. Nāgarā cintesuṃ – ‘‘ajja satthu loke uppannassa sattamāsādhikāni sattasaṃvaccharāni, mayañca daṭṭhumpi na labhāma, pageva bhikkhaṃ vā dātuṃ, pūjaṃ vā kātuṃ, dhammaṃ vā sotuṃ. Rājā – ‘mayhameva buddho, mayhameva dhammo, mayhameva saṅgho’ti mamāyitvā sayameva upaṭṭhahi. Satthā ca uppajjamāno sadevakassa lokassa atthāya hitāya uppanno. Na hi raññoyeva nirayo uṇho assa, aññesaṃ nīluppalavanasadiso. Tasmā rājānaṃ vadāma. Sace no satthāraṃ deti, iccetaṃ kusalaṃ. No ce deti, raññā saddhiṃ yujjhitvāpi saṅghaṃ gahetvā dānādīni puññāni karoma. Na sakkā kho pana suddhanāgareheva evaṃ kātuṃ, ekaṃ jeṭṭhapurisampi gaṇhāmā’’ti. Te senāpatiṃ upasaṅkamitvā tassetamatthaṃ ārocetvā – ‘‘sāmi, kiṃ amhākaṃ pakkho hosi, udāhu rañño’’ti āhaṃsu. So – ‘‘ahaṃ tumhākaṃ pakkho homi, api ca kho pana paṭhamadivaso mayhaṃ dātabbo’’ti. Te sampaṭicchiṃsu. So rājānaṃ upasaṅkamitvā – ‘‘nāgarā, deva, tumhākaṃ kupitā’’ti āha. Kimatthaṃ tātāti? Satthāraṃ kira tumheyeva upaṭṭhahatha, amhe na labhāmāti. Sace idānipi labhanti, na kuppanti, alabhantā tumhehi saddhiṃ yujjhitukāmā devāti. Yujjhāmi, tāta, nāhaṃ bhikkhusaṅghaṃ demīti. Deva tumhākaṃ dāsā tumhehi saddhiṃ yujjhāmāti vadanti, tumhe kaṃ gaṇhitvā yujjhissathāti? Nanu tvaṃ senāpatīti? Nāgarehi vinā na samattho ahaṃ devāti. Tato rājā – ‘‘balavanto nāgarā, senāpatipi tesaññeva pakkho’’ti ñatvā ‘‘aññānipi sattamāsādhikāni sattasaṃvaccharāni mayhaṃ bhikkhusaṅghaṃ dadantū’’ti āha. Nāgarā na sampaṭicchiṃsu. Rājā – ‘‘cha vassāni, pañca, cattāri, tīṇi, dve, ekavassa’’nti hāpesi. Evaṃ hāpentepi na sampaṭicchiṃsu. Aññe satta divase yāci. Nāgarā – ‘‘atikakkhaḷaṃ dāni raññā saddhiṃ kātuṃ na vaṭṭatī’’ti anujāniṃsu. Rājā sattamāsādhikānaṃ sattannaṃ saṃvaccharānaṃ sajjitaṃ dānamukhaṃ sattannameva divasānaṃ vissajjetvā cha divase kesañci apassantānaṃyeva dānaṃ datvā sattame divase nāgare pakkosāpetvā – ‘‘sakkhissatha, tāta, evarūpaṃ dānaṃ dātu’’nti āha. Tepi – ‘‘nanu amheyeva nissāya taṃ devassa uppanna’’nti vatvā – ‘‘sakkhissāmā’’ti āhaṃsu. Rājā piṭṭhihatthena assūni puñchamāno bhagavantaṃ vanditvā – ‘‘bhante, ahaṃ aṭṭhasaṭṭhibhikkhusatasahassaṃ aññassa vāraṃ akatvā yāvajīvaṃ catūhi paccayehi upaṭṭhahissāmīti cintesiṃ. Nāgarā na dāni me anuññātā, nāgarā hi ‘mayaṃ dānaṃ dātuṃ na labhāmā’ti kuppanti. Bhagavā sve paṭṭhāya tesaṃ anuggahaṃ karothā’’ti āha. Atha dutiyadivase senāpati mahādānaṃ sajjetvā – ‘‘ajja yathā añño koci ekabhikkhampi na deti, evaṃ rakkhathā’’ti samantā purise ṭhapesi. Taṃ divasaṃ seṭṭhibhariyā rodamānā dhītaraṃ āha – ‘‘sace, amma, tava pitā jīveyya, ajjāhaṃ paṭhamaṃ dasabalaṃ bhojeyya’’nti . Sā taṃ āha – ‘‘amma, mā cintayi, ahaṃ tathā karissāmi yathā buddhappamukho bhikkhusaṅgho paṭhamaṃ amhākaṃ bhikkhaṃ paribhuñjissatī’’ti. Tato satasahassagghanikāya suvaṇṇapātiyā nirudakapāyāsassa pūretvā sappimadhusakkarādīhi abhisaṅkharitvā aññāya pātiyā paṭikujjitvā taṃ sumanamālāguḷehi parikkhipitvā mālāguḷasadisaṃ katvā bhagavato gāmaṃ pavisanavelāya sayameva ukkhipitvā dāsigaṇaparivutā nagarā nikkhami. Antarāmagge senāpatiupaṭṭhākā – ‘‘amma, mā ito agamā’’ti vadanti. Mahāpuññā nāma manāpakathā honti, na ca tesaṃ punappunaṃ bhaṇantānaṃ kathā paṭikkhipituṃ sakkā hoti. Sā – ‘‘cūḷapitā mahāpitā mātulā kissa tumhe gantuṃ na dethā’’ti āha. Senāpatinā – ‘‘aññassa kassaci khādanīyabhojanīyaṃ dātuṃ mā dethā’’ti ṭhapitamha ammāti. Kiṃ pana me hatthe khādanīyaṃ bhojanīyaṃ passathāti? Mālāguḷaṃ passāmāti . Kiṃ tumhākaṃ senāpati mālāguḷapūjampi kātuṃ na detīti? Deti, ammāti. Tena hi, apetha, apethāti bhagavantaṃ upasaṅkamitvā mālāguḷaṃ gaṇhāpetha bhagavāti āha. Bhagavā ekaṃ senāpatissupaṭṭhākaṃ oloketvā mālāguḷaṃ gaṇhāpesi. Sā bhagavantaṃ vanditvā – ‘‘bhagavā, bhavābhave nibbattiyaṃ me sati paritassanajīvitaṃ nāma mā hotu, ayaṃ sumanamālā viya nibbattanibbattaṭṭhāne piyāva homi, nāmena ca sumanā yevā’’ti patthanaṃ katvā satthārā – ‘‘sukhinī hohī’’ti vuttā vanditvā padakkhiṇaṃ katvā pakkāmi. Bhagavā senāpatissa gehaṃ gantvā paññattāsane nisīdi. Senāpati yāguṃ gahetvā upagañchi, satthā pattaṃ pidahi. Nisinno, bhante, bhikkhusaṅghoti. Atthi no eko antarā piṇḍapāto laddhoti. So mālaṃ apanetvā piṇḍapātaṃ addasa. Cūḷupaṭṭhāko āha – ‘‘sāmi, mālāti maṃ vatvā mātugāmo vañcesī’’ti. Pāyāso bhagavantaṃ ādiṃ katvā sabbesaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ pahoti. Senāpatipi attano deyyadhammaṃ adāsi. Satthā bhattakiccaṃ katvā maṅgalaṃ vatvā pakkāmi. Senāpati – ‘‘kā nāma sā piṇḍapātamadāsī’’ti pucchi. Seṭṭhidhītā, sāmīti. Sappaññā sā itthī, evarūpāya ghare vasantiyā purisassa saggasampatti nāma na dullabhāti taṃ ānetvā jeṭṭhikaṭṭhāne ṭhapesi. Punadivase nāgarā dānamadaṃsu, punadivase rājāti ekantarikāya dānaṃ dātuṃ ārabhiṃsu. Rājāpi carapurise ṭhapetvā nāgarehi dinnadānato atirekataraṃ deti, nāgarāpi tatheva katvā raññā dinnadānato atirekataraṃ. Rājagehe nāṭakitthiyo daharasāmaṇere vadanti – ‘‘gaṇhatha, tātā, na gahapatikānaṃ gattavatthādīsu puñchitvā bāḷadārakānaṃ kheḷasiṅghāṇikādidhovanahatthehi kataṃ , suciṃ paṇītaṃ kata’’nti. Punadivase nāgarāpi dadamānā vadanti – ‘‘gaṇhatha, tātā, na nagaragāmanigamādīsu saṅkaḍḍhitataṇḍulakhīradadhisappiādīhi, na aññesaṃ jaṅghasīsapiṭṭhiādīni bhañjitvā āharāpitehi kataṃ, jātisappikhīrādīhiyeva kata’’nti. Evaṃ sattasu saṃvaccharesu sattasu māsesu sattasu divasesu ca atikkantesu atha bhagavato ayaṃ vitakko udapādi. Tena vuttaṃ – ‘‘sambodhito satta saṃvaccharāni satta māsāni satta divasāni atikkamitvā udapādī’’ti.
85. The thought, parivitakko udapādī, arose. When did it arise? It arose seven years, seven months, and seven days after his enlightenment.

The Blessed One stayed there, showing kindness to his father.

The king also thought: “My eldest son, having gone forth, has become a buddha. My second son, having gone forth, has become the foremost disciple. The son of the chaplain is the second foremost disciple. And these remaining monks, even in their householder days, having surrounded my son, wandered about.

Now even these all are my burden. I myself will attend to them with the four requisites. I will not give the opportunity to others.” From the gatehouse of the monastery to the gate of the royal palace, on both sides, having had a khadira wall built, having had it covered with mats, having had it covered over with cloths, and having had it covered over above, having had various puṣpa-dāma-vitāna decorated with golden stars and as tall as a palm tree hung, and having had the ground below strewn with painted carpets, having had full jars placed at the entrance on both sides, in the mālāvacaka, and having had flowers placed among perfumes and perfumes among flowers for the sake of the entire road being fragrant, he announced the time to the Blessed One.

The Blessed One, surrounded by the Saṃgha of monks, went to the royal palace as far as the inner courtyard, and having performed the meal duties, returned to the monastery.

No one else is able to see him, how much less to give alms, to do reverence, or to hear the Dharma!

The townspeople thought: “Today it is seven years and seven months since the Teacher arose in the world, and we do not even get to see him, how much less to give alms, to do reverence, or to hear the Dharma!

The king, thinking, ‘The Buddha is mine, the Dharma is mine, the Saṃgha is mine,’ attends to him himself, being possessive.

The Teacher, arising, has arisen for the benefit and welfare of the world with its devas.

Indeed, hell would not be hot for the king alone, but like a grove of blue lotuses for others.

Therefore, let us speak to the king.

If he gives us the Teacher, that would be good.

If he does not give us [the Teacher], having fought with the king, we will take the Saṃgha and perform meritorious deeds such as giving alms.

It is not possible to do this just with pure townspeople. Let us take one elder too.”

Having approached the commander-in-chief, having announced this matter to him, they said: “Lord, which side are you on, ours or the king’s?”

He said: “I am on your side, but the first day must be given to me.”

They agreed.

He approached the king and said: “The townspeople, Your Majesty, are angry.”

“Why, friends?” [he asked.] “You alone attend to the Teacher. We do not get to.”

"If they are able to obtain [the Teacher] even now, they will not be angry. If they do not obtain [the Teacher], they wish to fight with you, Your Majesty!"

“I will fight, friends, but I will not give the Saṃgha of monks."

"Your Majesty, your servants say, 'We will fight with you.' Whom will you take and fight with? Are you not the commander-in-chief? Without the townspeople, I am not capable, Your Majesty.”

Then the king, having learned that “the townspeople are powerful, and the commander-in-chief is on their side,” said: "Let even others give the Saṃgha of monks to me for seven years with seven months added!"

The townspeople did not agree.

The king reduced it: “Six years, five, four, three, two, one year.”

Even while he was reducing it like this, they did not agree.

He asked for seven other days.

The townspeople consented: “It is not fitting to act too harshly with the king now.”

The king, having given away in only seven days the provisions for giving that had been prepared for seven years with seven months added, having given alms to some without looking [at them] for six days, on the seventh day, having had the townspeople summoned, said: "Will you be able, friends, to give alms of this kind?"

They too, saying, “Is it not because of us that that arose for Your Majesty?”, said, “We will be able.”

The king, wiping away his tears with the back of his hand, having bowed to the Blessed One, [said:] “Venerable sir, I was thinking, ‘Without giving a turn to anyone else, I will attend to the Saṃgha of eighty-eight hundred thousand monks for as long as I live with the four requisites.’”
The townspeople are not now permitted by me [to give alms]. Indeed, the townspeople are angry, [thinking,] "We are unable to make a gift."

The Blessed One said, "Then, starting from today, show them favor."


Then, on the second day, the general prepared a great gift, [stationing] men all around, [saying,] "Today, ensure that not even a single alms-seeker is given [anything] by anyone else."

On that day, the treasurer's wife, weeping, said to her daughter, "If, dear, your father were alive, today I would feed the Ten-Powered One first."
.

She said to him, "Dear, do not worry. I will act in such a way that the Saṃgha of monks, with the Buddha at its head, will partake of our alms-food first."

Then, having filled a golden bowl worth one hundred thousand [coins] with rice porridge without water, having mixed it with ghee, honey, sugar, and so forth, having inverted it with another bowl, having surrounded it with sumana flower garlands, having made it similar to a garland of flowers, she herself, lifting it up at the time of entering the Blessed One's village, went out from the city, surrounded by a troop of servant girls.

On the road in between, the general's attendants said, "Dear, do not go this way."

The stories of those of great merit are pleasing, and it is not possible to reject the words of those who speak again and again.

She said, "Why do you not allow [me] to go to [my] paternal uncles, maternal uncles, [and] paternal grandfathers?"

The general had it established [that] -- "Do not give anything edible or enjoyable to anyone else" -- [so he] said, "Dear."

"But what edible or enjoyable thing do you see in my hand?" "We see a flower garland," they said.
.

"Does your general not even allow [you] to make an offering of a flower garland?" "He allows it, dear."

"Then, move aside, move aside," she said, and approaching the Blessed One, she had the flower garland accepted by the Blessed One.

The Blessed One, gazing at one of the general's attendants, had the flower garland accepted.

She, venerating the Blessed One, [made] a wish -- "Blessed One, may I never have a life of suffering when there is rebirth in existence or non-existence. Like this sumana flower garland, may I always be dear in the place of repeated rebirths, and may I be named sumanā." -- and having made [this] wish, having been told by the Teacher, "May you be happy," having venerated [him], having circumambulated [him] to the right, she departed.





The Blessed One, going to the general's house, sat down on the prepared seat.

The general, taking rice gruel, approached. The Teacher covered [his] bowl.

"[The] Saṃgha of monks [is] seated, venerable sir," [he said].

"Is there not even one alms-portion obtained in between?"

He removed the garland and saw an alms-portion.

The lesser attendant said, "Master, the woman deceived [me] by saying 'garland.'"

The rice porridge is sufficient for all the monks, having made the Blessed One the first [to partake].

The general also gave his gift-worthy-thing.

The Teacher, having done the meal-duty, having spoken a blessing, departed.

The general asked, "What [is] the name [of] that [woman] who gave the alms-portion?"

"[She is] the treasurer's daughter, master."

"She is a wise woman. For a man with such a [woman] living in [his] house, heavenly wealth is not difficult to obtain," he said, and having brought her, he established [her] in the place of the chief [wife].


On another day, the townspeople gave gifts. On another day, the king also began to give gifts on alternate days.

The king, also, having stationed roaming men, gave more than the gift given by the townspeople. The townspeople, also, having done likewise, [gave] more than the gift given by the king.

In the royal palace, dancing girls said to the young novices, "Take [this]. [It has] not been wiped on the clothes and so forth of householders, [but] has been made with hands washed of the phlegm, snot, and so forth of foolish children.
, It is clean and excellent," they said.

On another day, the townspeople also, while giving, said, "Take [this]. [It has] not been drawn together with rice, milk, curds, ghee, and so forth in towns, villages, [and] market towns, [and it has] not been made with the legs, heads, backs, and so forth of others having been broken [to acquire it, but] it has been made purely with ghee, milk, and so forth."

Thus, when seven years, seven months, [and] seven days had passed, then this thought arose in the Blessed One's mind.Thus it was said – “Having awakened, after passing seven years, seven months, and seven days, [he] arose."
86
86. ‘‘Tena kho pana, bhikkhave, samayena bandhumatiyā rājadhāniyā mahābhikkhusaṅgho paṭivasati aṭṭhasaṭṭhibhikkhusatasahassaṃ. Atha kho, bhikkhave, vipassissa bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa rahogatassa paṭisallīnassa evaṃ cetaso parivitakko udapādi – ‘mahā kho etarahi bhikkhusaṅgho bandhumatiyā rājadhāniyā paṭivasati aṭṭhasaṭṭhibhikkhusatasahassaṃ, yaṃnūnāhaṃ bhikkhū anujāneyyaṃ – ‘caratha, bhikkhave, cārikaṃ bahujanahitāya bahujanasukhāya lokānukampāya atthāya hitāya sukhāya devamanussānaṃ; mā ekena dve agamittha; desetha, bhikkhave , dhammaṃ ādikalyāṇaṃ majjhekalyāṇaṃ pariyosānakalyāṇaṃ sātthaṃ sabyañjanaṃ kevalaparipuṇṇaṃ parisuddhaṃ brahmacariyaṃ pakāsetha. Santi sattā apparajakkhajātikā, assavanatā dhammassa parihāyanti, bhavissanti dhammassa aññātāro. Api ca channaṃ channaṃ vassānaṃ accayena bandhumatī rājadhānī upasaṅkamitabbā pātimokkhuddesāyā’’’ti.
86. Now at that time, in the royal capital of Bandhumatī, there was dwelling a great assembly of monks, numbering six million eight hundred thousand.

Then, monks, it occurred as a thought in the mind of the Blessed Vipassī, the worthy, the fully enlightened one, while he was in seclusion and withdrawn: "Indeed, there is now a great assembly of monks dwelling in the royal capital of Bandhumatī, numbering six million eight hundred thousand. What if I were to give permission to the monks: ‘Wander forth, monks, on tour (cārikaṃ), for the good of the many, for the happiness of the many, out of compassion for the world, for the benefit, welfare and happiness of gods (deva) and humans (manussa). Let not two go in the same direction. Teach, monks, the Dharma (dhammaṃ) that is good in the beginning (ādikalyāṇaṃ), good in the middle (majjhekalyāṇaṃ), and good in the end (pariyosānakalyāṇaṃ), with its meaning (sātthaṃ) and with its expression (sabyañjanaṃ), utterly complete (kevalaparipuṇṇaṃ) and perfectly pure (parisuddhaṃ), the holy life (brahmacariyaṃ) make known (pakāsetha).

There are beings (sattā) with little dust in their eyes (apparajakkhajātikā), who are falling away through not hearing the Dharma (dhammassa). They will be those who will understand (aññātāro) the Dharma (dhammassa).

Moreover, at the end of six years precisely, the royal capital of Bandhumatī should be approached for the recitation of the Pāṭimokkha (pātimokkhuddesāyā).'"
87
87. ‘‘Atha kho, bhikkhave, aññataro mahābrahmā vipassissa bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa cetasā cetoparivitakkamaññāya seyyathāpi nāma balavā puriso samiñjitaṃ vā bāhaṃ pasāreyya, pasāritaṃ vā bāhaṃ samiñjeyya. Evameva brahmaloke antarahito vipassissa bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa purato pāturahosi. Atha kho so, bhikkhave, mahābrahmā ekaṃsaṃ uttarāsaṅgaṃ karitvā yena vipassī bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho tenañjaliṃ paṇāmetvā vipassiṃ bhagavantaṃ arahantaṃ sammāsambuddhaṃ etadavoca – ‘evametaṃ, bhagavā, evametaṃ, sugata. Mahā kho, bhante, etarahi bhikkhusaṅgho bandhumatiyā rājadhāniyā paṭivasati aṭṭhasaṭṭhibhikkhusatasahassaṃ, anujānātu, bhante, bhagavā bhikkhū – ‘‘caratha, bhikkhave, cārikaṃ bahujanahitāya bahujanasukhāya lokānukampāya atthāya hitāya sukhāya devamanussānaṃ; mā ekena dve agamittha; desetha, bhikkhave, dhammaṃ ādikalyāṇaṃ majjhekalyāṇaṃ pariyosānakalyāṇaṃ sātthaṃ sabyañjanaṃ kevalaparipuṇṇaṃ parisuddhaṃ brahmacariyaṃ pakāsetha. Santi sattā apparajakkhajātikā, assavanatā dhammassa parihāyanti, bhavissanti dhammassa aññātāro’’ti aññātāro (ssabbattha). Api ca, bhante, mayaṃ tathā karissāma yathā bhikkhū channaṃ channaṃ vassānaṃ accayena bandhumatiṃ rājadhāniṃ upasaṅkamissanti pātimokkhuddesāyā’ti. Idamavoca, bhikkhave, so mahābrahmā, idaṃ vatvā vipassiṃ bhagavantaṃ arahantaṃ sammāsambuddhaṃ abhivādetvā padakkhiṇaṃ katvā tattheva antaradhāyi.
87. Then, O monks, a certain Great Brahma, having divined the Lord Buddha Vipassi’s thought, as swiftly as a strong man might stretch his flexed arm, or flex it again.

Just so, having vanished from the Brahma world, he appeared before the Lord Buddha Vipassi, the arhat, the perfectly enlightened Buddha.

Then that Great Brahma, O monks, arranging his upper robe over one shoulder, and saluting the Lord Vipassi, the arhat, the perfectly enlightened Buddha, with joined palms, said this: "Just so, O Lord, just so, O Well-Farer!"

"Great indeed, O venerable one, is the community of monks now dwelling in the capital city of Bandhumati, numbering one hundred and sixty-eight thousand monks. May the Blessed One grant permission to the monks: 'Wander forth, O monks, on a journey for the good of the many, for the happiness of the many, out of compassion for the world, for the benefit, welfare, and happiness of gods and humans. Let not two go in the same direction. Teach, O monks, the dharma that is good in the beginning (ādikalyāṇaṃ), good in the middle (majjhekalyāṇaṃ), and good in the end (pariyosānakalyāṇaṃ), with the meaning and with the letter, perfectly complete, perfectly pure, proclaim the spiritual life (brahmacariyaṃ).

There are beings with little dust in their eyes who are falling away because they do not hear the dharma. There will be those who will understand the dharma.'"

"And we too, O venerable one, will act in such a way that the monks, at the end of six years, will come together to the royal capital of Bandhumati for the recitation of the pātimokkha."

Having spoken thus, O monks, that Great Brahma, having said this, and having paid homage to the Lord Vipassi, the arhat, the perfectly enlightened Buddha, and keeping him on his right, vanished there and then.
87.Aññataromahābrahmāti dhammadesanaṃ āyācitabrahmāva.
87.
A certain Mahābrahmā means Brahmā who requested the teaching of the Dharma.
88
88. ‘‘Atha kho, bhikkhave, vipassī bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho sāyanhasamayaṃ paṭisallānā vuṭṭhito bhikkhū āmantesi – ‘idha mayhaṃ, bhikkhave, rahogatassa paṭisallīnassa evaṃ cetaso parivitakko udapādi – mahā kho etarahi bhikkhusaṅgho bandhumatiyā rājadhāniyā paṭivasati aṭṭhasaṭṭhibhikkhusatasahassaṃ . Yaṃnūnāhaṃ bhikkhū anujāneyyaṃ – ‘caratha, bhikkhave, cārikaṃ bahujanahitāya bahujanasukhāya lokānukampāya atthāya hitāya sukhāya devamanussānaṃ; mā ekena dve agamittha; desetha, bhikkhave, dhammaṃ ādikalyāṇaṃ majjhekalyāṇaṃ pariyosānakalyāṇaṃ sātthaṃ sabyañjanaṃ kevalaparipuṇṇaṃ parisuddhaṃ brahmacariyaṃ pakāsetha. Santi sattā apparajakkhajātikā, assavanatā dhammassa parihāyanti, bhavissanti dhammassa aññātāro. Api ca, channaṃ channaṃ vassānaṃ accayena bandhumatī rājadhānī upasaṅkamitabbā pātimokkhuddesāyāti. ‘‘‘Atha kho, bhikkhave, aññataro mahābrahmā mama cetasā cetoparivitakkamaññāya seyyathāpi nāma balavā puriso samiñjitaṃ vā bāhaṃ pasāreyya, pasāritaṃ vā bāhaṃ samiñjeyya, evameva brahmaloke antarahito mama purato pāturahosi. Atha kho so, bhikkhave, mahābrahmā ekaṃsaṃ uttarāsaṅgaṃ karitvā yenāhaṃ tenañjaliṃ paṇāmetvā maṃ etadavoca – ‘‘evametaṃ, bhagavā, evametaṃ, sugata. Mahā kho, bhante, etarahi bhikkhusaṅgho bandhumatiyā rājadhāniyā paṭivasati aṭṭhasaṭṭhibhikkhusatasahassaṃ. Anujānātu, bhante, bhagavā bhikkhū – ‘caratha, bhikkhave, cārikaṃ bahujanahitāya bahujanasukhāya lokānukampāya atthāya hitāya sukhāya devamanussānaṃ; mā ekena dve agamittha; desetha, bhikkhave, dhammaṃ…pe… santi sattā apparajakkhajātikā , assavanatā dhammassa parihāyanti, bhavissanti dhammassa aññātāro’ti. Api ca, bhante, mayaṃ tathā karissāma, yathā bhikkhū channaṃ channaṃ vassānaṃ accayena bandhumatiṃ rājadhāniṃ upasaṅkamissanti pātimokkhuddesāyā’’ti. Idamavoca, bhikkhave, so mahābrahmā, idaṃ vatvā maṃ abhivādetvā padakkhiṇaṃ katvā tattheva antaradhāyi’. ‘‘‘Anujānāmi, bhikkhave, caratha cārikaṃ bahujanahitāya bahujanasukhāya lokānukampāya atthāya hitāya sukhāya devamanussānaṃ; mā ekena dve agamittha; desetha, bhikkhave, dhammaṃ ādikalyāṇaṃ majjhekalyāṇaṃ pariyosānakalyāṇaṃ sātthaṃ sabyañjanaṃ kevalaparipuṇṇaṃ parisuddhaṃ brahmacariyaṃ pakāsetha. Santi sattā apparajakkhajātikā, assavanatā dhammassa parihāyanti, bhavissanti dhammassa aññātāro. Api ca, bhikkhave, channaṃ channaṃ vassānaṃ accayena bandhumatī rājadhānī upasaṅkamitabbā pātimokkhuddesāyā’ti. Atha kho, bhikkhave, bhikkhū yebhuyyena ekāheneva janapadacārikaṃ pakkamiṃsu.
88. Then, monks, the Lord Vipassī, the arhat, the perfectly awakened one, arose from seclusion in the evening and addressed the monks: ‘Here, monks, when I was alone and secluded, such a thought arose in my mind: “This great bhikkhu-saṃgha dwells in the capital city of Bandhumatī, [numbering] sixty-eight hundred thousand bhikkhus.

Suppose I were to permit the bhikkhus: ‘Wander, monks, on tour for the benefit of the many, for the happiness of the many, out of compassion for the world, for the good, benefit, and happiness of gods and humans; let not two go in the same direction; teach, monks, the Dharma that is good in the beginning, good in the middle, and good in the end, with meaning and with expression, wholly complete, perfectly pure brahmacariya (holy life).

There are beings with little dust in their eyes who are falling away because they do not hear the Dharma. There will be those who will understand.

And again, monks, at the end of six years, the capital city of Bandhumatī should be approached for the recitation of the pātimokkha.

Then, monks, a certain great Brahmā, knowing with his mind the reflection in my mind, just as a strong man might extend his flexed arm or flex his extended arm, even so, vanished in the Brahmā-world and appeared before me.

Then that great Brahmā, monks, arranging his upper robe over one shoulder, and extending his joined palms towards me, said this to me: "Even so it is, Blessed One, even so it is, Well-Farer.

Great indeed, venerable sir, is the bhikkhu-saṃgha now dwelling in the capital city of Bandhumatī, [numbering] sixty-eight hundred thousand bhikkhus.

May the Blessed One permit the bhikkhus: ‘Wander, monks, on tour for the benefit of the many, for the happiness of the many, out of compassion for the world, for the good, benefit and happiness of gods and humans; let not two go in the same direction; teach, monks, the Dharma…pe… There are beings with little dust in their eyes who are falling away because they do not hear the Dharma. There will be those who will understand."

And again, venerable sirs, we will do likewise, just as the bhikkhus at the end of six years will approach the capital city of Bandhumatī for the recitation of the pātimokkha.’

This is what the great Brahmā said, monks. Having said this, he paid homage to me, circumambulated me to the right, and vanished right there.

‘I allow you, monks, to wander abroad for the good of the many, for the welfare and happiness of devas and humans. Do not go two together, monks, but teach the Dhamma that is lovely in its beginning, lovely in its middle, and lovely in its ending, both in the letter and in the spirit, and display the holy life fully complete and perfect.

There are beings with little dust in their eyes who are falling away because they do not hear the Dhamma. There will be those who will understand the Dhamma.”

But at the end of six years precisely you are to come together to the royal capital of Bandhumati to recite the disciplinary code.’

And the majority of those monks left that very day to wander about the country.
89
89. ‘‘Tena kho pana samayena jambudīpe caturāsīti āvāsasahassāni honti. Ekamhi hi vasse nikkhante devatā saddamanussāvesuṃ – ‘nikkhantaṃ kho, mārisā, ekaṃ vassaṃ; pañca dāni vassāni sesāni ; pañcannaṃ vassānaṃ accayena bandhumatī rājadhānī upasaṅkamitabbā pātimokkhuddesāyā’ti. Dvīsu vassesu nikkhantesu… tīsu vassesu nikkhantesu… catūsu vassesu nikkhantesu… pañcasu vassesu nikkhantesu devatā saddamanussāvesuṃ – ‘nikkhantāni kho , mārisā, pañcavassāni; ekaṃ dāni vassaṃ sesaṃ; ekassa vassassa accayena bandhumatī rājadhānī upasaṅkamitabbā pātimokkhuddesāyā’ti. Chasu vassesu nikkhantesu devatā saddamanussāvesuṃ – ‘nikkhantāni kho, mārisā, chabbassāni, samayo dāni bandhumatiṃ rājadhāniṃ upasaṅkamituṃ pātimokkhuddesāyā’ti. Atha kho te, bhikkhave, bhikkhū appekacce sakena iddhānubhāvena appekacce devatānaṃ iddhānubhāvena ekāheneva bandhumatiṃ rājadhāniṃ upasaṅkamiṃsu pātimokkhuddesāyāti pātimokkhuddesāya (?).
‘And at that time there were eighty-four thousand religious residences in Jambudīpa.

After one year had passed, the deities announced: “Friends, one year has passed; now five years remain; at the end of five years you must go to the royal capital of Bandhumatī to recite the Pātimokkha.”

After two years had passed… after three years had passed… after four years had passed… after five years had passed, the deities announced: “Friends, five years have passed; now one year remains; at the end of one year you must go to the royal capital of Bandhumatī to recite the Pātimokkha.”

After six years had passed, the deities announced: “Friends, six years have passed, now is the time to go to the royal capital of Bandhumatī to recite the Pātimokkha!”

Then, monks, some of those monks, by their own psychic power, and some by the psychic power of the deities, came to the royal capital of Bandhumatī in just one day to recite the Pātimokkha.
89.Caturāsīti āvāsasahassānīti caturāsīti vihārasahassāni. Te sabbepi dvādasasahassabhikkhugaṇhanakā mahāvihārā abhayagiricetiyapabbatacittalapabbatamahāvihārasadisāva ahesuṃ.
89. "Eighty-four thousand residences," that is, eighty-four thousand vihāras.

All those great vihāras, each filled with twelve thousand monks, were similar to the Abhayagiri-cetiya-pabbata and the Cittalapabbata-mahāvihāra.
90
90. ‘‘Tatra sudaṃ, bhikkhave, vipassī bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho bhikkhusaṅghe evaṃ pātimokkhaṃ uddisati – ‘Khantī paramaṃ tapo titikkhā, Nibbānaṃ paramaṃ vadanti buddhā; Na hi pabbajito parūpaghātī, Na samaṇo samaṇo (sī. syā. pī.) hoti paraṃ viheṭhayanto. samaṇo (sī. syā. pī.) ‘Sabbapāpassa akaraṇaṃ, kusalassa upasampadā; Sacittapariyodapanaṃ, etaṃ buddhānasāsanaṃ. ‘Anūpavādo anūpaghāto anupavādo anupaghāto (pī. ka.), pātimokkhe ca saṃvaro; anupavādo anupaghāto (pī. ka.) Mattaññutā ca bhattasmiṃ, pantañca sayanāsanaṃ; Adhicitte ca āyogo, etaṃ buddhānasāsana’nti. Devatārocanaṃ
90. It is said, "There, good monks, the Blessed One, the worthy arhan, the perfectly awakened Buddha, recites the Pātimokkha to the bhikṣu-saṅgha as follows:

'Forbearance is the highest austerity, titikkhā.

The Buddhas say nirvāṇa is supreme;

He is not an ascetic who harms another,

Nor is he a śramaṇa who injures another.

Abstaining from all evil, undertaking what is wholesome,

Purifying one's own mind: this is the teaching of the Buddhas.

Not speaking ill, not doing harm, restraint according to the Prātimokṣa,

Knowing moderation in food, secluded lodging,

Devotion to higher thought: this is the teaching of the Buddhas."

Invocation of the Deities
90.Khantī paramaṃ tapoti adhivāsanakhanti nāma paramaṃ tapo. Titikkhāti khantiyā eva vevacanaṃ. Titikkhā saṅkhātā adhivāsanakhanti uttamaṃ tapoti attho. Nibbānaṃ paramanti sabbākārena pana nibbānaṃ paramanti vadanti buddhā. Na hi pabbajito parūpaghātīti yo adhivāsanakhantivirahitattā paraṃ upaghāteti bādheti hiṃsati, so pabbajito nāma na hoti. Catutthapādo pana tasseva vevacanaṃ. ‘‘Na hi pabbajito’’ti etassa hi na samaṇo hotīti vevacanaṃ. Parūpaghātīti etassa paraṃ viheṭhayantoti vevacanaṃ. Atha vā parūpaghātīti sīlūpaghātī. Sīlañhi uttamaṭṭhena paranti vuccati. Yo ca samaṇo paraṃ yaṃ kañci sattaṃ viheṭhayanto parūpaghātī hoti, attano sīlaṃ vināsako, so pabbajito nāma na hotīti attho. Athavā yo adhivāsanakhantiyā abhāvato parūpaghātī hoti, paraṃ antamaso ḍaṃsamakasampi sañcicca jīvitā voropeti, so na hi pabbajito. Kiṃ kāraṇā? Malassa apabbājitattā. ‘‘Pabbājayamattano malaṃ, tasmā pabbajitoti vuccatī’’ti (dha. pa. 388) idañhi pabbajitalakkhaṇaṃ. Yopi na heva kho upaghāteti, na māreti, api ca daṇḍādīhi viheṭheti, so paraṃ viheṭhayanto samaṇo na hoti. Kiṃ kāraṇā? Vihesāya asamitattā. ‘‘Samitattā hi pāpānaṃ , samaṇoti pavuccatī’’ti (dha. pa. 265) idañhi samaṇalakkhaṇaṃ. Dutiyagāthāya sabbapāpassāti sabbākusalassa. Akaraṇanti anuppādanaṃ. Kusalassāti catubhūmikakusalassa. Upasampadāti paṭilābho. Sacittapariyodapananti attano cittajotanaṃ, taṃ pana arahattena hoti. Iti sīlasaṃvarena sabbapāpaṃ pahāya samathavipassanāhi kusalaṃ sampādetvā arahattaphalena cittaṃ pariyodāpetabbanti etaṃ buddhānaṃ sāsanaṃ ovādo anusiṭṭhī ti. sabbapāpassā Akaraṇa Kusalassā Upasampadā Sacittapariyodapana Tatiyagāthāya anūpavādoti vācāya kassaci anupavadanaṃ. Anūpaghātoti kāyena upaghātassa akaraṇaṃ. Pātimokkheti yaṃ taṃ paatimokkhaṃ, atipamokkhaṃ, uttamasīlaṃ, pāti vā agativisesehi mokkheti duggatibhayehi, yo vā naṃ pāti, taṃ mokkhetīti ‘‘pātimokkha’’nti vuccati. Tasmiṃ pātimokkhe ca saṃvaro. Mattaññutāti paṭiggahaṇaparibhogavasena pamāṇaññutā. Pantañca sayanāsananti sayanāsanañca saṅghaṭṭanavirahitanti attho. Tattha dvīhiyeva paccayehi catupaccayasantoso dīpito hotīti veditabbo. Etaṃ buddhāna sāsananti etaṃ parassa anupavadanaṃ anupaghātanaṃ pātimokkhasaṃvaro paṭiggahaṇaparibhogesu mattaññutā aṭṭhasamāpattivasibhāvāya vivittasenāsanasevanañca buddhānaṃ sāsanaṃ ovādo anusiṭṭhīti. Imā pana sabbabuddhānaṃ pātimokkhuddesagāthā hontīti veditabbā. anūpavādo Anūpaghāto Pātimokkhe Mattaññutā Pantañca sayanāsana Etaṃ buddhāna sāsana Devatārocanavaṇṇanā
90. Khantī paramaṃ tapo means patience is the highest austerity; namely, forbearance is the highest austerity.

Titikkhā means it is a synonym of patience.

Titikkhā is enumerated as forbearance; the meaning is the highest austerity.

Nibbānaṃ parama means the Buddhas say that nirvāṇa is the highest in every way.

Na hi pabbajito parūpaghātī means one gone forth does not hurt another. Whoever, due to lacking forbearance, harms, injures, or afflicts another is not called a pabbajito.

The fourth line is also a synonym of that.

"Na hi pabbajito" is a synonym of this: na samaṇo hoti.

Parūpaghātī means paraṃ viheṭhayanto.

Alternatively, parūpaghātī means sīlūpaghātī.

Indeed, sīla is called para because of its highest meaning.

And that renunciate who injures any being, harming another, destroying his own sīla, is not called a pabbajito; this is the meaning.

Alternatively, whoever, due to the absence of forbearance, is a parūpaghātī, intentionally depriving another of life, even down to a mosquito, is not a true pabbajito.

Why? Because the defilements have not been discarded.

"One discards one's own defilements, therefore, one is called a pabbajito." (Dha.

Pa.

388) This is the characteristic of a pabbajito.

Even someone who does not directly injure or kill but afflicts with sticks, etc., that samaṇa who harms another is not.

Why? Because he is not calmed from harming.

"Because of the calming of evils, one is called a samaṇo." (Dha.

Pa.

265) This is the characteristic of a samaṇo.

In the second verse, sabbapāpassā means of all unwholesome things.

Akaraṇa means non-production.

Kusalassā means of four-level wholesome qualities.

Upasampadā means attainment.

Sacittapariyodapana means the purification of one's own mind; however, that occurs through arhatship.

Thus, by the restraint of moral conduct, abandoning all evil, developing the wholesome through samatha and vipaśyanā, purifying the mind with the fruit of arhatship: this is the teaching of the Buddhas, the advice, the instruction.

Sabbapāpassā akaraṇa kusalassā upasampadā sacittapariyodapana

In the third verse, anūpavādo means the non-reviling of anyone by speech.

Anūpaghāto means the non-harming of injury by body.

Pātimokkhe means that which is the paatimokkha, atipamokkha, the highest morality, which protects from, or liberates from, the fears of bad migrations through particular destinies; or, that which protects him, that liberates him, is called "pātimokkha."

In that pātimokkha, there is restraint (saṃvaro).

Mattaññutā means knowing moderation in the context of receiving and using.

Pantañca sayanāsana means a secluded dwelling and seat is devoid of association; this is the meaning.

There, with just two causes, contentment with the four requisites is shown; thus, it is to be understood.

Etaṃ buddhāna sāsana means this: non-reviling of another, non-harming, pātimokkha restraint, moderation in receiving and using, the practice of secluded dwellings for the sake of the state of mastery over the eight samāpattis is the teaching of the Buddhas, the advice, the instruction.
These verses on the recitation of the prātimokṣa are known to belong to all the Buddhas.

Abstaining from reproach (anūpavāda), abstaining from injury (anūpaghāta), restraint according to the prātimokṣa, moderation in food (mattaññutā), and secluded lodging—this is the teaching of the Buddhas (etaṃ buddhāna sāsana).

Explanation of the shining of the deities (devatārocanavaṇṇanā).
91
91. ‘‘Ekamidāhaṃ, bhikkhave, samayaṃ ukkaṭṭhāyaṃ viharāmi subhagavane sālarājamūle. Tassa mayhaṃ, bhikkhave, rahogatassa paṭisallīnassa evaṃ cetaso parivitakko udapādi – ‘na kho so sattāvāso sulabharūpo, yo mayā anāvutthapubbo anajjhāvuṭṭhapubbo (ka. sī. ka.) iminā dīghena addhunā aññatra suddhāvāsehi devehi. Yaṃnūnāhaṃ yena suddhāvāsā devā tenupasaṅkameyya’nti. Atha khvāhaṃ, bhikkhave, seyyathāpi nāma balavā puriso samiñjitaṃ vā bāhaṃ pasāreyya, pasāritaṃ vā bāhaṃ samiñjeyya, evameva ukkaṭṭhāyaṃ subhagavane sālarājamūle antarahito avihesu devesu pāturahosiṃ . Tasmiṃ, bhikkhave, devanikāye anekāni devatāsahassāni anekāni devatāsatasahassāni anekāni devatāsatāni anekāni devatāsahassāni (syā.) yenāhaṃ tenupasaṅkamiṃsu; upasaṅkamitvā maṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ aṭṭhaṃsu. Ekamantaṃ ṭhitā kho, bhikkhave, tā devatā maṃ etadavocuṃ – ‘ito so, mārisā, ekanavutikappe yaṃ vipassī bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho loke udapādi. Vipassī, mārisā, bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho khattiyo jātiyā ahosi, khattiyakule udapādi. Vipassī, mārisā, bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho koṇḍañño gottena ahosi . Vipassissa, mārisā, bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa asītivassasahassāni āyuppamāṇaṃ ahosi. Vipassī, mārisā, bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho pāṭaliyā mūle abhisambuddho. Vipassissa, mārisā, bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa khaṇḍatissaṃ nāma sāvakayugaṃ ahosi aggaṃ bhaddayugaṃ. Vipassissa, mārisā, bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa tayo sāvakānaṃ sannipātā ahesuṃ. Eko sāvakānaṃ sannipāto ahosi aṭṭhasaṭṭhibhikkhusatasahassaṃ. Eko sāvakānaṃ sannipāto ahosi bhikkhusatasahassaṃ. Eko sāvakānaṃ sannipāto ahosi asītibhikkhusahassāni. Vipassissa, mārisā, bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa ime tayo sāvakānaṃ sannipātā ahesuṃ sabbesaṃyeva khīṇāsavānaṃ. Vipassissa, mārisā, bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa asoko nāma bhikkhu upaṭṭhāko ahosi aggupaṭṭhāko. Vipassissa, mārisa, bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa bandhumā nāma rājā pitā ahosi. Bandhumatī nāma devī mātā ahosi janetti. Bandhumassa rañño bandhumatī nāma nagaraṃ rājadhānī ahosi. Vipassissa, mārisā , bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa evaṃ abhinikkhamanaṃ ahosi evaṃ pabbajjā evaṃ padhānaṃ evaṃ abhisambodhi evaṃ dhammacakkappavattanaṃ. Te mayaṃ, mārisā, vipassimhi bhagavati brahmacariyaṃ caritvā kāmesu kāmacchandaṃ virājetvā idhūpapannā’ti …pe… ‘‘Tasmiṃyeva kho, bhikkhave, devanikāye anekāni devatāsahassāni anekāni devatāsatasahassāni anekāni devatāsatāni anekāni devatāsahassāni (syā. evamuparipi) yenāhaṃ tenupasaṅkamiṃsu; upasaṅkamitvā maṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ aṭṭhaṃsu. Ekamantaṃ ṭhitā kho, bhikkhave, tā devatā maṃ etadavocuṃ – ‘imasmiṃyeva kho, mārisā, bhaddakappe bhagavā etarahi arahaṃ sammāsambuddho loke uppanno. Bhagavā, mārisā, khattiyo jātiyā khattiyakule uppanno. Bhagavā, mārisā, gotamo gottena. Bhagavato, mārisā, appakaṃ āyuppamāṇaṃ parittaṃ lahukaṃ yo ciraṃ jīvati, so vassasataṃ appaṃ vā bhiyyo. Bhagavā, mārisā, assatthassa mūle abhisambuddho. Bhagavato, mārisā, sāriputtamoggallānaṃ nāma sāvakayugaṃ ahosi aggaṃ bhaddayugaṃ . Bhagavato, mārisā, eko sāvakānaṃ sannipāto ahosi aḍḍhateḷasāni bhikkhusatāni. Bhagavato, mārisā, ayaṃ eko sāvakānaṃ sannipāto ahosi sabbesaṃyeva khīṇāsavānaṃ. Bhagavato, mārisā, ānando nāma bhikkhu upaṭṭhāko ahosi aggupaṭṭhāko. Bhagavato, mārisā, suddhodano nāma rājā pitā ahosi. Māyā nāma devī mātā ahosi janetti. Kapilavatthu nāma nagaraṃ rājadhānī ahosi. Bhagavato, mārisā, evaṃ abhinikkhamanaṃ ahosi evaṃ pabbajjā evaṃ padhānaṃ evaṃ abhisambodhi evaṃ dhammacakkappavattanaṃ. Te mayaṃ, mārisā, bhagavati brahmacariyaṃ caritvā kāmesu kāmacchandaṃ virājetvā idhūpapannā’ti. anekāni devatāsatāni anekāni devatāsahassāni (syā. evamuparipi)
91. Monks, on one occasion I was staying at Ukkaṭṭhā in the Subhaga forest at the root of a royal Sāla tree.

Then, monks, as I was in seclusion, this thought arose in my mind: ‘There is no abode of beings easily accessible that has not been visited by me in this long journey, except for the devas of the Pure Abodes.

Suppose I were to visit the devas of the Pure Abodes now?”

Then, monks, as swiftly as a strong man might stretch out his flexed arm, or flex his extended arm, I vanished from Ukkaṭṭhā in the Subhaga forest at the root of a royal Sāla tree and appeared among the Aviha devas.

In that community of devas, monks, many thousands of devas, many hundreds of thousands of devas came to me, saluted me, and stood to one side.

Standing to one side, those devas said to me: “Sir, it is ninety-one aeons since the Buddha Vipassī, the arhat, the perfectly enlightened one, arose in the world.

The Buddha Vipassī, sir, was of the Khattiya (warrior) caste by birth, and arose in a Khattiya family.

The Buddha Vipassī, sir, was of the Koṇḍañña clan.

In the time of the Buddha Vipassī, sir, the lifespan was eighty thousand years.

The Buddha Vipassī, sir, attained perfect enlightenment at the foot of a trumpet flower tree (pāṭaliyā).

In the time of the Buddha Vipassī, sir, there was a pair of disciples named Khaṇḍa and Tissa, the foremost and excellent pair.

In the time of the Buddha Vipassī, sir, there were three assemblies of disciples.

There was one assembly of sixty-eight hundred thousand monks.

There was one assembly of one hundred thousand monks.

There was one assembly of eighty thousand monks.

In the time of the Buddha Vipassī, sir, these three assemblies of disciples were all of khīṇāsavas (those whose corruptions are destroyed).

In the time of the Buddha Vipassī, sir, the monk Asoka was his personal attendant, the chief attendant.

In the time of the Buddha Vipassī, sir, the king Bandhuma was his father.

The queen Bandhumatī was his mother, the one who gave birth to him.

The capital city of King Bandhuma was named Bandhumatī.

The renunciation of the Buddha Vipassī, sir, his going forth, his striving, his perfect enlightenment, and his turning of the wheel of dharma were like this.

Having lived the brahmacariya (holy life) under the Buddha Vipassī, we have abandoned desire for sensual pleasures and have been reborn here…

Monks, in that community of devas, many thousands of devas, many hundreds of thousands of devas came to me, saluted me, and stood to one side.

Standing to one side, those devas said to me: “Sir, in this auspicious aeon, the Buddha, the arhat, the perfectly enlightened one has arisen in the world.

The Buddha, sir, was of the Khattiya caste by birth, and arose in a Khattiya family.

The Buddha, sir, is of the Gotama clan.

In the time of the Buddha, sir, the life span is short, limited, and fleeting; whoever lives long lives for a hundred years, more or less.

The Buddha, sir, attained perfect enlightenment at the foot of an assattha tree.
Venerable ones, for the Blessed One, there was a pair of disciples named Śāriputra and Maudgalyāyana, an excellent and auspicious pair.

Venerable ones, for the Blessed One, there was one assembly of disciples consisting of twelve hundred and fifty monks.

Venerable ones, for the Blessed One, this one assembly of disciples consisted entirely of those whose āsavas are destroyed (khīṇāsavānaṃ), who are all Arahants.

Venerable ones, for the Blessed One, there was a monk named Ānanda, the attendant, the chief attendant (aggupaṭṭhāko).

Venerable ones, for the Blessed One, there was a king named Śuddhodana, the father.

Māyā by name was the queen and mother (mātā janetti).

Kapilavastu by name was the city, the capital (rājadhānī).

Venerable ones, for the Blessed One, such was the great renunciation (abhinikkhamanaṃ), such was the going forth (pabbajjā), such was the striving (padhānaṃ), such was the perfect enlightenment (abhisambodhi), such was the turning of the wheel of Dharma (dhammacakkappavattanaṃ).

We, venerable ones, having lived the holy life (brahmacariyaṃ) under the Blessed One, having abandoned desire for sensual pleasures (kāmesu kāmacchandaṃ virājetvā), have arisen here.

Note: Many hundreds of gods, many thousands of gods (evamuparipi).
91. Ettāvatā ca iminā vipassissa bhagavato apadānānusārena vitthārakathanena – ‘‘tathāgatassevesā, bhikkhave, dhammadhātu suppaṭividdhā’’ti evaṃ vuttāya dhammadhātuyā suppaṭividdhabhāvaṃ pakāsetvā idāni – ‘‘devatāpi tathāgatassa etamatthaṃ ārocesu’’nti vuttaṃ devatārocanaṃ pakāsetuṃ ekamidāhantiādimāha. Tattha subhagavaneti evaṃnāmake vane. Sālarājamūleti vanappatijeṭṭhakassa mūle. Kāmacchandaṃ virājetvāti anāgāmimaggena mūlasamugghātavasena virājetvā. Yathā ca vipassissa, evaṃ sesabuddhānampi sāsane vutthabrahmacariyā devatā ārocayiṃsu, pāḷi pana vipassissa ceva amhākañca bhagavato vasena āgatā. subhagavane Sālarājamūle Kāmacchandaṃ virājetvā Tattha attano sampattiyā na hāyanti, na vihāyantīti avihā. Na kañci sattaṃ tapantīti atappā. Sundaradassanā abhirūpā pāsādikāti sudassā. Suṭṭhu passanti, sundarametesaṃ vā dassananti sudassī. Sabbeheva ca saguṇehi bhavasampattiyā ca jeṭṭhā, natthettha kaniṭṭhāti akaniṭṭhā. avihā. atappā. sudassā. sudassī. akaniṭṭhā. Idha ṭhatvā bhāṇavārā samodhānetabbā. Imasmiñhi sutte vipassissa bhagavato apadānavasena tayo bhāṇavārā vuttā. Yathā ca vipassissa, evaṃ sikhīādīnampi apadānavasena vuttāva. Pāḷi pana saṅkhittā. Iti sattannaṃ buddhānaṃ vasena amhākaṃ bhagavatā ekavīsati bhāṇavārā kathitā. Tathā avihehi. Tathā atappehi. Tathā sudassehi. Tathā sudassīhi. Tathā akaniṭṭhehīti sabbampi chabbīsatibhāṇavārasataṃ hoti. Tepiṭake buddhavacane aññaṃ suttaṃ chabbīsatibhāṇavārasataparimāṇaṃ nāma natthi, suttantarājā nāma ayaṃ suttantoti veditabbo. Ito paraṃ anusandhidvayampi niyyātento iti kho bhikkhavetiādimāha. Taṃ sabbaṃ uttānamevāti. iti kho bhikkhave Iti sumaṅgalavilāsiniyā dīghanikāyaṭṭhakathāyaṃ Mahāpadānasuttavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.
91. Thus, in accordance with the apadāna of Vipassī, the Fortunate One, with this extended account—"Indeed, monks, the dharma-dhātu of the Tathāgata is well-penetrated"—having revealed the state of being well-penetrated of the dharma-dhātu thus spoken, now, in order to reveal the announcement of the deities, which was said, "The deities also announced this matter to the Tathāgata," he spoke, beginning with, "He spoke thus."

There, subhagavane means in a forest named thus.

Sālarājamūle means at the root of the chief of forest trees.

Kāmacchandaṃ virājetvā means having abandoned sensual desire by means of the path of the non-returner, by way of the eradication of the root.

Just as with Vipassī, so also in the dispensation of the remaining Buddhas, deities who had lived the brahmacariya announced [this]. But the pāḷi came by way of Vipassī and by way of our Fortunate One.

Subhagavane, Sālarājamūle, Kāmacchandaṃ virājetvā.

There, "they do not decline in their own prosperity, they do not disappear," is avihā.

"They do not torment any being," is atappā.

"Of beautiful appearance, lovely, inspiring confidence," is sudassā.

"They see well, or their seeing is beautiful," is sudassī.

"And they are the eldest by all good qualities and by the prosperity of existence, there is none here who is younger," is akaniṭṭhā.

*Avihā.
Atappā.
Sudassā.
Sudassī.
Akaniṭṭhā.*

Here, having remained, the recitations should be brought to harmony.

In this sūtra, three recitations have been spoken of by way of the avadāna of Vipassī, the Fortunate One.

Just as with Vipassī, so also with Sikhī and others, it was spoken of by way of the avadāna.

But the pāḷi is condensed.

Thus, by way of the seven Buddhas, twenty-one recitations have been spoken by our Fortunate One.

Likewise, be without decay (avihehi).

Likewise, be without torment (atappehi).

Likewise, be of beautiful appearance (sudassehi).

Likewise, be of beautiful seeing (sudassīhi).

Likewise, be not the youngest (akaniṭṭhehi), thus all this becomes 2600 recitations.

In the Buddhist word in the three baskets, there is no other sūtra with a measure of 2600 recitations; this sūtra should be understood to be called the king of sūtras (suttantarājā).

Furthermore, in order to convey the two connections, he spoke, beginning with "iti kho bhikkhave."

All that is simply clear.

iti kho bhikkhave

Thus the Great Lineage Commentary on the Long Discourses,

The Account of the Great apadāna-sūtra is finished.
