92. ‘‘Atha khvāhaṃ, bhikkhave, avihehi devehi saddhiṃ yena atappā devā tenupasaṅkamiṃ…pe… atha khvāhaṃ, bhikkhave, avihehi ca devehi atappehi ca devehi saddhiṃ yena sudassā devā tenupasaṅkamiṃ. Atha khvāhaṃ, bhikkhave, avihehi ca devehi atappehi ca devehi sudassehi ca devehi saddhiṃ yena sudassī devā tenupasaṅkamiṃ. Atha khvāhaṃ, bhikkhave, avihehi ca devehi atappehi ca devehi sudassehi ca devehi sudassīhi ca devehi saddhiṃ yena akaniṭṭhā devā tenupasaṅkamiṃ. Tasmiṃ, bhikkhave, devanikāye anekāni devatāsahassāni anekāni devatāsatasahassāni yenāhaṃ tenupasaṅkamiṃsu, upasaṅkamitvā maṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ aṭṭhaṃsu . ‘‘Ekamantaṃ ṭhitā kho, bhikkhave, tā devatā maṃ etadavocuṃ – ‘ito so, mārisā, ekanavutikappe yaṃ vipassī bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho loke udapādi. Vipassī, mārisā, bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho khattiyo jātiyā ahosi. Khattiyakule udapādi. Vipassī, mārisā, bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho koṇḍañño gottena ahosi. Vipassissa, mārisā, bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa asītivassasahassāni āyuppamāṇaṃ ahosi. Vipassī, mārisā, bhagavā arahaṃ sammāsambuddho pāṭaliyā mūle abhisambuddho. Vipassissa, mārisā, bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa khaṇḍatissaṃ nāma sāvakayugaṃ ahosi aggaṃ bhaddayugaṃ. Vipassissa, mārisā, bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa tayo sāvakānaṃ sannipātā ahesuṃ. Eko sāvakānaṃ sannipāto ahosi aṭṭhasaṭṭhibhikkhusatasahassaṃ. Eko sāvakānaṃ sannipāto ahosi bhikkhusatasahassaṃ. Eko sāvakānaṃ sannipāto ahosi asītibhikkhusahassāni. Vipassissa, mārisā, bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa ime tayo sāvakānaṃ sannipātā ahesuṃ sabbesaṃyeva khīṇāsavānaṃ. Vipassissa, mārisā, bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa asoko nāma bhikkhu upaṭṭhāko ahosi aggupaṭṭhāko. Vipassissa, mārisā, bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa bandhumā nāma rājā pitā ahosi bandhumatī nāma devī mātā ahosi janetti. Bandhumassa rañño bandhumatī nāma nagaraṃ rājadhānī ahosi. Vipassissa, mārisā, bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa evaṃ abhinikkhamanaṃ ahosi evaṃ pabbajjā evaṃ padhānaṃ evaṃ abhisambodhi, evaṃ dhammacakkappavattanaṃ. Te mayaṃ, mārisā, vipassimhi bhagavati brahmacariyaṃ caritvā kāmesu kāmacchandaṃ virājetvā idhūpapannā’ti. Tasmiṃyeva kho, bhikkhave, devanikāye anekāni devatāsahassāni anekāni devatāsatasahassāni yenāhaṃ tenupasaṅkamiṃsu; upasaṅkamitvā maṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ aṭṭhaṃsu. Ekamantaṃ ṭhitā kho, bhikkhave, tā devatā maṃ etadavocuṃ – ‘ito so, mārisā, ekatiṃse kappe yaṃ sikhī bhagavā…pe… te mayaṃ, mārisā, sikhimhi bhagavati tasmiññeva kho mārisā, ekatiṃse kappe yaṃ vessabhū bhagavā…pe… te mayaṃ, mārisā, vessabhumhi bhagavati…pe… imasmiṃyeva kho, mārisā, bhaddakappe kakusandho koṇāgamano kassapo bhagavā…pe… te mayaṃ, mārisā, kakusandhamhi koṇāgamanamhi kassapamhi bhagavati brahmacariyaṃ caritvā kāmesu kāmacchandaṃ virājetvā idhūpapannā’ti.
92. Then, monks, I went with the Aviha gods to where the Atappa gods were…and so on…Then, monks, I went with the Aviha gods, the Atappa gods to where the Sudassa gods were.

Then, monks, I went with the Aviha gods, the Atappa gods, and the Sudassa gods to where the Sudassi gods were.

Then, monks, I went with the Aviha gods, the Atappa gods, the Sudassa gods, and the Sudassi gods to where the Akaniṭṭha gods were.

In that assembly of gods, monks, many thousands of deities, many hundreds of thousands of deities came to where I was; having come, they greeted me and stood to one side.

Standing to one side, monks, those deities said this to me: ‘From here, sir, ninety-one aeons ago, the Blessed Vipassī, the worthy one, the fully enlightened Buddha, arose in the world.

Vipassī, sir, was a Blessed One, a worthy one, a fully enlightened Buddha, a noble of birth ( kṣatriya).

He arose in a noble family.

Vipassī, sir, was a Blessed One, a worthy one, a fully enlightened Buddha, of the Koṇḍañña clan.

Vipassī, sir, the Blessed One, the worthy one, the fully enlightened Buddha, had a life span of eighty thousand years.

Vipassī, sir, the Blessed One, the worthy one, the fully enlightened Buddha, attained full enlightenment at the foot of a trumpet-flower tree ( pāṭaliyā mūle abhisambuddho).

Vipassī, sir, the Blessed One, the worthy one, the fully enlightened Buddha, had a pair of disciples named Khaṇḍa and Tissa, the foremost and excellent pair.

Vipassī, sir, the Blessed One, the worthy one, the fully enlightened Buddha, had three assemblies of disciples.

One assembly of disciples was of six million eight hundred thousand monks.

One assembly of disciples was of one hundred thousand monks.

One assembly of disciples was of eighty thousand monks.

These were the three assemblies of disciples of Vipassī, sir, the Blessed One, the worthy one, the fully enlightened Buddha, all of whom were with the destruction of the āsavas.

Vipassī, sir, the Blessed One, the worthy one, the fully enlightened Buddha, had a personal attendant monk named Asoka, the foremost attendant.

Vipassī, sir, the Blessed One, the worthy one, the fully enlightened Buddha, had a king named Bandhuma as his father and a queen named Bandhumatī as his mother.

The capital city of King Bandhuma was named Bandhumatī.

Vipassī, sir, the Blessed One, the worthy one, the fully enlightened Buddha, such was his renunciation (abhinikkhamanaṃ), such his going forth (pabbajjā), such his striving (padhānaṃ), such his full enlightenment (abhisambodhi), and such his turning of the wheel of the Dharma (dhammacakkappavattanaṃ).

We, sir, lived the holy life in Vipassī the Blessed One, having eradicated desire for sensual pleasures, have arisen here.’

In that same assembly of gods, monks, many thousands of deities, many hundreds of thousands of deities came to where I was; having come, they greeted me and stood to one side.

Standing to one side, monks, those deities said this to me: ‘From here, sir, thirty-one aeons ago, the Blessed Sikhī…and so on… We, sir, in Sikhī the Blessed One, in that same assembly, sir, thirty-one aeons ago, the Blessed Vessabhū…and so on… We, sir, in Vessabhū the Blessed One…and so on… In this fortunate aeon, sir, Kakusandha, Koṇāgamana, Kassapa were Blessed Ones…and so on… We, sir, in Kakusandha, Koṇāgamana, Kassapa, the Blessed Ones, having lived the holy life, having eradicated desire for sensual pleasures, have arisen here.’
93
93. ‘‘Tasmiṃyeva kho, bhikkhave, devanikāye anekāni devatāsahassāni anekāni devatāsatasahassāni yenāhaṃ tenupasaṅkamiṃsu; upasaṅkamitvā maṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ aṭṭhaṃsu. Ekamantaṃ ṭhitā kho, bhikkhave, tā devatā maṃ etadavocuṃ – ‘imasmiṃyeva kho, mārisā, bhaddakappe bhagavā etarahi arahaṃ sammāsambuddho loke uppanno. Bhagavā, mārisā, khattiyo jātiyā, khattiyakule uppanno. Bhagavā, mārisā, gotamo gottena. Bhagavato, mārisā, appakaṃ āyuppamāṇaṃ parittaṃ lahukaṃ yo ciraṃ jīvati, so vassasataṃ appaṃ vā bhiyyo. Bhagavā, mārisā, assatthassa mūle abhisambuddho. Bhagavato, mārisā, sāriputtamoggallānaṃ nāma sāvakayugaṃ ahosi aggaṃ bhaddayugaṃ. Bhagavato , mārisā, eko sāvakānaṃ sannipāto ahosi aḍḍhateḷasāni bhikkhusatāni. Bhagavato, mārisā, ayaṃ eko sāvakānaṃ sannipāto ahosi sabbesaṃyeva khīṇāsavānaṃ. Bhagavato, mārisā, ānando nāma bhikkhu upaṭṭhāko aggupaṭṭhāko ahosi. Bhagavato, mārisā, suddhodano nāma rājā pitā ahosi. Māyā nāma devī mātā ahosi janetti. Kapilavatthu nāma nagaraṃ rājadhānī ahosi. Bhagavato, mārisā, evaṃ abhinikkhamanaṃ ahosi, evaṃ pabbajjā, evaṃ padhānaṃ, evaṃ abhisambodhi, evaṃ dhammacakkappavattanaṃ. Te mayaṃ, mārisā, bhagavati brahmacariyaṃ caritvā kāmesu kāmacchandaṃ virājetvā idhūpapannā’ti.
93. And there, monks, in that assembly of gods, many thousands of gods, many hundreds of thousands of gods came to me; having come, having saluted me, they stood to one side.

Standing to one side, monks, those gods said this to me: 'In this fortunate aeon ( bhaddakappa), the Blessed One (bhagavā), the worthy one (arahaṃ), the fully awakened one (sammāsambuddho) has arisen in the world ( loke uppanno).

The Blessed One (bhagavā), good sirs (mārisā), is a noble (khattiyo) by birth (jātiyā), born in a noble family (khattiyakule uppanno).

The Blessed One (bhagavā), good sirs (mārisā), is a Gautama by clan (gottena).

The Blessed One (bhagavato), good sirs (mārisā), has a short life span (āyuppamāṇaṃ), limited (parittaṃ), brief (lahukaṃ); whoever lives long, lives a hundred years, a little more or less.

The Blessed One (bhagavā), good sirs (mārisā), is fully awakened (abhisambuddho) at the foot of an assattha tree.

The Blessed One (bhagavato), good sirs (mārisā), had a pair of disciples named Śāriputta and Maudgalyāyana (sāriputtamoggallānaṃ nāma sāvakayugaṃ), an excellent and auspicious pair (aggaṃ bhaddayugaṃ).

The Blessed One (bhagavato), good sirs (mārisā), had one assembly (sannipāto) of disciples (sāvakānaṃ) consisting of twelve hundred and fifty monks (bhikkhusatāni).

The Blessed One (bhagavato), good sirs (mārisā), this one assembly (eko sāvakānaṃ sannipāto) was entirely of those whose āsavas are destroyed (khīṇāsavānaṃ).

The Blessed One (bhagavato), good sirs (mārisā), had a monk named Ānanda (ānando nāma bhikkhu) as attendant (upaṭṭhāko), the chief attendant (aggupaṭṭhāko).

The Blessed One (bhagavato), good sirs (mārisā), had a king named Śuddhodana (suddhodano nāma rājā) as his father (pitā).

Māyā (māyā) was the name of his mother (mātā), the queen (devī), who gave birth to him (janetti).

Kapilavastu (kapilavatthu) was the name of the city (nagaraṃ), his capital (rājadhānī).

The Blessed One (bhagavato), good sirs (mārisā), such was his renunciation (abhinikkhamanaṃ), such his going forth (pabbajjā), such his striving (padhānaṃ), such his full awakening (abhisambodhi), such his turning of the wheel of the Dharma (dhammacakkappavattanaṃ).

We, good sirs (mārisā), having lived the holy life (brahmacariyaṃ caritvā) under the Blessed One (bhagavati), having abandoned desire for sensual pleasures (kāmesu kāmacchandaṃ virājetvā), have arisen here (idhūpapannā).'
94
94. ‘‘Iti kho, bhikkhave, tathāgatassevesā dhammadhātu suppaṭividdhā, yassā dhammadhātuyā suppaṭividdhattā tathāgato atīte buddhe parinibbute chinnapapañce chinnavaṭume pariyādinnavaṭṭe sabbadukkhavītivatte jātitopi anussarati, nāmatopi anussarati, gottatopi anussarati, āyuppamāṇatopi anussarati, sāvakayugatopi anussarati, sāvakasannipātatopi anussarati ‘evaṃjaccā te bhagavanto ahesuṃ’ itipi. ‘Evaṃnāmā evaṃgottā evaṃsīlā evaṃdhammā evaṃpaññā evaṃvihārī evaṃvimuttā te bhagavanto ahesuṃ’ itipīti. ‘‘Devatāpi tathāgatassa etamatthaṃ ārocesuṃ, yena tathāgato atīte buddhe parinibbute chinnapapañce chinnavaṭume pariyādinnavaṭṭe sabbadukkhavītivatte jātitopi anussarati, nāmatopi anussarati, gottatopi anussarati, āyuppamāṇatopi anussarati, sāvakayugatopi anussarati, sāvakasannipātatopi anussarati ‘evaṃjaccā te bhagavanto ahesuṃ’ itipi. ‘Evaṃnāmā evaṃgottā evaṃsīlā evaṃdhammā evaṃpaññā evaṃvihārī evaṃvimuttā te bhagavanto ahesuṃ’ itipī’’ti. Idamavoca bhagavā. Attamanā te bhikkhū bhagavato bhāsitaṃ abhinandunti. Mahāpadānasuttaṃ niṭṭhitaṃ paṭhamaṃ.
94. Thus, monks, the dhamma-element is well-penetrated by the Tathāgata. Because the dhamma-element is well-penetrated, the Tathāgata remembers past Buddhas who have attained final Nirvāṇa, having cut off proliferation, cut off the root, put an end to the round, overcome all suffering, also by birth, also by name, also by clan.

He remembers also their life span, also their pairs of disciples, also their assemblies of disciples, thinking, ‘They, the Blessed Ones, were of such a birth.’

‘They were called thus, their clan was thus, their morality was thus, their dhamma was thus, their wisdom was thus, their dwelling was thus, their liberation was thus.’

Furthermore, the deities announced this matter to the Tathāgata, [saying,] “The Tathāgata, regarding past Buddhas, who attained final Nirvāṇa, having cut off proliferation, cut off the root, put an end to the round, [and] overcome all suffering, remembers also their birth, also their name, also their clan, also their life span, also their pairs of disciples, also their assemblies of disciples, [thinking,] ‘They, the Blessed Ones, were of such a birth.’

‘They were called thus, their clan was thus, their morality was thus, their dhamma was thus, their wisdom was thus, their dwelling was thus, their liberation was thus.’”

The Blessed One said this.

The monks, uplifted, rejoiced in the Blessed One’s words.

The Great Lineage Discourse is finished, the first.
